### 1735 — CR-2 Revision 3 Closed The Turn/Message Lifecycle Bypass And The Self-Minted-Evidence Seam
- FINDING (F1, review of CR-TASK-260830-y6infr-2 rev 2): `parsePiTurnJSONL` tracked only the outer `agent_start`/`agent_end` lifecycle plus "some assistant `message_end` exists". It had no state for an open turn or an open message, so missing `turn_start`, missing `message_start`, and duplicate `turn_start` were all admitted as success.
- FIX: added `turnOpen`/`messageOpen` booleans enforced on every turn/message-scoped event case in `pi_turn_result.go`, plus a trailing EOF backstop requiring both false at stream end. `TestPiTurnTranslatorRefusesTurnAndMessageLifecycleViolations` reproduces the three reviewer attacks plus four more (turn_end without an open turn, turn_end/agent_end firing with a still-open message/turn, duplicate message_start).
- FINDING (F2): the darwin integration test drove the real Process-B reader to a genuine refusal, then switched to `recordingSanitizedObservationReader` (a self-minted, always-succeeding fake) right before calling `BuildAndRunPiTurn` — the production launch decision never actually consumed the real broker-backed observation.
- FIX: added an `/agents-infra/resources` handler to the shared fake-runtime test helper so a `resource_pressure_mode = "provider"` profile lets the real `SharedRuntimeSanitizedEngineObservationReader` succeed against the real (fake-backed) broker; `BuildPiPluginGraph`/`BuildAndRunPiTurn` are now driven with that exact real reader. Added a second, independent, real shared-runtime lease (via the same `acquireSharedRuntimeLease` client `RunPi` uses) representing Process A's own lease lifecycle, proving it and the pre-existing Process-B peer lease/runtime survive Process-A's fake success and cancellation runs unchanged.
- ANOMALY: the previously-published `mutants-rev2.log` printed `exit=0` for every mutant regardless of real outcome. Root cause not in the current script — `.temp/TASK-260830-y6infr/mutants.sh`'s `run_mutant` captures `local code=$?` correctly; verified by injecting both a real test failure and a real Go build failure and observing correct exit codes (1 and 1) in both cases. The buggy log must have come from an earlier/differently-formatted script version. No `run_mutant` code change was needed; republished a freshly regenerated, verified log.
- FINDING: the new lifecycle guards have deliberate defense-in-depth (multiple independent events re-check `turnOpen`/`messageOpen`, plus the EOF backstop), so several F1 narrowing mutants had to be compound (2-4 line edits) — a single-line narrowing left the attack caught by a neighboring redundant check and falsely looked like "guard doesn't matter". 18/18 mutants killed with real exit codes (`mutants-rev3.log`).

### 1600 — CR-1 Revision 2 Closed Production Wiring, Real Process-B Evidence, And The Parser Bypass
- FINDING (F1, review of CR-TASK-260830-y6infr-1 rev 1): `BuildAndRunPiTurn`, `BuildPiPluginGraph`, `ResolvePiPluginGraph`, and `NewSanitizedEngineObservationAdapter` had no non-test caller, and the only `SanitizedEngineObservationReader` was a test closure that self-minted "good" facts in memory.
- FIX: Added `SharedRuntimeSanitizedEngineObservationReader` (`agents_management_engine_reader.go`), a real, non-test adapter that performs one bounded `SharedRuntimeStatusReport` read (the same read-only entry point `agents-infra runtime status` uses) against agents-infra's own Process-B broker, then derives the closed 17-fact set from that live status plus the already-resolved `PiProfile`. It refuses (returns an error, invents nothing) for any fact it cannot back with real data — e.g. `resource_pressure_mode=disabled` genuinely cannot measure loaded-model memory, so MemoryAccounting refuses rather than fabricating a byte count. `ResolvePiPluginGraph` now fills a nil status/observations reader with this reader plus `localruntime.NewCLIStatusReader()`. Wired a real, non-test production call site: `agents-infra pi turn --prompt TEXT [--deadline D]` in `main.go` resolves the graph and drives `BuildAndRunPiTurn`.
- FINDING (F2): the claimed "fake Process-B lifecycle" evidence was only a marker file `FAKE_PROCESS_B_MARKER` written by the fake Process-A shell script itself — no broker, lease, or runtime child ever participated.
- FIX: `pi_shared_engine_observation_darwin_test.go` reuses the repo's existing real broker/lease integration harness (`startSharedLeaseHelper`, `buildSharedFakeRuntime`, `SharedRuntimeStatusReport`) to start a genuine broker holding a genuine lease over a genuine (test-built, non-live-model) runtime child, drives the new real reader against it, then runs `BuildAndRunPiTurn` (success and cancellation) through the real production consumer and asserts the independently-held broker/lease/runtime PID are byte-for-byte unchanged afterward — proving Process-A cleanup never reaches Process B, with real reproduction instead of a stand-in marker.
- FINDING (F3): `parsePiTurnJSONL` accepted a stream with no authoritative `message_end` (empty `finalText` treated as success) and admitted `tool_execution_*` events after `agent_end` (only `message_*`/`turn_*` events carried the `!sawAgentStart || sawAgentEnd` lifecycle guard).
- FIX: added `sawFinalAssistantMessage` (required at EOF) and the same lifecycle guard on all three tool-execution event cases. Two narrowing mutants killed (evidence: `.temp/TASK-260830-y6infr/mutants-rev2.log`), plus two mutants against the new engine-reader refusal/attestation gates.
- STATUS: development still pins the immutable pseudo-version `v0.5.1-0.20260830114459-046baef11790`; `pi turn` is a real CLI verb now, but full end-to-end activation still needs the stable upstream tag the dedicated delivery task owns.

### 1100 — Guards Renamed The Adapter And Split Refusal From Dispatch
- FINDING: Fail-closed plane discovery caught the concrete adapter carrying an `MLX` identity literal on the generic launch path even though it never branched on one; `MLXEngineObservationAdapter` is now `SanitizedEngineObservationAdapter` and engine identity arrives only as injected data from the trusted assembly.
- DECISION: The concrete assembly may declare and refuse an identity but never dispatch on one. `agents_management_registry.go` is guarded by AST: an identity conditional must be a plain fail-closed refusal, and any identity `switch`/case is rejected.
- FINDING: `--profile` was reachable only on the schema-1 surface. The exact assertion now applies to the legacy standalone surface too, so `agents-infra pi spawn --profile X ...` is not a CLI parse error.
- EVIDENCE: 11 narrowing mutants killed plus one no-live mutant; the fixed-filename discovery mutant admits the called-helper live read, proving package-complete discovery — not the import denial list — is what closes that bypass. Log: `.temp/TASK-260830-y6infr/mutants.log`.
- STATUS: Development still pins the immutable pseudo-version `v0.5.1-0.20260830114459-046baef11790` of commit `046baef11790e93a7967230eec760c4563432270`. The consumer boundary `infra.BuildAndRunPiTurn` ships as a library seam; no CLI verb activates it before the stable upstream tag and final pin.

### 1345 — A Sweep For Adverse Findings Propagated Adverse Findings; The Favourable Ones From The Same Round Sat Unpropagated At Five Sites, Three Of Them Acceptance-Criteria Deliverables

- **THE REUSABLE ONE. A sweep instruction that names a direction only fixes that direction — verifying propagation of a correction has to check both polarities, or the sweep becomes a new instance of the defect it was chartered to fix, with the bias flipped.** Round four's review (1330 below) asked for and got a sweep of every read this ADR calls free, and the fix propagated its two **adverse** findings (N18, N19) into every downstream conclusion. It did not occur to anyone, including the reviewer who wrote the sweep instruction, to run the identical check on the two **favourable** findings from the same round — N13 (`sqlite_home` defaults inside `CODEX_HOME`, closing the stated "substantive risk") and N16 (codex's keyring→file fallback is gated on `Auto`, never live on the keyring branch). Both sat standing-as-open at **five sites**: §6's options-comparison table still called the closed `sqlite_home` question "a live cross-account leakage risk"; §8 still counted the withdrawn N4 hazard toward "two things, both adverse"; §13's own corrections table contradicted itself across two rows on the same finding (Q12); §11.3's implementation-gate paragraph still promised a discharged item "could still close B1-codex". **Three of the five are sections the task's own Definition of Done names as required deliverables** — the options comparison, the hazard carried forward, the implementation breakdown. The document read *more pessimistic about codex than its own evidence supported*, at exactly the places a reader goes to compare options and price the hazard. It did not feel like an error because it erred toward caution — which is exactly why a review built to catch overclaiming missed it on the first pass.
- **THE SECOND ONE, and it recurred one level up from where it was first caught.** Round one misfiled a free documentary read (Q2b, the vendor-terms question that decided the whole product) as "a decision, not the agent's to make," and that misclassification was named and fixed in round two. Round four's own sweep, while auditing *reads*, applied an identical misclassification to its own *reclassification step*: four items were moved out of the "free read" bucket into "needs a harness," and two of the four (Q4 — is the codex store-selector default stable across versions; Q5 — does claude canonicalize the config dir beyond NFC) had a free documentary half that was never separated out. Running the documentary halves cost two more free reads and did not need an account or a synthetic harness: `AuthCredentialsStoreMode`'s `#[default]` on `File` is unchanged across seven `rust-v*` tags spanning the earliest stable release to an unreleased alpha; claude's config-dir derivation applies exactly `.normalize("NFC")` to the raw env value and nothing else — no `resolve`, no trailing-slash trim, no symlink dereference, so a trailing-slash or symlinked spelling that survives NFC intact hashes to a different Keychain service name. **The pattern is the same each time: "this needs an experiment" is a classification claim, and classification claims get the same scrutiny as factual ones — check for a documentary half before filing something as harness-only.**
- **VERDICT: none moved.** This was a correction sweep, not new research, and every outcome from round four stands — B0 GO, B1-claude CONDITIONAL GO, B1-codex HELD/UNESTABLISHED, A permanent NO-GO, C GO, qwen provisional-with-a-mechanism. The fix used the `~~struck~~ — withdrawn` convention round four introduced for exactly this shape, applied to the five stale sites plus the two new documentary notes (N21, N22).
- BOUNDARY HELD: no account created, enrolled or authenticated; no credential, token or Keychain value read, printed or persisted; no `security` invocation; no login, logout, revoke or rotation. Evidence was `gh api` reads of a public repository (seven tags) and byte-window extraction from an already-installed, already-analysed claude binary.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_multi-account-auth-architecture.adr.md` only (§0 new Revision 5 paragraph, §2.4.1 rewritten with the Q4/Q5 split, new N21/N22 notes, §4 Q4/Q5 rows and recount paragraph, §6 B1-verdict row + B0 Security/gives-up rows, §8 hazard list, §11.2 W14/W16, §11.3 status paragraph, §13 Q12 row + N13 site list, §14 ledger rows 43–44). `README.md` and this logbook needed no correction — verified against the sweep, not assumed. +126/−26 lines, commit `e7a7212`, docs-only.

### 1330 — Six Free Reads Sat Unrun For Three Rounds; Running Them Cost An Hour, Moved A Verdict, And Broke A Completeness Claim About The Option We Are Shipping

- **THE REUSABLE ONE, and it is a procedure rather than an insight. When a document ranks its own unrun work, that ranking is a promise it has to be audited against — so before finalising, enumerate every read the document calls *free* and confirm each was actually run.** Review found the same defect three rounds running in `TASK-260720-3gcfd1` and, correctly, stopped fixing instances and asked for the sweep. The result: §4's cost column named **eight free reads**. One had been run, one was 403-blocked, and **six had not been run in three rounds** — while the same document called one of them *"the highest-value item on this table"*, *"the substantive risk and it should be first"*, and *"a free source audit nobody has run"*. All three superlatives described the same unrun item. **The tell is superlatives about work instead of results.** Running all six cost fourteen `gh api` calls, two `curl` probes, four local file reads and one `strings` pass.
- **THE SECOND ONE, and it is the counter-lesson, recorded so the first is not over-learned. Cheapness is a reason to run a read; it is never evidence that the read is load-bearing.** Four of the six moved something. **None moved the verdict it had been ranked against.** Round three staked B1-codex's reopening list on `sqlite_home` (Q15) and the keyring fallback (Q12), calling them *"most of what would settle it is ours and free"*. Both were run. `sqlite_home` **defaults inside `CODEX_HOME`**, so credential-isolated codex profiles do not share a state DB; the keyring path has **no** plaintext fallback at all, so the doc-versus-binary conflict this ADR carried for two rounds **never existed** — the fallback strings belong to `auto`'s implementation. Both favourable. Neither decisive, because **both were mechanism questions and what is open on codex is entitlement**. The only thing that can still decide it is a second account. **Rank a free read by what it could decide, not by what it costs.**
- **THE THIRD ONE, and it is what actually blocked the round. The read that closes a question also tells you what the question was next to — and here it falsified a completeness claim about the option we are adopting now.** Fetching `sqlite_home`'s default surfaced **`CODEX_SQLITE_HOME`**, an ambient environment variable that appeared **nowhere** in the ADR, the auth-lifecycle design, the custody research, `README.md` or `LOGBOOK.md`. The ADR said *"Namespace inputs: **One**"* for codex and *"W13 is the only one B0 depends on"*. Both false. The count came from `Codex Auth` occurring exactly once — precise about the **credential namespace**, spent at the scope of **state isolation and B0's prerequisites**. Same precise-where-derived / wider-where-used shape as rounds one to three, on the axis nobody enumerated. **An ambient input nobody composes is precisely the defect the adopted option exists to remove.**
- CHANGE OF METHOD THAT FOUND THE REST: **stop grepping for the literal you expect and enumerate what the program reads from its environment.** Codex turns out to read **eight** ambient inputs affecting its state root, credential or OAuth endpoints. Two are materially dangerous: `CODEX_API_KEY` takes precedence over *"any other auth method"* by the vendor's own source comment — so an ambient key makes a composed launch **run as the wrong identity while reporting the right one** — and `CODEX_REFRESH_TOKEN_URL_OVERRIDE` is accepted with **no allowlist**, where Claude's `CLAUDE_CODE_CUSTOM_OAUTH_URL` is checked against a three-entry list and **throws**. The ADR had treated Claude as the larger attack surface and codex as the clean single-literal story; **on this axis the comparison inverts.**
- **VERDICT MOVED: qwen "not modellable" → provisional-with-a-mechanism.** Three rounds recorded *"there is no state root, so the epic's qwen ambition has no mechanism"* — on the strength of **our own plugin** declaring `HomeEnvVar: ""`. The audit that would have checked it was listed as free in every round and run in none. qwen reads **`QWEN_HOME`**, and it namespaces the credential: `getOAuthCredsPath()` resolves `oauth_creds.json` through it. **A vendor limitation was reported where a repository defect existed.** The parallel claim about muse, gemini, agy and pi is withdrawn as unaudited — the identical claim about qwen died to one read.
- ALSO FOUND, and recorded at the standing the evidence carries rather than the one it invites: **Claude Code 2.1.248 ships a second, file-based, named-profile credential store** — `ANTHROPIC_CONFIG_DIR` / `ANTHROPIC_PROFILE` selecting `configs/<name>.json` + `credentials/<name>.json`, accepting `authentication.type = "user_oauth"`, internally labelled "WIF profile". That is most of the shape the multi-account design assumes it must build on the Keychain. **No verb that writes those files was found**, the subsystem is named for workload-identity federation, and the directory does not exist on this host. **Logged as a mechanism sighted in shipped code with its enrolment path unread** — not as an option, because spending a mechanism finding as a permission finding is the error this task spent three rounds learning.
- ANOMALY WORTH THE MOST OPERATIONALLY: **a `gh api search/code` zero is a failed or partial read, not an absence.** `repo:QwenLM/qwen-code QWEN_CODE_HOME OR QWEN_HOME` returned `total_count: 0` for a constant that is plainly in `packages/core/src/config/storage.ts`. It was found by reading the file a *different* query pointed at. This project already treats a `curl` 403 as a failed read; **a search tool whose zero looks like a result deserves the same suspicion**, and one evidence-ledger row resting on such a zero is now labelled accordingly.
- METHOD CORRECTION MADE MID-ROUND: the first pass read `openai/codex` at the **default branch**. That is a different program from the installed one. Everything was re-read at tag **`rust-v0.150.1`** (matching `codex-cli 0.150.1`) and every environment-variable literal was independently corroborated in the installed Mach-O binary with `strings`. The tag and the build agree. **"Read the vendor's source" is not one method** — reading `main` and reporting it as evidence about the installed CLI is the same widening, one layer down. The one claim that could not be pinned this way (qwen, not installed here) is labelled as unpinned rather than quietly levelled up.
- ANSWERED AGAINST THE DESIGN, recorded because sweeps are not supposed to only produce good news: the proposed credential root `~/Library/Application Support/agents-infra/auth/` is **inside** the installer-managed config dir on all three platforms, not outside it. File-level disjoint today — the installer writes one file there — but directory-level nested, so a future recursive uninstall takes credentials with it. The originally-proposed test ("assert disjointness") **cannot pass as written**.
- COST OF THE EPISODE, stated plainly: **B0's prerequisite list went from one item to five**, and it is the option recommended for scheduling now. B0 is still GO and every finding is a further argument for it. But *"ready to schedule"* described a smaller job than the document had checked.
- BOUNDARY HELD: no account created, enrolled or authenticated on either provider; no credential, token or Keychain value read, printed or persisted; **no `security` invocation**; no login, logout, revoke or rotation; no vendor CLI subcommand that touches state. The ambient-variable survey tested **presence by name only**. Evidence was `gh api` reads of two public repositories, two `curl` status probes, read-only `strings`/`grep` over already-installed binaries, and read-only reads of this repository.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_multi-account-auth-architecture.adr.md` (new §2.4 + N13–N20a, §0, §4 incl. new Q16/Q17/Q18, §5.1, §5.2, rewritten §5.3, §7.3, §9, §10.1 + new G-B0-7/8/9, §11.1 + new W20/W21/W22, rewritten §11.2 with a status column + new W23/W24, §11.3 + new H14, §12.1, §12.2, §12.3, §12.4, §13, §14 rows 31–42, §15 AC6/AC8/AC9), `README.md`, this logbook. **Decision only — nothing implemented; the multi-account work breakdown stays unstarted for both providers.**

### 1210 — A No-Go Whose Three Reasons All Failed Inspection, Including The One Review Supplied To Rescue It (**partly corrected at 1330 — two bullets below rested on reads that had still not been run**)

- **THE REUSABLE ONE. A claim stated precisely once and used in a widened form thereafter reads as evidence-backed, and the tell is that the precise form stays in the evidence section while the widened form goes into the summary, the README and the logbook.** Round two of `TASK-260720-3gcfd1` quoted OpenAI correctly, exactly once: account switching is *"not yet supported in **Codex desktop** or the native ChatGPT mobile apps"*. The word `desktop` then appeared **nowhere else in 1336 lines**. All seven *uses* of that quote compressed it to "Codex" — including the up-front verdict table, the operator-facing `README.md` and the 1140 entry above. A statement about a **GUI app** was spent as a vendor position on a **CLI**. **When you cite a quote more than once, grep your own document for the quote's narrowest word; if it appears once, every other site is a paraphrase you have not checked.**
- **THE SECOND ONE, and it is the sharper of the two. A distinction that makes one verdict cheap and is then declined for the sibling verdict is where a reader will correctly look for motivated reasoning.** Round two's best work was separating the **derivation** (`sha256(dir)[:8]`, binary-inspected, needs a per-release pin) from the **property** the feature actually needs (*different config dir ⇒ different credential entry*, vendor-published). That dissolved a recurring version-gate cost for Claude. It was then withheld from Codex in one sentence — *"Codex pays §7.2 in full"* — which three facts in the same document contradicted. Applying it symmetrically was the whole of round three's work and it changed the finding. **Apply your own best distinction to the case it hurts before you publish the case it helps.**
- **THE THIRD ONE, and it is the one worth the most: a correction carries the same standard as a claim, and the check has to be run in the direction that would preserve the answer.** Review supplied a premise to rescue the collapsing cost argument — codex's `file` store *is* plaintext `auth.json`, the design refuses a `degraded:plaintext` profile, therefore codex is forced onto `keyring` where the derivation does live — and called it "a genuinely better codex pillar". **The design it cites says the opposite in a row written to prevent exactly that reading.** `bk6owf` §6.2: *"`degraded:plaintext` means **unexplained** plaintext, not plaintext"*, and codex-on-`file` is classified **`active`** — *"the vendor's packaged default custody, not a fallback"* — because *"calling codex-on-`file` `degraded:plaintext` would refuse every default codex enrolment for a hazard that did not occur"*. Adopting the premise would have preserved the verdict on a **second** false reason. **A reviewer-supplied premise arrives with authority and no citation check; run one, especially when it lets you keep your conclusion.**
- WHAT THE SYMMETRIC APPLICATION FOUND: `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` is Codex's **packaged default**, and on the file branch the credential is just `auth.json` inside `CODEX_HOME` — a property OpenAI **documents** — with **no derivation on the path at all**; the `cli|sha256(home)[:16]` derivation exists only on the *keyring* branch. The version-gate cost does not reach Codex's default store. There **is** a real version dependency there, and neither round named it: `bk6owf` §7.1 had already spotted that the *branch selector* is itself a version-fixed constant, and round three confirmed the vendor documents that key's **default value nowhere** — the config reference lists no default and the only worked example sets `"keyring"`, which is not what ships. That cost is real, relocated, and **escapable** by setting the key explicitly per profile, the same move that dissolved claude's.
- **VERDICT MOVED: B1-codex NO-GO → HELD/UNESTABLISHED.** Not because permission was found — because all three reasons for the no-go failed and nothing replaced them. Entitlement for the Codex CLI is **UNESTABLISHED**: a third status distinct from round one's "no evidence" (an unperformed read) and round two's "closed by published position" (a widened quote). Nobody has published a yes and nobody a no. **Unchanged: B1-claude CONDITIONAL GO, B0 GO, A permanent NO-GO, C GO. ~~qwen not-modellable~~ — STRUCK at 1330: qwen reads `QWEN_HOME` and it namespaces the credential; the missing state root was our own plugin declaration, not a vendor limit. Both halves of multi-account stay unstarted** — now for the same honest reason, that the evidence is not in, rather than one gated and one closed.
- ~~**WHAT ACTUALLY MIGHT CLOSE IT, and nobody has run it.**~~ **RUN AT 1330 — and it did not close it. See the 1330 entry.** The audit was three `gh api` calls against public first-party source: `sqlite_home` **defaults inside `CODEX_HOME`** (precedence `config.toml` › `$CODEX_SQLITE_HOME` › `$CODEX_HOME`), so two credential-isolated codex profiles do **not** share a state database. The cross-account leakage worry is **closed, favourably**, and the reopening-list item is discharged. **The framing in this bullet was wrong in a way worth keeping visible:** it called this *"what actually might close it"*, and it never could have — it was a **mechanism** question, and what is open on codex is **entitlement**. The surviving half of the lesson stands and is sharper for it: *a free read you rank first and do not run is still a free read you did not run*, and correctly labelling it "unknown" does not discharge it. ~~*three rounds argued about a vendor sentence while the one free read that could decide the question went unrun*~~ — **STRUCK at 1330**: three rounds did leave it unrun, but it could not have decided the question.
- COROLLARY, and it is the practical cost of the whole episode: **a stale no-go is worse than an honest unknown.** Round two's framing said only OpenAI could reopen B1-codex, so every reopening condition was something the vendor must do. In fact three of the five are ours and free. A reader inheriting "NO-GO, the vendor closed it" runs none of them, forever. **Sharpened at 1330:** the two free ones were then run, both came back favourable, and **neither reopened anything** — they were mechanism questions, and B1-codex is held on entitlement. The corollary survives; its optimism does not. *Being ours and free does not make a question load-bearing.*
- BETTER SOURCE FOUND, and it should have been found in round two: OpenAI publishes **markdown source endpoints** at `developers.openai.com/codex/*.md` (HTTP 200), which are the same documents without a tag-stripping step and are explicitly **surface-scoped** — the body is divided into `ContentModeSwitch` blocks tagged `app`/`cli`/`ide`/`web`, so a claim about the CLI can be read off the vendor's own scoping instead of inferred. Round two scraped the rendered HTML. `CODEX_HOME` appears **exactly once** in the auth doc, as a file location; `multiple account`, `account switch` and `switching` appear **zero** times.
- SILENCE ESTABLISHED BY READING THE PLACE IT WOULD APPEAR, twice over — the pattern worth copying. (i) The CLI-scoped auth doc that would carry a multi-account statement, read successfully, carries none. (ii) `openai/codex` **Discussion #25630, "Switch Between Accounts"**, read via `gh api graphql`: **no accepted answer, and every comment `authorAssociation: NONE`** — no maintainer, member or collaborator has replied to the vendor's own thread on exactly this question. A successful read returning nothing is an absence; a 403 is not, and `help.openai.com` is still 403 from this host, so the switching article remains **second-hand and is now labelled as such in the README too**.
- ANOMALY — **a second evidence-ledger row that does not re-run, written in the same commit as the lesson about the first one.** Round two corrected row 9 (`7,7,7,8,8` → 10 in every build) and wrote "a row that does not re-run is worse than no row" into this logbook. Row 21 in that same commit recorded 20 account-switching issues, 19 open, 1 closed. The real numbers under its own recorded query are **27 / 21 open / 6 closed**. ~~The cause was method, not phrasing: `gh api search/issues` pages at **30 by default** and the row recorded no page size or counting step.~~ **STRUCK at 1330 — that cause does not reproduce, and asserting it broke this entry's own third bullet.** The query returns **27** items, which fits inside a thirty-item page, so default paging cannot have truncated round two's result to 20/19/1; run with `-f per_page=100` and run with the default page size, it returns **27 / 21 open / 6 closed both ways**. The other candidate — the narrower phrasing `account switching in:title` — gives 16 / 13 / 3, also not 20/19/1. **Neither candidate cause reproduces, so the honest status of the original miscount's cause is *unknown*.** What is fixed, and does re-run, is the method: record `-f per_page=100` and "count `items` by `state`" as part of the row. **Writing the lesson is not the same as applying it — and do not name a cause you have not reproduced, one bullet after writing that a correction carries the same standard as a claim.**
- FREE READ, PARTIAL ANSWER, REPORTED AS PARTIAL: `log_dir` is documented as defaulting to `$CODEX_HOME/log`; `sqlite_home` is documented with **no default at all**. That is half of the codex cross-profile isolation unknown answered and half still open, recorded as half rather than rounded to either end — a documented default for one key is not evidence about the other. **Completed at 1330 from source rather than docs:** `sqlite_home` **does** default inside `CODEX_HOME`. The honesty of reporting a half as a half was right; **what it did not do was prompt anyone to read the source that held the other half, which was three `gh api` calls away.** Reporting a partial correctly and leaving the cheap completion unrun for a round is the 1330 entry's whole subject.
- BOUNDARY HELD: no account created, enrolled or authenticated on either provider; no credential value read, printed or persisted; no `security` invocation; no login, logout, revoke or rotation. Round three evidence was unauthenticated `curl` of public vendor docs, `gh api` reads of a public repository, and one web search.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_multi-account-auth-architecture.adr.md` (§0, §2.3 N11/N12/N12b/N12c, new §7.3.1–7.3.3, §5.2, §6, §9, §10, §11.2, §11.3 + new H13, rewritten §12.2/§12.3 + new §12.4, §4, §13, §14 rows 19/21/25 + new 27–30, §15), `README.md`, this logbook. Decision only — nothing implemented, and the multi-account work breakdown stays unstarted for **both** providers.

### 1140 — "No Evidence" Was A Read Nobody Did, And Doing It Reversed The Verdict For One Provider (**partly corrected at 1210 — the codex claim below was widened past its source**)

- **CORRECTION NOTICE (1210).** This entry's codex claim — that OpenAI "publishes that account switching is 'not yet supported in Codex'" and that this made the codex NO-GO rest on *affirmative vendor evidence* — **dropped a scope word from its own source**. The sentence is scoped to **Codex desktop** and the native ChatGPT mobile apps: two GUI surfaces, not the Codex **CLI**, which is what this epic uses. "Not yet" is a roadmap note, not a prohibition. The claim is struck below and the corrected version is in the 1210 entry. **The Claude half of this entry is fully supported and stands unchanged** — a later reviewer re-fetched every Anthropic source and quote-checked it. **The codex verdict itself then moved at 1210 — NO-GO → HELD/UNESTABLISHED** — once this reason and the cost argument beside it both failed inspection.
- **THE REUSABLE ONE. An unperformed read is not an absence, and the tell is the cost column.** The 0842 entry above declared that the axis deciding the whole product had "**no** evidence, not weak evidence". The unknowns table had classified that question's cost as "*a decision, and it is not the agent's to make*" — while eight sibling rows in the same table were correctly classified as **free source audits**. That one misclassification is the entire mechanism of the failure: it licensed skipping the single load-bearing input while substantial effort went into binary inspection the inputs had already largely settled. Reading published terms and product documentation is free and takes twenty minutes. Only the *judgement* about whether to rely on what they say was ever the operator's. **Before you report a question as unanswerable, check whether its cost column says "decision" for something that is actually a read.**
- **WHAT THE READ FOUND, and it is not what the no-go assumed.** Anthropic's own Claude Code docs at `code.claude.com/docs/en/authentication` state that Claude Code "*keys the macOS Keychain entry to that directory too, so a session with a different `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` reads a different entry*", and the env-var reference calls `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` "*useful for running multiple accounts side by side*" **with a worked `claude-work` alias example**. The mechanism three tasks reverse-engineered from minified binaries is a published product contract, and the use case is vendor-recommended. The Consumer Terms restrict *sharing* an account with other people and place no limit on how many accounts one person holds.
- **THE SECOND LESSON — a documented property is a different dependency class than a derived one.** The no-go's cost argument priced a version allowlist against the `sha256(NFC(dir))[:8]` service-name derivation: every claude upgrade refuses every profile-selected launch until a human re-runs the pin, roughly every two days. But multi-account never needed the *formula* — it needs the *property*, "different dir ⇒ different entry", and the vendor now promises it. **Pricing a gate without asking whether the thing it guards is actually on the path is how a cost argument survives longer than it should.** The allowlist is dropped for claude and stands in full for codex.
- **THE THIRD LESSON — one verdict over two vendors is the same under-specification the ADR itself diagnosed.** Its own N5 says any design that says "namespace the state root" without naming the provider is under-specified — and then it wrote "nobody has established that **either** vendor permits". The providers are sharply asymmetric and the asymmetry runs opposite to the assumption: **Anthropic is silent in its legal terms and loud in its product documentation; OpenAI is loud in its help centre about ChatGPT web and ~~negative about the surface that matters~~ *silent about the surface that matters*** — corrected at 1210: OpenAI's negative sentence is about Codex *desktop*, and the CLI is unaddressed either way.
- CORRECTED VERDICT: **B1-claude CONDITIONAL GO** — one empirical gate left (does a second concurrent enrolment leave the first working server-side over 24 h), runnable by an operator who already holds two accounts. ~~**B1-codex NO-GO, now on affirmative vendor evidence**: OpenAI publishes that account switching is "not yet supported in Codex"…~~ **STRUCK at 1210 — widened past its source; see the correction notice above.** What survives: the reachable Codex docs mention multi-account nowhere and treat `CODEX_HOME` purely as the location of `auth.json`, and `openai/codex` returns account-switching issues (**27, 21 open, 6 closed — also corrected at 1210; this entry's 20/19/1 does not re-run**) none closed as already-supported, including reports of projects, conversations and usage quota surviving a switch across accounts. **B1-codex is no longer a NO-GO at all: it is HELD/UNESTABLISHED as of 1210, because the cost argument in this entry's price paragraph also failed on codex's default store.** The epic's headline capability is **not deliverable as one feature**, which is different from neither half working.
- ADVERSE FINDING — **the docs and the binary disagree about codex's `keyring` store.** `learn.chatgpt.com/docs/auth` documents a plaintext fallback for `auto` **only**; `keyring` is documented as storing in the OS credential store, full stop. The binary carries `failed to {load,save} … keyring, falling back to file storage:` on **both** legs. So `keyring` cannot be relied on to mean "never plaintext", and `3moaky`'s "force `cli_auth_credentials_store = keyring`" implication is weakened further, not rescued.
- FAILED READ, RECORDED AS SUCH — `help.openai.com`, `openai.com/policies` and `chatgpt.com` return **HTTP 403 to every request from this host**, browser UA and fetch tool alike. The OpenAI account-switching text reached this task through the search index on two independently-phrased queries and is labelled **second-hand** everywhere it appears; the ToU free-tier multi-account clause could not be corroborated at all and nothing depends on it. **A 403 is a failed read, not an absence** — which is the same distinction this whole entry is about, applied to the evidence *for* the correction rather than against it.
- ANOMALY — an evidence-ledger row that does not re-run. Round one recorded `CLAUDE_CODE_CUSTOM_OAUTH_URL` occurrence counts as 7,7,7,8,8 across five builds. The real count is **10 in every build** via `LC_ALL=C grep -ao … | wc -l`; the builds are Mach-O arm64 executables, so a `grep -c` without `-a` returns exit 1 and no count at all. A ledger exists so a later reader can re-run it — a row that does not re-run is worse than no row.
- BOUNDARY HELD: no account created, enrolled or authenticated on either provider; no credential value read, printed or persisted; no `security` invocation; no login, logout, revoke or rotation. Entitlement was attacked with documents only.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_multi-account-auth-architecture.adr.md` (new §2.3 documentary read, §7.3 dependency scoping, per-provider §12, corrected §14), `README.md`, this logbook. Decision only — nothing implemented, and the multi-account work breakdown stays unstarted for **both** providers.
