### 0912 — Auth Lifecycle: The Hazard Is Closed By Making The Sentence Unsayable, Not By A Warning
- DESIGN: `.research/260831_extensible-auth-method-lifecycle.design.md` separates provider, runtime, profile alias, account identity, auth method and credential handle, and puts a lifecycle (enrol/use/refresh/retire) over it for both providers' real custody models. The credential handle is a **locator, never a container**: it holds non-secret coordinates so `status` can observe presence with `stat` and metadata lookups, and no operation dereferences it to a value.
- THE LOAD-BEARING MOVE: every auth method declares a custodian class, and native-OAuth methods are pinned to `vendor-opaque` and cannot be declared anything else (invariant C1). "agents-infra holds the Claude subscription credential" is not expressible in the model, so no code path can reach for the ACL/permission enforcement that triggers Claude's fail-open-to-plaintext. A prohibition list plus a warning would have been the wrong shape — the model refuses to represent the mistake.
- DETECTION IS STAT-ONLY AND THREE-VALUED: `<state_root>/.credentials.json` present means the fallback fired -> `degraded:plaintext`, refuse to launch, explicit per-profile dated acknowledgement to override. A failed or denied observation is `unknown`, never `absent` and never `active` — a permission error reading the state root is not evidence that the fallback did not fire.
- ORPHANED-ITEM HAZARD, NEW HERE: on macOS the credential is NOT inside the state root — the Keychain item lives in the login keychain under a service derived FROM the root's path. Deleting the root destroys the derivation input and strands an item nothing can name. So vendor logout must run BEFORE the root is deleted, and agents-infra never deletes a Keychain item it did not create (a wrong derivation would delete some other account's credential); it prints the non-secret coordinates as a tombstone instead.
- VERSION GATE REFUSES, IT DOES NOT WARN: claude range 2.1.234-2.1.248 (the reviewer's F1 breadth, not 2.1.248 alone); codex **0.150.1 only**, because that is the single version the derivation was proven at and no range was invented. Unreadable version = `unknown` = refuse. Each pin needs its negative variants — different-root, NFC-vs-NFD, and digest **narrowing** to 7/9 hex — because a delete-only mutant proves the suffix exists and says nothing about its derivation.
- `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` set in the operator environment is a refusal condition: it repoints the credential namespace independently of the config dir and would silently detach a profile from its own root.

### 0912 — The Home Variable Must Be Written On Every Launch, Because Today It Is Inherited
- NEW FINDING, this repo: `runClaude`/`runCodex` in `tools/agents-infra/main.go` build `exec.Command` and never assign `cmd.Env`. The only two `cmd.Env` assignments in the whole module are `pi_launch_posix.go:642` and `pi_platform_windows.go:140`. A primary session therefore lands in whatever namespace the operator's shell happened to carry — the primary-session launcher is a fourth gap alongside the three `home_env` layers, and it is in THIS repo, not the skill repos.
- SHARPENING of the research's "not injected": claude's `ChildEnv` is `agentic.WithRunContext(filterRuntimeEnv(parent), req)` and `runtimeEnvKeys` is exactly `["CLAUDECODE"]` (`skill-agents-management/pkg/agentic/systems/claude/env.go:112`). The child does not get a default — it **inherits** the parent's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. So a parent with one set propagates its account to every child regardless of what that child's runtime declared. Inheritance silently decides the account; that is a cross-account leakage path, not merely a missing feature.
- CONSEQUENCE: the fix is "write the home variable on every launch of a home-bearing runtime, including writing the DEFAULT home explicitly when no profile is selected", so the value always comes from a decision and never from an ancestor.
- THE FIX BELONGS IN ONE PLACE: `planCommand` already does `cmd.Env = plan.Env` (`skill-project-management/tools/board-cli/internal/spawn/spawn.go:940`), so if `ChildEnv` writes the home into `Plan.Env`, `internal/spawn` needs no change at all. Recorded so it is not patched twice in two places that later disagree.

### 0842 — Multi-Account Auth: Split Verdict (**corrected at 1140 — see below; the original headline claim was false**)
- **CORRECTION NOTICE (1140).** This entry originally recorded, as THE REUSABLE ONE, that vendor entitlement had "**no** evidence, not weak evidence". **That was false.** It described a read that had not been performed, not an absence, and free published first-party material answers the question on both providers. The claim is struck below, the reusable lesson is replaced, and the corrected verdict is in the 1140 entry. The binary-inspection findings in this entry all reproduce and stand.
- DECISION (`TASK-260720-3gcfd1`, ADR `.research/260831_multi-account-auth-architecture.adr.md`): split verdict. agents-infra-owned Keychain custody of a native login is **NO-GO permanently**; deterministic child-environment composition for **one** account per runtime is **GO now**; provider-native delegation is **GO for managed automation**; ~~qwen stays not-modellable (`HomeEnvVar: ""`)~~ **STRUCK at 1330 — `QWEN_HOME` exists; that was our plugin's defect reported as qwen's limit**. ~~multi-account native switching is NO-GO on present evidence~~ — **superseded at 1140: CONDITIONAL GO for Claude, NO-GO for Codex.**
- ~~**THE REUSABLE ONE.** …it has **no** evidence, not weak evidence…~~ **STRUCK.** The first half of that observation was right and is kept: three research tasks established the *mechanism* and nobody had touched the *entitlement*, and when the researched half is the tractable half, "we proved a lot" reads like progress and can be zero progress on the decision. **The second half was the error.** "Nobody has established it" was asserted without looking, and the corrected lesson is in the 1140 entry.
- WHAT SAVED IT FROM BEING A BARE NO: the plumbing gap blocking multi-account is also a **live defect today**. `runClaude`/`runCodex` never assign `cmd.Env` and claude's spawn `ChildEnv` strips exactly one key, so every namespace variable is **inherited** from the ancestor shell, and `providerlimits` keys rate-limit identity off `os.Getenv` in the *parent*. Fixing that needs no version gate, no pin, no second account, no credential. Splitting the option in two turned a no-go into a scheduled correctness fix plus a held feature.
- FINDING — **setting `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` to the default path is not a no-op.** `t = e!==void 0 ? !e : !process.env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`: the service-name hash suffix is empty **only** when the variable is unset or empty, and the live item has no suffix. So `bk6owf`'s Invariant L1 ("when no profile is selected the launch writes the default home explicitly") would have moved every default claude launch into an empty namespace and reported the operator as not logged in. Corrected to **L1′**: compose the child environment, and for the default profile that means *removing* the variable. An explicit unset is as much a decision as an assignment.
- FINDING — **the claude namespace has three environment inputs, not one.** `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (hash suffix), `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` (replaces the dir input), and `CLAUDE_CODE_CUSTOM_OAUTH_URL` (literal `-custom-oauth` infix via `getOauthConfig`, which **throws** unless the value is one of three approved fedstart/staging endpoints). Codex, by contrast, has exactly one input and a single literal `Codex Auth` service. A design that says "namespace the state root" without naming the provider is under-specified.
- SETTLES a `1g880w` unknown, positively: `OAUTH_FILE_SUFFIX` **is** non-empty in the shipped prod binary's reachable set — `["", "-staging-oauth", "-local-oauth", "-custom-oauth"]`, with the build channel hardcoded `"prod"` and `-custom-oauth` selected from a plain env var. Verified in all five installed builds 2.1.234–2.1.248.
- NARROWS `bk6owf` Q12, adversely: codex's `login/src/auth/storage.rs` carries `failed to load CLI auth from keyring, falling back to file storage:` **and** `failed to save auth to keyring, falling back to file storage:`. So a keyring→file fallback path exists on both legs. **Honest label: these are string literals, not control flow** — whether the fallback is gated on `store == auto` is still unestablished. Q12 moves from *unknown* to *a fallback exists, gating unknown*, which is a narrowing, not an answer. A third unmodelled backend ("encrypted auth storage") also exists.
- ANOMALY — **a version range stated as an interval asserts versions nobody saw.** `bk6owf` pinned claude to `2.1.234 – 2.1.248` on four verified points. 2.1.235 was installed and unverified; it is now verified (five points), and the interval still claims ten unexamined versions (2.1.237–2.1.246). Ranges over an ordered version space should be **allowlists of verified points**, not intervals, unless the interpolation is stated. Choosing the allowlist made the priced cost worse, which is the point.
- THE PRICE, stated because the decision has to own it: 5 claude builds landed on this host in 11 days (14 upstream patch versions across the window). With multi-account enrolled and an honest allowlist gate, **every** claude upgrade refuses **every** profile-selected launch until a human runs the pin test with all negative variants and extends the list — roughly every two days, on the critical path of the primary tool. Codex refuses on its very next upgrade; its range is one version. **Partly superseded at 1140, and further at 1210:** this prices the `sha256[:8]` *derivation*, and multi-account does not need the derivation on Claude — it needs "different config dir, different Keychain entry", which Anthropic documents. ~~The price stands in full for codex~~ — **STRUCK at 1210**: codex's *packaged default* `file` store has no derivation either and its isolation property is vendor-documented, so the price does not stand there. A real but different and escapable version dependency was found in its place (the store-selector default). It does not apply to claude.
- CARRIED FORWARD UNCHANGED: `bk6owf` §6.1's three-way split of the plaintext hazard — declaration eliminated by C1, implementation mitigated by a CI grep, input mitigated by C2's launch-time gate that a consistent two-field rewrite still passes. Two thirds run at build or launch time. Not averaged into one reassuring word.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_multi-account-auth-architecture.adr.md`, `README.md` index. Decision only — nothing implemented, and the multi-account work breakdown is recorded unstarted by design.

### 0810 — A Fixed Invariant Leaks In The Section The Rework Never Opened, And The Cited Instances Undercount It
- FINDING (reviewer, CR rev2 of `TASK-260720-bk6owf`, recorded here because the reviewer role is read-only on the repo): both rev1 defects were fixed correctly *at the site they were found* and both survived elsewhere. The rework's own hunk map is the tell — 20 hunks, every one inside a section a finding named, and section 8 was never opened.
- SHAPE 1 — the corrected claim survives verbatim where nobody looked. 3.2/3.3/4.3/6.1/10 all said custodian class is a total function with no declared field. `design.md:798` (8.3, extensibility) still said "the custodian class is a property the method declares" — rev1 text, in the one section governing how a *new* method enters the system. Its add-a-method recipe was "one registry entry and one adapter", omitting the mapping arm 3.2 makes mandatory.
- SHAPE 2 — the invariant leaks one line from its own fix. 5.4 refused `remove` on `local: unknown|failed`. `design.md:248` (3.6) still drew `active|degraded|unknown --logout--> retired-local --remove--> removed`, unconditional, and the producer had edited that same diagram one line above. `retired-local` was a positive assertion of local retirement produced from `unknown`, and it appeared exactly once in 908 lines, in no vocabulary and no other section.
- SHAPE 3 — deleting a derived field does not remove its input surface. 3.3 dropped stored `custodian_class` because "an on-disk record an operator or a migration could edit" is an input surface. But `custodian_class = f(method)` and `method` is a field of that same record (3.5). A migration flipping `method` to `api-key` passes H3 (coordinates unchanged), passes the pin, and reads `active` in 6.2 — landing a native credential on the `host-owned` branch where 5.3's prohibitions do not apply by their own scoping.
- **THE REUSABLE ONE, and it is the count, not the shapes.** rev3 stopped fixing citations and swept all 908 lines for both invariants. The reviewer cited 3 sites; the sweep found **11**. Five more carried the *same two claims* in sections no finding had named: `refresh_capability()` let an adapter declare *who refreshes*, which is the "Who refreshes" column of 3.2's class table and therefore the pairing C1 removed wearing another word; 4.3's **Refresh** column sat un-annotated beside a **Custodian** column explicitly marked "rendered"; H2 in 3.3 said "never `absent`" and omitted "never `active`" while H2 in section 10 said both; 6.2's `absent` row required "every observation succeeded" and its `active` row did not; 5.1 step 8 had the same asymmetry; and a 7.2 pin negative ("no keyring item appears") was satisfiable by a *failed* lookup.
- THE PATTERN IN THAT LIST: the leaks cluster on the **reassuring** side of each invariant. `absent` gets hardened because absence obviously needs evidence; `active` reads like the default and gets missed. A declared *custodian* gets deleted; a declared *refresh owner* — the same claim, different noun — survives. Sweep for the concept's synonyms and for the invariant's *other* direction, not for the sentence that was rewritten.
- ROOT CAUSE (all of it): a claim of the form "X is structural, not checked" is a claim about the *whole document*, but it gets verified against the paragraph that makes it, and its fix gets verified against the line that was cited.
- METHOD THAT WORKED: grep the verb near the concept (`declares`, `set`, `configured`, `stored`) rather than the rewritten sentence; read the diff's hunk map and treat zero-hunk sections as the suspect set; and for a two-sided invariant, enumerate both sides separately — the side nobody worries about is the side that leaks.
- ALSO FIXED IN rev3: 3.2 now admits the profile record as a third checked surface and adds **C2**, a launch-time gate refusing a computed class that disagrees with the recorded backend/store (`E_AUTH_CUSTODY_INCONSISTENT`). C2 is labelled checked, not structural: it turns a one-field forgery into a two-field consistent rewrite, which is a reduction and not an elimination, and 6.1 now budgets three halves rather than two. Q13 carries the seal-the-record decision.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_extensible-auth-method-lifecycle.design.md` (908 -> 1047). STATUS: rev2 changes requested -> rev3 handed to review.

### 0748 — Correction: `already-absent` Is A Positive Claim, And A Read Failure Can Forge It
- CORRECTS the 0912 entry below on two points, and records the shape that produced both. Original entries left intact.
- FINDING (the reusable one): a result vocabulary that carries `unknown` on one field and omits it on another **leaks at the omission**, and the leak is only dangerous if the omitting field gates something irreversible. `TASK-260720-bk6owf`'s design gave `logout` a `remote_revoke: {revoked|not-attempted|unsupported|unknown}` and a `local: {invalidated|already-absent|failed}` — and gated `remove`'s state-root deletion on `local`. `already-absent` is a **positive assertion of absence**, so a locked login keychain, a swallowed permission error, or a vendor that exits 0 printing nothing all classify as "there was nothing there" and destruction proceeds. On macOS that destroys the Keychain service-name derivation input and strands an item nothing can ever name again.
- ROOT CAUSE: the invariant ("`unknown` never falls back to `absent` or `active`") was stated once and then re-derived by hand at eleven consumer sites. Ten held. The one that leaked was the destructive one. Stating an invariant does not distribute it.
- FIX: `local` gains `unknown`; `invalidated` and `already-absent` each require a **successful** metadata observation, never an exit code; vendor silence is `unknown`; `remove` refuses on `unknown` and `failed` (`E_AUTH_LOGOUT_UNCONFIRMED`, nothing deleted); the tombstone is written by every `remove` that did not positively confirm invalidation, not only by `leave-vendor-state`.
- CORRECTION 1 — "not expressible" was false. The 0912 entry says the custodian-class pairing "is not expressible in the model". The same document then called it "a config error refused at load". If it is refused at load it **is** expressible, and the mechanism was a checked gate wearing the word structural. Now genuinely inexpressible: custodian class is a total function `Method -> class` with no field on any input surface, and the `CredentialHandle` lost its stored `custodian_class` because an on-disk record is an input surface too. What remains checked (mapping exhaustiveness; the `chmod`-absent grep) is now labelled as checked.
- CORRECTION 2 — "the only two `cmd.Env` assignments in the whole module" was a narrow grep reported as an enumeration. `grep "cmd.Env"` is case-sensitive and misses `command.Env`, `piCmd.Env`, `runtimeCmd.Env`. The module has **seven** child-environment assignments: `pi_shared_broker_darwin.go:384`, `pi_shared_client_darwin.go:516,693`, `pi_launch_posix.go:223,306,642`, `pi_platform_windows.go:140`. All seven are pi launches and pi declares `HomeEnvVar: ""`, so the conclusion and P-AI-1's scope are unchanged — but the stated evidence was not the evidence.
- NOTE: codex's packaged default store is `file`, so a lifecycle written only for the keyring branch pins a derivation the default configuration does not use — a gate that passes vacuously rather than one that fails. `degraded:plaintext` now means *unexplained* plaintext, keyed on the recorded store: codex-on-`file` is `active`, codex-on-`keyring`-with-`auth.json` is degraded because that failure posture is unknown.
- SCOPE: `.research/260831_extensible-auth-method-lifecycle.design.md` (c3a467a). STATUS: handed to review as CR rev2.

### 0648 — CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR Does Namespace The macOS Keychain; The Prior Audit Was Wrong
- REFUTATION: `TASK-260720-3moaky` decision item 4 said Claude has no per-account selector on macOS and that `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` relocates credentials on Linux/Windows only. True of the docs, **false of the implementation at 2.1.248**. The Keychain SERVICE name carries a `-${sha256(NFC(configDir)).hex[:8]}` suffix whenever `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is set. Proven by driving the real binary against a synthetic empty profile behind a `security` shim that logs only the non-secret `-a`/`-s` selectors: it queried `Claude Code-credentials-31c6920d`, the independently computed digest, and reported `Not logged in` while the live default-namespace item sat untouched. The live item's service is exactly `Claude Code-credentials` with no suffix, which is what the same function emits when the variable is unset — both halves corroborated.
- CODEX DERIVATION, MEASURED not inferred: Keychain service `Codex Auth`, account `cli|sha256(canonical CODEX_HOME).hex[:16]`. Two synthetic homes logged in with a fabricated non-credential string produced `cli|c2e40b5f045197b0` and `cli|c8c1b4ad6d576395`, exact matches; a third never-enrolled home reported `Not logged in` as the negative control. Both synthetic items deleted, absence re-verified, live `~/.codex/auth.json` mtime and the live Claude item's `cdat`/`mdat` unchanged throughout.
- CODEX DEFAULT IS FILE, NOT KEYCHAIN: the packaged client bakes `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` into its fixed defaults, so `~/.codex/auth.json` (0600) is the effective default custody on this machine and Keychain is opt-in. `auto` was never the default here.
- CLAUDE FAILS OPEN, AND THAT KILLS EXTERNAL CUSTODY: the store is a composite `keychain-with-plaintext-fallback`. On a non-transient Keychain write failure it writes `<storageDir>/.credentials.json` at 0600 with `Warning: Storing credentials in plaintext.` **and deletes the Keychain item**. So the obvious enforcement mechanism for external custody — deny the CLI write access to the item it owns — does not fail closed, it downgrades the credential to plaintext on disk. External Keychain custody of a native login is a NO-GO on that alone, before the refresh argument.
- REFRESH IS AN IN-PLACE OVERWRITE: `security add-generic-password -U -a $USER -s <svc> -X <hex(json)>`. The live item's `cdat` is 2026-08-19 and `mdat` is 2026-08-31 — same item, rewritten, no re-login. Any externally held copy is stale from the next refresh onward, silently. Side hazard: the hex payload goes on `security -i` stdin only while the command line is <= 4032 bytes; above that it is passed **as argv**, i.e. into the process table.
- BETTER THAN ASSUMED, ONE POINT: Claude write-back IS serialized cross-process by a `proper-lockfile` on `<storageDir>/.storage-write` (10 retries, 15s stale). Lock scope and Keychain namespace derive from the same directory, so they cannot disagree. The prior audit had this as unknown.
- UNDOCUMENTED LEVER: `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` repoints the credential namespace independently of `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; set-but-empty selects the default unsuffixed namespace.

### 0648 — The home_env Gap Is Three Layers Deep, And Two Of Its Descriptions Were Off
- CONFIRMED: an operator cannot run two genuinely separate simultaneous accounts for one agentic system today. `remoteconfig.SpawnRuntimeDeclaration.HomeEnv` has exactly four non-test references — the field, its doc, a `strings.TrimSpace`, and a read-only copy into `project_config()` at `auth.go:578`. Nothing injects it.
- CORRECTION 1: it is **normalized**, not validated. `agentic_system` and `broker` are parsed against closed vocabularies and refused with typed errors; `home_env` gets a trim and nothing else — no env-var-name syntax check, no agreement check against the system's declared `HomeEnvVar`. A typo is accepted in silence, which is worse than validated-and-unused because it looks configured.
- CORRECTION 2: the limit plane is not blind to home env vars — it reads the **wrong one**. `providerlimits/identity.go:112-123` does `os.Getenv(capabilities.HomeEnvVar)`, i.e. the PARENT orchestrator's own process environment. It never sees the launch's home. So if a child were launched under a different `CODEX_HOME` today, `IdentityKey(provider, home)` would still hash the parent's home and both accounts' rate-limit state would collide on one file — exactly the drift `identity.go`'s own header comment warns about.
- LAYER 2, the one that is purely dead: `agentic.LaunchRequest.Home` -> `Plan.Home` is computed in `plan.go:176-190` and read by no production code. Neither the codex nor the claude plugin's `childEnv` writes `HomeEnvVar` into the child environment, and the launch site `internal/spawn/spawn.go:938-945` consumes Binary, Argv, Env, Stdin, WorkDir — `Home` is not among them.
- ALL THREE FAILURES ARE SILENT. Whatever closes this needs a negative test per layer that fails when the home is dropped, not a positive test that it is carried.

### 0125 — Runtime Decision: NO-GO On Both Candidates, Python mlx-lm Stays Default
- DECISION: **NO-GO on MLX Swift and NO-GO on llama.cpp.** Python `mlx-lm` remains the default local Qwen runtime; no installed configuration was changed and `profiles.qwen-local` is untouched. Published as `articles/260831_local-qwen-runtime-comparison-study/` and `.research/260831_local-qwen-runtime-comparison-study.md` (byte-identical).
- WEIGHTING, PRE-REGISTERED BEFORE THE NUMBERS: peak resident memory and decode throughput equal, with TTFT, 75,000-token capacity, tool-call parity, stability and migration risk alongside. Best overall compromise, explicitly not the single-axis winner — and llama.cpp *is* the single-axis winner on speed.
- MLX SWIFT: scored, `accepted=false`, one blocker — `long_prompt_8k` peak footprint **1.1512x** against a `<= 1.10` bar, reproduced at 1.144x and 1.151x across two independent reruns on two builds. Retired as a candidate.
- LLAMA.CPP: rejected **for now, not retired**, on three things — the gate refused the pair (exit 4, no `decision.json`) because two `contextPolicy` terms are `not-reported`; an equally-weighted axis (memory) produced **no comparison at all**, zero windows scored on both sides; and of the measured advantage only TTFT (0.629x at 8k) and prefill (1.590x) are outside this host's ~±20% single-run noise floor, while decode (+15.8%, +20.1%) is not.
- NO BREAK-EVEN EXISTS: the pre-registered crossover `L* = (TTFT_p - TTFT_l) / (1/r_l - 1/r_p)` needs one runtime leading TTFT and the other leading decode. llama.cpp leads both, so `L*` is **-15.73** (`short_prompt`) and **-1946.62** (`long_prompt_8k`) output tokens — negative, therefore no crossover at any positive response length. Reported as an absence rather than manufactured.
- CROSS-ARM COMPARISON REFUSED: MLX Swift and llama.cpp may not be tabled against each other. Different incumbent build and KV pin, different memory instrument, two campaigns two days apart — and **the incumbent itself decoded 1.46-1.51x faster in the earlier campaign** (10.271 -> 6.781 and 9.723 -> 6.670 tok/s), a swing larger than either candidate's advantage and not currently understood.
- REOPENING CONDITIONS, in priority order: fix the memory instrument at its design (read the mapped component in-process instead of forking `vmmap`; merely re-deriving the cost term upward was refused as confirmation bias), then re-run the pair for memory, then repeat the scored scenarios three times, then raise a live effective-configuration report with llama.cpp upstream, then explain the +6.2 GB anonymous soak climb, then investigate why the incumbent's configured prompt cache never fires.
- STANDING CONSTRAINT: the pinned `mlx-lm` fork is held until an accepted replacement carries its fixes. `STORY-260830-2vrhg1`'s `--max-kv-size` is exactly such a fix and must not be stranded.

### 0125 — The Article's Own Verifier Was Attacked, Not Assumed
- FINDING: `reproduce.zsh` in the article snapshot re-asserts the structural claims the decision rests on (exit 4, no decision file, non-overlapping intervals, exact prompt-token parity, **zero memory windows scored on both sides**, **no positive break-even**), not just checksums. 13 narrowing mutants — each falsifying one claim and then regenerating `expected-figures.json` and `SHA256SUMS` from its own mutated artifacts, so only the structural block can catch it — are all caught, each by the check naming the claim it attacks.
- SURVIVORS, REPORTED: replacing `recompute.py` with a stub that copies the expectation passes cleanly (**B1**), and deleting the structural block passes cleanly (**D1**, the expected delete-only control). A green run proves the article's figures follow arithmetically from the sealed records **as shipped**; it does not prove a modified snapshot is honest. Same class boundary the MLX Swift arm reported for its own gate, and closed no further for the same reason.
- FULL CAMPAIGN: `articles/260831_local-qwen-runtime-comparison-study/artifacts/analysis/mutant-campaign.md`.

## 2026-08-30

### 1527 — Pi Process-A Contract Exposed Two Boundary Regressions
- REGRESSION: A generic `ExitCode()` handler admitted ordinary `exec.ExitError` and changed legacy provider-child exit 1 to its raw child code; `main.go` now recognizes only `PiTurnProcessAError`.
- FINDING: Windows returned success from `ValidatePiExecutionEnvironment` without validating malformed, duplicate, loader, endpoint, or model-control entries.
- FIX: `tools/agents-infra/internal/infra/pi_environment.go` applies the closed environment contract on every platform and distinguishes malformed input from denied names.

### 1527 — Accepted Agents-Management Candidate Integrated
- DECISION: The trusted assembly registers real Pi, local-models, MLX engine, sanitized observation adapter, and runtime declarations through `vendorplugin.Registry`; launch callers cannot supply evidence.
- DECISION: Pi thinking remains profile configuration while the system declares `EffortTransportNone`; `qwen-infra` remains a product label and cannot alias shipped qwen-code/Alibaba.
- STATUS: Development pins immutable commit `046baef11790e93a7967230eec760c4563432270` as pseudo-version `v0.5.1-0.20260830114459-046baef11790`; the required stable immutable release tag is not yet published upstream.

### 2113 — The Fixed Pin Term Traded Places With A Broken One
- FINDING: The corrected llama.cpp pair ran in full (six scenarios, both runtimes, sequential, MTP off, 58m55s, exit **4**). `contextPolicy` refused again, but not on KV: `kv=76800` now agrees on both sides because STORY-260830-2vrhg1 gave the pinned `mlx-lm` fork `--max-kv-size`. The refusal moved to the pin's other two terms — `prefill-step=not-reported;reasoning=not-reported` on the candidate.
- ROOT CAUSE: The same change that made the incumbent pinnable made llama.cpp unpinnable. LOGBOOK 0705 moved all three `contextPolicy` terms off argv and onto live `/v1/models` `meta.runtime_config`, because argv is not what the process parsed. `llama-server` b10621 publishes no such report.
- MEASURED, not inferred: probed the same build and GGUF under `--ubatch-size 2048 --reasoning-effort medium`. `/v1/models` `meta` = `vocab_type, n_vocab, n_ctx, n_ctx_train, n_embd, n_params, size, ftype`. `/props` = `build_info, model_path, total_slots, chat_template, modalities, default_generation_settings{n_ctx, params}`. `/slots[]` = `id, is_processing, n_ctx, speculative`. **No `n_batch` or `n_ubatch` anywhere.** `params.reasoning_format` read `"none"` under `--reasoning-effort medium`, so it is the response-format field and does not track the launch — the same `/props` trap already recorded for speculation.
- DECISION NEEDED: either llama.cpp gains a live effective-configuration report, or this pair is permanently unscoreable, or an admission clause is weakened to read argv for a runtime that cannot report. The third reopens the exact defect the live derivation closed and was not taken.

### 2113 — A Coverage Bound Sized To A 0.9 GiB Target Refuses A 28 GiB One
- REGRESSION AGAINST THE REAL WORKLOAD: `maximumMappedFileSampleGapSeconds` = `samplingIntervalSeconds + 2 x observedMappedFileReadCostSeconds` = 7.0 s, with the reader cost calibrated at 0.608-0.850 s against ~3 MB and ~0.9 GiB targets. On the actual 26-45 GiB runtimes one `vmmap -summary` costs a **median 2.2-2.6 s and up to 5.8 s**, derived from the run's own raw series as `gap - 5.0 s sleep`. 268 of 288 baseline and 179 of 200 candidate mapped observations exceeded the bound.
- CONSEQUENCE: memory refused on every **scenario window and both process-wide peaks** of both runtimes except `short_prompt` on the candidate. **No scenario in the pair has a scored memory value on both sides, including `context_75k`.** The gate failed safe and said so; it did not score from a stale value.
- BUT THE ACCOUNTING IS NOT "EVERY WINDOW": four more windows — `warmupMemory` and `soakMemory` on both runtimes — came back `status=measured`, `issues=[]`, with populated `scoredBytes` (29,120,518,072 / 29,827,094,504 baseline; 34,248,152,988 / 44,346,176,540 candidate). They are `measured` because they **never reach the coverage gate**: `BenchmarkPass.swift:100` and `:107` construct them by direct `RuntimeMemoryPeak(summarizing:)`, while the gate lives in `BenchmarkFootprintSampler.coveredPeak` (`BenchmarkFootprintSampler.swift:333-345`), reached only from `currentWindowPeak()`, `processPeakSoFar()` and `capturePeaks()`. `RuntimeMemoryPeak.init(summarizing:)` sets `.measured` on "all reads complete" alone, with no coverage judgement, so `validatedScoredBytes` returns a value for them.
- AND THEY ARE THE WORST-COVERED SERIES IN THE PAIR: baseline `soakMemory` is 20 stamps at 13.862/14.682/15.117 s min/median/max mapped gaps, **19 of 19 over the 7.0 s bound** and 19 of 19 over the Mach 125 ms bound; candidate `soakMemory` is 20 stamps at 10.141/10.916/13.129 s, likewise 19/19 on both bounds. Both `warmupMemory` windows are a single point, which `coveredPeak` would refuse as `resident-memory-sampling-coverage-insufficient`. So a 15 s-gap series publishes with a score while a 0.05 s series with one 0.62 s hiccup is refused as `partial`.
- BOUNDED: `RuntimeBenchmark.decide` references neither window — neither name appears anywhere in `Sources/MLXSwiftRuntimeContract/` — so **no scored comparison consumed them** and the pair's admission outcome is unaffected. This is a reporting defect, not a scoring one, and the bypass itself is an open instrumentation debt.
- DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE: re-deriving the cost from the 28 GB targets would put the bound near 16 s and would probably make these windows score. Doing that after seeing one's own run refused, on data whose direction was already known, is the confirmation-bias move the brief names. Recorded for review to rule on before the next run, not after.
- SEPARATE: the Mach series held its own 125 ms bound inside every fast scenario window (0.060-0.069 s max gaps) and exceeded it only under load — 0.618 s and 0.273 s inside baseline `context_75k` at host load 13.995, and eight 0.13-0.29 s gaps on the candidate's whole-pass series.

### 2113 — Corrected Decode Overturns The Withdrawn Deficit
- MEASURED: on one shared generated-event definition (`content`, `reasoning`, `reasoning_content`), llama.cpp decodes **faster** than the Python baseline on both scenarios where decode is meaningfully measured — 8.145 vs 6.781 tok/s (+20.1%) and 7.724 vs 6.671 (+15.8%). The withdrawn frame-derived "about 10% slower" claim is overturned; revision 1's direction reproduces.
- WITHDRAWN AS A DECODE RESULT: `context_75k` decode (8.776 vs 8.027 tok/s) is **not reportable and must not reach the article**. Its budget is `completionTokens: 16` on both runtimes, so the figure is 15 tokens over a ~1.9 s (baseline) / ~1.7 s (candidate) tail after a 950-1280 s prefill; a single scheduling hiccup moves it several percent. That scenario stays valid for capacity, TTFT and prefill, and is to be cited for those three only.
- REPRODUCED: `long_prompt_8k` TTFT 107.216 vs 67.427 s (0.629x, revision 1 read 0.623x); prefill 72.601 vs 115.443 tok/s (1.590x, revision 1 read 1.604x).
- NOT A COMPARISON: the gate refused the pair, so none of these is a scored delta. They are the two records' own readings, printed because producing them was the task and omitting them would hide a result rather than qualify it.

### 2113 — The Declared Cache Asymmetry Runs Backwards
- MEASURED from sealed `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` on every scenario of both runtimes; nothing was unknown or malformed. `llama-server` reuses KV **across scenarios**: its first `multiturn_prefix_reuse` turn already found 5,736 of 7,784 prompt tokens cached, warmed by `long_prompt_8k` before it, which is why its TTFT there is 0.726 s against 105.206 s. `stability_soak` reported 18 cached tokens on all 20 iterations.
- MEASURED: the incumbent's configured `--prompt-cache-size 1 --prompt-cache-bytes 8GB` did **not fire once** — `cached_tokens` 0 on all six scenarios and all 26 turns, corroborated by timing rather than trusted alone (347.591 s over three turns on one 7,784-token prefix is about three full prefills at its own 72.6 tok/s).
- CONSEQUENCE: `multiturn_prefix_reuse` and `stability_soak` are one-sided hits and would have been refused per scenario even had `contextPolicy` admitted the pair. `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml` still declares the asymmetry the other way round and should be corrected to what was measured.
- OPEN, WITH ITS PROVENANCE ATTACHED: llama.cpp's resident upper bound climbed +6,201,119,136 B across the soak while the baseline's fell by 509,935,592 B. **This is not a leak and not a memory regression, and it is not a gated number.** It is a two-point first-to-last delta off the `soakMemory` window — one of the four series that never faced the coverage gate (see the coverage entry above), 20 stamps at a ~10.9 s median cadence, 19 of 19 gaps outside both bounds. The whole climb sits in the Mach anonymous component (9,905,647,432 to 16,106,766,568 B) while the mapped component is constant at 28,239,409,972 B. Cite it only as an open observation carrying that provenance, or not at all.

### 1746 — A Coverage Claim Must Be Sized To Its Reader
- ROOT CAUSE: `maximumMappedFileSampleGapSeconds` asserted 125 ms over a component read only by a `/usr/bin/vmmap -summary` fork. That fork costs 0.608-0.672 s against a ~3 MB target and 0.783-0.850 s against a ~0.9 GiB target on this host (8 consecutive production-shaped calls each), so two mapped observations can never be 125 ms apart. The memory dimension had an **empty admissible set**: every window was `partial`, `validatedScoredBytes` was nil, every per-scenario and process-wide memory delta became an `unmeasured` blocker, and `accepted = blockers.isEmpty` could never be true. Review drove windows from 0.05 s to 7.0 s and every one returned `status=partial scored=nil`.
- FIX: The mapped bound is now derived rather than asserted — `samplingIntervalSeconds + 2 x observedMappedFileReadCostSeconds` = 7.0 s, one cadence plus the reader cost plus one reader cost of scheduling headroom. The Mach component keeps its independent 20 Hz series and its own 125 ms bound, which is the part of revision 3 worth keeping. What the wider bound costs is stated rather than implied: every emitted `RuntimeMemoryPeak` carries `mappedFileObservationLimitSeconds` and a `mappedFileObservabilityNote` saying mapped-file transients shorter than that cadence are not observable, and `validatedScoredBytes` returns nil for a `measured` peak that does not carry them. The mapped component that matters here is the model weights, resident for the whole run; a 125 ms claim over them bought nothing real.
- PRODUCTION EVIDENCE: Four probes at the production sampler class. `benchmark-memory-sampler-probe` now requires the window to **score** (137,348,696 B) while catching the 150 ms anonymous transient, instead of requiring a refusal as revision 3 did. `benchmark-mapped-file-sampler-probe` requires a sustained 256 MiB file-backed region to reach the score: scored mapped component 268,645,172 B, equal to the direct reading. `benchmark-memory-coverage-refusal-probe` is the executed narrowing mutant — the same class on the same workload with the bound narrowed to 125 ms refuses with `resident-mapped-file-sampling-gap` while the unnarrowed control scores. The override can only narrow; `BenchmarkFootprintSampler` clamps it to the contract bound, so it is not a path to a laxer gate.
- SCOPE: Revision 4 instrumentation only. The hour-scale pinned pair was again deliberately not run; revision-1 decode, TTFT and 75k memory numbers remain historical.

### 1746 — Stopping The Fast Sampler First Cost Every Process-Wide Memory Number
- ROOT CAUSE: `BenchmarkFootprintSampler.stop()` set one flag for both loops. The 20 Hz Mach loop checked it at the top of its iteration and exited immediately, while the 5 s `vmmap` loop finished an in-flight read that landed with a Mach timestamp one reader-cost later. Every process-wide series therefore ended with a hole exactly one `vmmap` cost wide. `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift:674-675` stops the sampler and then takes `processPeakSoFar()`, so that hole went straight into the whole-process memory delta.
- EVIDENCE: Found by tightening the smoke's memory assertions from "scored or refused" to "scored", not by reading the code. In that run **20 of 20** sessions had a `partial` process peak on the larger runtime, with tail gaps of 0.358-0.494 s against a 125 ms bound; the small fake candidate was unaffected because `vmmap` on it costs under 125 ms. The mechanism is teardown ordering, not host load — a 20 Hz `proc_pid_rusage` loop against a target being walked by `vmmap` showed a 0.060-0.063 s maximum gap over 6 runs, no blackout.
- FIX: `stop()` retires the slow loop first, waits for its in-flight read to land with the fast loop still covering that interval, and only then retires the fast loop. This costs nothing extra; the old code already waited for the same thread.
- REGRESSION: `benchmark-memory-stop-coverage-probe` stops the sampler with a read deliberately in flight and requires the finalised process peak to be scoreable. It also requires the race to have actually happened, so a mistimed attempt fails rather than passing vacuously. With the simultaneous-stop mutant restored it exits 1 with `status=partial issues=[mach-physical-footprint-sampling-gap]`; with the fix it exits 0 and scores.

### 1746 — A Green Suite That Never Accepts Cannot Report That Acceptance Broke
- REGRESSION: In revision 3's own green run every admitted-path check exited 3 while the suite reported 118 PASS / 0 FAIL. `assert_peak_or_coverage_refusal` and an exit-0-or-3 control absorbed a total loss of the memory dimension, so "refused correctly" and "never scores" were indistinguishable. The comment claiming the wide-band thresholds existed so an honestly measured pair reaches `accepted=true` was true of no check in the file.
- FIX: The control now requires exit 0, `accepted=true`, no blockers, and a scored memory delta with numeric baseline and candidate on both the process and at least one scenario. The mmap-memory assertions dropped the refusal tolerance entirely and now require `measured` peaks and scored deltas. The refusal branch keeps its own separate fixture in `benchmark-memory-coverage-refusal-probe` rather than living as a tolerance inside the positive checks.
- EVIDENCE: 119 PASS / 1 FAIL, and every admitted-path check is back at **exit 0**: `a measured pair reaches an accepted decision (exit 0)`, `the accepted control scores resident memory on every delta it reports`, and the four memory probes. The single FAIL was the new series-coverage assertion applied to `warmupMemory`, which is one synchronous point reading rather than a sampled window and is read by no decision; the assertion was scoped to series and re-verified by replaying the exact block against that run's artifacts (exit 0). No production source changed after that run.

### 1432 — Cache Reuse Comparability Must Execute At The Decision Entry
- ROOT CAUSE: Revision 2 documented the cache policies and declared one-sided reuse non-comparable, but no structured reuse fact reached `RuntimeBenchmark.decide`; identical prompt-token counts therefore remained scoreable even when only one runtime reused KV state.
- FIX: Every scenario now seals `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` as a validated `hit`, `miss`, or `unknown` observation. `RuntimeBenchmark.decide` refuses one-sided hits, unknown or malformed observations, and applicability mismatches before scoring TTFT, prefill, decode, wall time, or memory; symmetric reported hits and misses remain comparable.
- PRODUCTION EVIDENCE: `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` drives identical prompt-token counts through `benchmark-run` with a baseline miss and candidate hit, requires exit 3, an explicit `short_prompt/cache_reuse is one-sided` blocker, and `unmeasured`/`non-comparable` timing deltas. The symmetric no-hit control remains scoreable. The transcript digest includes the structured reuse fact, so post-observation edits invalidate the seal.
- SCOPE: Cache comparability only. The bounded-KV production merge and all its decoy, missing, malformed, rewritten, and repeated-argv refusals remain untouched.

### 1432 — Mapped-File Freshness Is Independent Evidence
- ROOT CAUSE: Revision 2 copied one `vmmap` mapped-file value into 20 Hz Mach samples and timestamped each composite at the fresh Mach time. Coverage inspected only that composite timestamp, so a stale mmap component became fresh evidence and a short file-backed peak could be missed while the window remained `measured`.
- FIX: Raw memory samples now carry independent Mach and mapped-file timestamps. Reused mapped values retain their original observation time; `samplingCoverageIssue` evaluates the two series independently, and production appends an explicit failure so insufficient, stale, or gapped mapped coverage is `partial` with no score.
- PRODUCTION EVIDENCE: The separate file-backed fixture mapped and touched 256 MiB for less than the refresh interval. The direct reader saw 268,645,172 B while the periodic peak retained 82,944 B, but the real `benchmark-mapped-file-sampler-probe` exited 0 because the window refused scoring with `resident-mapped-file-sampling-gap`. The anonymous 128 MiB fixture separately exited 0 after proving the Mach series caught its transient and only the independently stale mapped series caused refusal.
- SCOPE: Revision 3 instrumentation only. The hour-scale pinned comparison was deliberately not rerun; revision-1 decode, TTFT, and 75k memory numbers remain historical and cannot support the decision.

### 1254 — Benchmark Refusal Must Reap Its Owned Launcher
- ROOT CAUSE: `BenchmarkRunCommand.drive` terminated `model-harness` only on selected return paths. A provenance refusal thrown after the runtime became ready bypassed those calls and left the task-owned launcher and synthetic runtime reparented to PID 1.
- FIX: `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift:510` installs an unconditional scope-exit termination immediately after a successful spawn; the existing explicit shutdown remains idempotent. The production decoy-provenance attack in `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` now also fails if any process naming its exact task-scoped config survives the exit-5 refusal.
- EVIDENCE: The full smoke reproduced the leak after the expected provenance refusal; a raw process snapshot was captured before only the exact task-owned process group was terminated. ### 0551 — Observed Argv Uses The Observed Runtime's Parser Semantics
- ROOT CAUSE: `RuntimeBenchmark.contextPolicy` scanned kernel argv with first-occurrence precedence, while `mlx_lm.server` uses Python argparse store actions and therefore applies the last repeated value; `2048, 999` was recorded as `2048` and admitted.
- FIX: `RuntimeBenchmark.swift:701` owns one runtime parser registry: `python-mlx-lm` accepts argparse long-option spelling and last-wins repetition; `mlx-swift` accepts spaced unique flags and treats duplicates as unresolved; unknown runtimes and conflicting aliases refuse.
- EVIDENCE: The exact unit witness was red before the fix. Unit narrowing covers different/equal Python repetitions, repeated reasoning JSON, Swift duplicate refusal, reasoning-alias conflict, repeated context bounds, and unknown runtimes; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` drives the real `benchmark-run` call site with baseline argv `--prefill-step-size 2048 --prefill-step-size 999` and requires exit 4 with effective `999` in the refusal.

### 0456 — Missing Live KV Evidence No Longer Falls Back To Argv
- ROOT CAUSE: `RuntimeBenchmark.contextPolicy` treated an answered `/v1/models` response without `meta.n_ctx` as permission to reuse caller-requested `--max-kv-size`; production `benchmark-run` therefore accepted a pair whose attestations explicitly said `notReported`.

Prefill and reasoning come from `ProcessObservation.arguments`, so the record carries kernel argv rather than caller-supplied profile argv.

Full Swift tests (290/24), strict format, macOS arm64 Release build, and smoke (0 failures) exit 0.

### 0354 — Benchmark Revision Bound To The Observed Python Process
- ROOT CAUSE: `benchmark-run` derived `mlx-lm` provenance from caller-supplied `--python-bin`, so a decoy server could be measured while an unrelated immutable environment supplied the accepted revision.
- FIX: The production run now observes the serving process, requires the configured package-owned `mlx_lm.server` entry point in Darwin's exact shebang script slot (`argv[1]`), verifies the installed distribution's RECORD and immutable `direct_url.json`, and treats `--python-bin` only as an equality assertion; the candidate and model-harness pins now come from their observed executables as well.

Live `/v1/models` metadata supplies `kv=76800`; a failed or malformed read produces `kv=unread` and refuses.
- SCOPE: The benchmark-only profiles use fork commit `ec9eea0af1d741cd4eb21c8766478a3e79dd44d6` and a 76,800-token KV bound. The deployed default profile is unchanged.
- AUDIT: Thresholds, prompts, and profile declarations are explicit policy/workload inputs rather than runtime-identity claims. Served model and context remain live runtime reports under the documented non-malicious-runtime trust boundary; no other acceptance fact is pinned from a caller-selected executable.

## 2026-08-29

### 2318 — Status Publication Revalidates Its Resource Decision
- ROOT CAUSE: `handleConnection` observed resources and snapshotted broker state in separate critical sections, so a newer acquire could latch pressure between them while the wire response still published `healthy/admitted`.
- FIX: `tools/agents-infra/internal/infra/pi_shared_broker_darwin.go` revalidates status/admission generations and snapshots latch, broker state, and leases under one lock; superseded status publishes explicit `unknown/refused`.
- EVIDENCE: Real status/acquire handlers reproduce the observe-to-publish race before the fix; disabling final revalidation makes `TestSharedBrokerProductionPressureCannotSupersedeStatusBeforePublication` exit 1.
- STATUS: Current latch and draining precedence apply only to a current status decision; stale provider facts are never laundered into availability.

### 2317 — Diagnostic Polls No Longer Own Admission Freshness
- ROOT CAUSE: Healthy status polls incremented the same generation used at lease reservation, allowing repeated observability to supersede every healthy acquire.
- DECISION: Admission and diagnostic observations use separate generations; only admission starts and direct pressure evidence advance admission invalidation.
- EVIDENCE: Four real healthy status requests cannot starve a paused healthy acquire, while a real pressured status invalidates it before reservation; re-coupling status to admission generation makes the starvation test exit 1.
- STATUS: Existing leases and the single broker-owned runtime remain unchanged across both schedules.

### 2312 — Retention Architecture Needs Bounded Liveness And Upgrade Recovery

- FINDING: preserving every legacy lifecycle log while forcing `within_policy=false` and `soak_ready=false` gives upgraded profiles no path back to health.
- REQUIRED: pin per-operation lock/scan budgets with fail-closed status, and add an explicit audited, resumable operator retirement or archive flow that never adopts ambiguous legacy evidence.
- EVIDENCE: `retention-architecture-revision-required.md` is attached to the Task; corrected architecture run `RUN-260829-c75634` is active on exact base `6d051f54`.

- DECISION: `tools/agents-infra/internal/infra/pi_shared_supervision.go` owns one runtime-key-scoped persisted ledger; every shared profile supplies restart limit, initial/max backoff, stable-run, and quarantine durations explicitly.
- FIX: `RunSharedRuntimeBroker` persists readiness/failure facts, `acquireSharedRuntimeLease` automatically retries only ledger-proven runtime failures, and status/manual quarantine use the same resolved path contract.
- EVIDENCE: Real `handleConnection` and `runSharedPiSession` abrupt-death fixtures release leases; focused race, full infra/root packages, vet, Darwin/Linux/Windows builds, formatting, and diff checks exit 0. Narrowed backoff and active-lock bypass mutants each exit 1 on their named negative test.
- STATUS: `TASK-260829-2t5xmi` implementation ready for review.

### 1610 — One Conservative Resident Bound Restores The Memory Decision Without Reviving Mach-Only Bias
- REVIEW FINDING: refusing `ri_phys_footprint` for llama.cpp was correct but left the owner's first-class memory criterion wholly undecidable. The existing 1.41 GiB Mach / 26.6 GiB resident-mapped observation showed a platform measurement existed; the quantity merely needed an honest name and symmetric collection.
- DECISION: both runtimes are scored on `peak_resident_memory_upper_bound_bytes`, defined as exact Mach physical footprint plus the upper edge of `vmmap -summary`'s rounded resident `mapped file` bucket. The record retains the raw Mach reading, literal vmmap token, mapped-file upper component, sample counts, status, and `scoreSemantics=conservative-upper-bound` over warm-up, scenario, soak, and process-peak windows.
- FAIL-CLOSED CONTRACT: complete output with no mapped-file row is measured zero. OS/tool read failure, incomplete output, malformed row, and any partial window remain distinct; none has a scored value. The legacy `peakPhysicalFootprintBytes` stays raw diagnostic evidence and is never a decision metric.
- DIRECTION: Mach-only scoring strongly favoured llama.cpp. The replacement can double-count on a runtime whose Mach footprint already charges mapped pages, so its residual direction is runtime-dependent rather than claimed neutral. MTP-off now travels in every generated record and decision with its explicit direction **against llama.cpp**.
- PRODUCTION EVIDENCE: `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` drives anonymous and mmap-backed fixture runtimes through `benchmark-run`, requires the same accounting name on both, a positive mapped component on the mmap process, scored warm-up/scenario/soak/process windows, and no fallback to the old metric. The first rework smoke correctly failed because the parser rejected vmmap's two-line header; that read defect was fixed rather than treated as absence.
- VALIDATION: final Release production smoke exit 0 with 0 failures; `swift test -c release` exit 0 with 400 tests / 32 suites; Xcode macOS arm64 Release build exit 0; strict swift-format, shellcheck warning-level, and `git diff --check` exit 0. Earlier red smoke and lint attempts remain itemized in the task outcome rather than rewritten as passes.
- SCOPE: `RuntimeMemoryAccounting.swift`, `BenchmarkFootprintSampler.swift`, `BenchmarkPass.swift`, `BenchmarkScenarios.swift`, `RuntimeBenchmark.swift`, `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift`, `BenchmarkRunPins.swift`, contract tests, production smoke, and benchmark documentation.

### 1545 — Baseline Ran Outside Story Worktree
- ANOMALY: The contended baseline commands used the primary checkout `tools/agents-infra` directory instead of the assigned Story worktree; their exit status does not describe candidate `ee8ae3c` and must not be cited as task validation.
- DECISION: Every subsequent Go command uses the absolute Story worktree module path and records its real exit code.

### 1540 — Shared-Runtime Baseline Was Contended
- ANOMALY: Two uncached `go test ./internal/infra -count=1` processes overlapped after the first 30-second yield lost its session handle; the observed 262.406s exit 1 contained three readiness/fan-in fixture failures and is not valid single-run baseline evidence.
- DECISION: Run one directly tracked test process at a time and use focused `-run` masks before the bounded package split.
- STATUS: No overlapping `go test ./internal/infra` process remained after the failed run.

### 1443 — Cross-Runtime Clocks Need One Generated-Event Definition
- ROOT CAUSE: `BenchmarkHTTPDriver.stream` recognized `content` and `reasoning` but not llama.cpp's `reasoning_content`, then ended decode on the response tail rather than the last generated event. Field naming moved only llama.cpp's start boundary; post-token framing moved both end boundaries by runtime-specific transport work.
- FIX: `RuntimeStreamDelta.read` owns the event definition across all three fields; production records TTFT at the first and decode through the last such event. `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` drives `reasoning` versus `reasoning_content` through `benchmark-run` and requires both to remain scored.
- DIRECTION: the omitted llama.cpp field inflated TTFT, understated prefill, and overstated decode — mixed, not uniformly pro-llama.cpp. Response-tail time understated decode for whichever runtime delayed usage or `[DONE]`; direction not established.
- SCOPE: `RuntimeStreamDelta.swift`, `BenchmarkHTTPDriver.swift`, `BenchmarkScenarios.swift`, `RuntimeStreamDeltaTests.swift`, `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh`.

### 1443 — Mach Footprint Cannot Score mmap-Loaded GGUF Weights
- ROOT CAUSE: `ri_phys_footprint` excludes llama.cpp's clean resident file-backed GGUF pages; the observed 1.41 GiB footprint versus 28.09 GiB RSS and 26.6 GiB clean mapped-file residency was a known underestimate, not model memory.
- DECISION: do not substitute unstable RSS. Exact `llama-server` executables and `.gguf` artifacts use `unmeasurable-mmap-loaded-weights`; every process/scenario/soak/warm-up footprint is `nil`, the record states that the old reading favours that runtime, and decision scoring fails closed.
- FIX: `RuntimeMemoryAccounting` classifies the reviewed executable/artifact; `BenchmarkFootprintSampler`, `BenchmarkPass`, and `BenchmarkRunCommand` keep every production memory entry on that source. Production-entry smoke requires the llama-shaped pair to exit rejected on unmeasured footprint rather than accepted on the old reading.
- SCOPE: `RuntimeMemoryAccounting.swift`, `BenchmarkFootprintSampler.swift`, `BenchmarkPass.swift`, `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift`, `RuntimeStreamDeltaTests.swift`, `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh`.

### 1402 — Decode Claim Withdrawn; Authoritative Streamed Usage Reverses The Single-Probe Delta

- REGRESSION: TASK-260828-2wcrph's first `probe-ttft.py` outputs carried `"usage": {}`. Its published paired decode values could only be reconstructed by substituting non-empty SSE frame counts for completion tokens; the claimed slower direction had no token-count evidence and is withdrawn from the report and README.
- EVIDENCE: rerun requested `stream_options.include_usage=true` and retained every timestamped SSE packet. Both runtimes returned exactly 7,784 prompt / 106 completion / 7,890 total tokens. Python: TTFT 103.310s, prefill 75.346 tok/s, decode 8.449 tok/s. llama.cpp: TTFT 76.784s, prefill 101.375 tok/s, decode 8.772 tok/s. The 1.038x decode delta is one bounded observation, not a general direction; frame counts are diagnostics only.
- DECISION: all gate-recorded llama.cpp TTFT/prefill/decode remains excluded from the migration decision. `reasoning_content` omission has mixed direction: llama.cpp TTFT inflated, prefill understated, decode overstated. TASK-260829-3cwcb6 owns the production fix and full rerun.
- SCOPE: `.research/260829_llamacpp-against-the-python-baseline.md`, `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype/README.md`, `.temp/TASK-260828-2wcrph-rework/` raw evidence and negative probe tests.

### 0310 — F6: The Same Rule One Level Deeper, And The Real Failure Was Three Rounds Of Overclaiming

- ROOT CAUSE, the sixth time: **a value this program could not understand was defaulted instead of refused** — now inside the tool declaration. Review misspelled `parameters.required` as `parameters.require`. The gate read that as the *supported absence* of `required`, `requiredArguments` came out `[]`, the parity check in `BenchmarkScenarios.tool` had no argument key to demand back, a runtime that called the tool with `{}` satisfied it, and the shipped `benchmark-run` exited **0** with `accepted: true`.
- INSIGHT, and it is the one worth keeping: **the recurring failure is no longer the defect class, it is the claim discipline.** Three revisions in a row asserted an audit or invariant broader than what was built — rev3 "every reading walked" (it walked runtime readings, not input files), rev4 "unknown keys refused at every level" (two levels unchecked), and each time a reviewer found the gap in one attempt. A false completeness claim is worse than the gap it hides, because it stops the next reader looking. State the invariant implemented, name the boundary where it stops, list what is unvalidated by design.
- FIX, deliberately narrow: `function.parameters.required` is **required** for a benchmark tool scenario and an explicit `[]` means "no mandatory arguments". `function.parameters` is required and must be an object; `type` must be `"function"`. `tool.parameters.required` is out of `supportedAbsences`, which now holds **five** entries. Only the tool fields the benchmark itself reads are validated — `validatedToolFields` — and everything below is forwarded to the runtime **verbatim and unread**, named in a new `unvalidatedByDesign`, both pinned by tests.
- DECISION, and it is the one the brief demanded: **no allowlist over arbitrary JSON Schema.** The parameter block is an opaque document this gate forwards and has no business grading. The residual is stated rather than hidden — a misspelling inside `parameters` that is not one of the five named fields still reaches the runtime unremarked. `required` is the only key in there the gate itself reads, which is why it is the only one made mandatory.
- REGRESSION: 3 mutants, **all killed, zero survivors**. **M-F6-1** restores the optional `required` in one line and the misspelled suite through the shipped `benchmark-run` is **measured and accepted, exit 0, `"accepted" : true`**, with `tool_call` recorded `succeeded: true` in both records — the finding reproduced at the production entry. **M-F6-2** (an explicit `required: []` becomes a fault) refuses the empty-demand pass at the production entry, exit 5. **M-F6-3** is the completeness promise rev4 claimed — a full allowlist over the declaration and function objects — and it **refuses the smoke's own control suite, exit 5**, because its tool carries a `description`; that is what the unkeepable promise costs. M11 and M12 re-run: exit 1 with 18 and 19 issues, both killed; counts not comparable to rev4's 18/16 because both were re-authored from their descriptions and the suite grew by six tests. Revisions 3 and 4's bound mutants were also re-run against THIS source rather than cited: **A8** (narrow `unpinnableConditions` by `kv=unread`) 4 red, **A9** (the relaxation this task was told not to make) 3 red, **M-F5-2** 3 red, **M-F5-3** 10 red, and **M-F5-1** reproduces its finding at the production entry again — the string `"2027"` suite **measured and accepted, exit 0**, 15 contract tests red.
- ANOMALY, pre-existing and now with a frequency: the exec-stub `ProcessObservation` flake recorded in the 0130 entry bit **2 of 6** `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` runs in this session, always as `the attestation for "gate-smoke-candidate" was opened and never closed` on the control pass, cascading into ~15 downstream FAILs. It **fails closed**, it is unrelated to this delta — nothing here touches observation — and it clears on retry. It is still undecided and it will bite the real 28 GB run, where a retry costs an hour rather than two minutes.
- SCOPE: `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` — `PromptSuiteSchema.swift`, `PromptSuiteSchemaTests.swift`, `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` (new section 6), `README.md`. Report `.research/260828_llamacpp-in-the-benchmark-gate.md` revision 5: new §0F6 and §5.3, plus two corrections written into revision 4's own tables. `unpinnableConditions` untouched.
- EVIDENCE: `swift build --build-tests` exit 0; `swift test` **385 tests / 30 suites** exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` against the Release product **95 checks, 0 failures** exit 0; `swift build -c release` exit 0; `xcrun swift-format lint --strict --recursive Sources Tests` exit 0; `shellcheck scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0. Each run directly as its own process. The 28 GB model was never loaded and revision 3's real 29 GB cross-format run was not repeated; the part of it this delta could have broken — the shipped suite's `tool_call` declaration passing validation — is asserted at the production entry instead.
- STATUS: `TASK-260828-3fgca3` handed back to review.

### 0200 — llama.cpp Measured: Refused On The KV Pin, And Two Of The Three Speed Metrics Were Not Measuring The Same Thing

- RESULT: the pinned six-scenario suite ran **three times end to end**, `llama-server` b10621-c1d0e7a00 against `mlx_lm-relux.server`, same host, sequential, `(none)` resident before and after all three. **Every scenario succeeded on both runtimes, including `context_75k` at 73,016 prompt tokens, and prompt-token parity was exact (1.0000) on all six.** Tool-call parity and the 20-iteration soak passed on both. All three invocations exited **4**, unscored.
- REFUSAL, and it is correct: `contextPolicy` differs, `kv=unbounded` against `kv=8192`/`kv=76800`. **`mlx_lm-relux.server` has no flag that bounds its KV cache and reports no bound; `llama-server` has no unbounded mode.** No argv on either side can make them agree, so **llama.cpp can never be scored against this Python incumbent** — the only admissible baseline is the Swift prototype, which has `--max-kv-size`. TASK-260828-2jbufw had already measured "KV bound: no flag; unbounded" for `mlx_lm.server`; nobody had followed it to the conclusion, and `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml` still told the operator to "match it to the baseline's `--max-kv-size`". Corrected in place.
- FINDING 1, the dangerous one, **reported not fixed**: `timeToFirstTokenSeconds`, `prefillTokensPerSecond` and `decodeTokensPerSecond` are **not comparable across this pair**. All three derive from the first frame carrying `delta.content` or `delta.reasoning`; `mlx_lm.server` publishes `reasoning`, **`llama-server` publishes `reasoning_content`**. llama.cpp's clock therefore starts after its think block, inflating TTFT, understating prefill and overstating decode. The original corrected decode figures and direction were unsupported; the 1402 correction withdraws them and records the authoritative streamed-usage rerun. The production gate remains unchanged here because TASK-260829-3cwcb6 owns its fix and full rerun.
- FINDING 2, **fixed and mutant-proved**: `RuntimeSpeculation.read` did `(slot["params"] as? [String: Any]) ?? slot` — consult `params`, fall back to the slot only when `params` is *absent*. On this build `speculative` is a **top-level** slot field and `params` appears only *after* a slot has served a request, carrying sampling settings that never name it. So the reader went blind as soon as traffic touched every slot: the 20-iteration soak did exactly that, and a llama.cpp pass provably not speculating recorded `speculation=unread` and was refused. Worse, `{"speculative": true, "params": {"speculative": false}}` read as `reported(false)` — **a drafting runtime scored as MTP-off.** Fix reads both placements, neither shadowing the other, any `true` still refusing. Proved at the production entry: the two post-fix runs both record `speculation=off`.
- FINDING 3, memory, and it is the owner's first-class question: **`ri_phys_footprint` cannot see `mmap`-loaded weights.** One process, one moment, holding the 27B model: footprint **1.41 GiB**, `ps` RSS **28.09 GiB**, `vmmap` mapped-file resident **26.6 GiB with 0 dirty**. `maxPeakFootprintRatio` would have scored llama.cpp at ~0.08x and called it twelve times more frugal. The 26.6 GiB checks out to 0.01 GiB as the whole GGUF **minus** the 451 MB `blk.64` MTP head llama.cpp logs as ignored — the first runtime confirmation that declared non-equivalence #1 is real in memory. Bounded honestly (`footprint + 26.6 GiB`), llama.cpp is **provably lower at `context_75k` — 41.05 GiB vs 45.20 GiB, ~9% — and at 8k with a matched arena, indeterminate at small working sets.** Modestly frugal, not an order of magnitude.
- MEASURED, and it makes `--ctx-size` a resident cost rather than a limit: the KV arena is **65,536 B/token** (16 full-attention layers of 64, 4 KV heads, head_dim 256, K+V, f16). Predicted 8192→76800 delta 4.19 GiB; **observed 4.19 GiB** on `short_prompt`. Paid in full on a 41-token prompt.
- ANOMALY: the baseline declaration "`llama-server` holds no cross-request prompt cache" is **backwards**. `multiturn_prefix_reuse` cost Python ~three full prefills (235.52s / 282.74s) and llama.cpp one or fewer (84.47s / **36.68s**, below a single prefill) — so the incumbent's configured `--prompt-cache-size 1 --prompt-cache-bytes 8GB` did **not** fire, llama.cpp's slot KV reuse did, and it survives across scenarios. That figure is contaminated by scenario order and should not be read as a clean 0.13x. Separately, llama.cpp's anonymous footprint climbed 1.86→11.43 GiB across one pass; not diagnosed.
- PROCESS FAILURE, mine, recorded because it bears on evidence: six side probes outlived the shells that launched them — job control does not survive a tool-call boundary — including two 29 GB `llama-server` instances, which pushed the host out of memory and produced three `{"code":500,"message":"Compute error."}` probe responses that are evidence of nothing. Found by `ps`, killed, host verified clean **before** the runs whose numbers are reported. Kill background probes by PID in the same call that starts them.
- REGRESSION: 3 mutants, all killed. **M-2wcrph-1** restores the pre-fix `params ?? slot` reader — exit 1, 3 tests red including the drafting-scored-as-quiet case. **M-2wcrph-2** (*narrowing*: a slot naming the field nowhere reads as "not speculating") — exit 1, 6 issues. **M-2wcrph-3** (*widening*: `contextPolicy` dropped from `Pins.firstMismatch`, the change that would let this pair be scored) — exit 1, the new refusal test reddens.
- SCOPE: `RuntimeAttestation.swift` (`RuntimeSpeculation.read`), `RuntimeObservationReadingTests.swift` (+5 tests), `RuntimeBenchmarkContextBoundTests.swift` (`boundedMLXArgv` → `boundedSwiftArgv`, which described an `mlx_lm.server` launch carrying a flag it does not have, +2 tests), `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml`, `README.md`. Report `.research/260829_llamacpp-against-the-python-baseline.md`. `unpinnableConditions` untouched.
- EVIDENCE: `swift build` exit 0; `swift test` **392 tests / 30 suites** exit 0; `xcodebuild -configuration Release` exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` **0 failures** exit 0; `swift format lint --strict --recursive Sources Tests` exit 0; `shellcheck scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` exit 0. Three real `benchmark-run` invocations against the 28 GB MLX directory and the 29 GB GGUF, all exit 4. Sessions, logs and probes under `.temp/TASK-260828-2wcrph/`.
- STATUS: `TASK-260828-2wcrph` handed to review.

### 0135 — F5: The Gate Sealed And Scored The Wrong Request, Because The Suite Was An Input Nobody Validated
- ROOT CAUSE, the same sentence a fifth time, one layer earlier than the last three: **a value this program could not understand was defaulted instead of refused.** A required `context_75k` scenario carrying `"prefix_repeats": "2027"` — the count as a JSON *string* — hit `spec["prefix_repeats"] as? Int`, which is `nil` for an `NSString`, so the no-prefix branch ran, the 16,232-token prefix was never built, a **15-token** prompt was measured, and the shipped `benchmark-run` exited **0** with `accepted: true`.
- INSIGHT, and it is the one that generalises: **transcript sealing is orthogonal to this class.** Both passes launched honestly, both processes were observed from the kernel, both records sealed over transcripts that recorded their requests *faithfully* — the requests were simply the wrong ones. The capacity scenario, the one that decides whether a runtime can serve the context the whole evaluation exists to measure, was hollow while the decision read as earned. No amount of provenance downstream can notice that the thing being measured is not the thing that was asked for.
- FIX, structural rather than a check: `PromptSuiteSchema` in the contract library decodes the suite with `JSONDecoder` into `JSONValue` — which separates `.string("2027")` from `.int(2027)` from `.bool(true)` from `.double(20.5)` exactly, where `JSONSerialization` bridges booleans *and* numbers to `NSNumber` — validates the complete document before either runtime is launched, and hands the drivers typed `Scenario` values. **There is no `as?` cast left in `BenchmarkScenarios.swift`**, so there is no bypass mutant to write for the call site: the drivers cannot be handed an unvalidated suite because the type they need is the type validation produces. `BenchmarkScenarios.run` also stopped being optional-returning; the old signature returned `nil` for an unrecognised `kind` and the loop skipped it, so a misspelled kind silently removed a required scenario from a pass.
- CORRECTION, added 2026-08-29 by the revision-5 rework: the AUDIT bullet below says *"All refuse now"* and the report's table said *"any unknown key, at any level — refused"*. **Both claims were wider than the code.** Unknown keys were refused at the document object and at each scenario object; the tool declaration and its JSON-Schema parameter block went unchecked. F6 is exactly that gap: a misspelled `parameters.require` read as the supported absence of `required`, and `accepted: true` out of the shipped binary. `tool.parameters.required` was not a safe supported absence and is gone. See the F6 entry above.
- AUDIT, over the surface revision 3's audit missed, with every default named: `prompt` `?? ""`; `turns` `?? []` and `iterations` `?? 0`, each of which drove **zero requests and then reported the scenario as succeeded**; `max_tokens` `?? 256`, so a suite asking for 16 got 256; `prompt_template` `?? ""`; `parameters.required` `?? []`, a parity check that demands nothing back; `filler_paragraph` present-and-empty, which multiplies to nothing and hollows out every prefix count with no malformed field anywhere; and unknown keys ignored, so `prefix_repeat` for `prefix_repeats` is the same 15-token prompt reached by a typo. All refuse now. The six intentionally supported absences are listed in `PromptSuiteSchema.supportedAbsences` and asserted whole by a test, the treatment `unpinnableConditions` gets.
- CORRECTION, and it matters more than the fix: **revision 3's logbook entry and report claimed an audit that did not hold.** "Every reading in the invocation was walked" was true only of readings against a *runtime*. Both the 0130 entry above and `.research/260828_llamacpp-in-the-benchmark-gate.md` §0R.4 now carry that correction in place.
- DECISION, deliberate: `--skip` on a required scenario is still **not** refused before launch, though it would be cheap. `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` uses `--skip tool_call` as the only case that drives admission's `requiredScenarios` call site; a pre-launch refusal would delete that evidence. The pair is still refused one layer later, exit 4. `unpinnableConditions` was not touched.
- DECISION: `version` and `comment` are recognised as **keys** and their values are deliberately not typed. Nothing is read out of them — the shipped suite writes `"version": 1` as a number — and a rule that cannot change what the gate measures only makes it look more careful than it is. Key recognition is what catches a misspelled field name, which is the real hazard.
- REGRESSION: 3 mutants, **all killed, zero survivors**. **M-F5-1** restores the collapse in one line and the shipped `benchmark-run` **measures the 15-token capacity scenario and accepts the pair, exit 0** — the finding reproduced at the production entry, with 15 contract tests red. **M-F5-2** and **M-F5-3** are the narrowing pair and each refuses a suite this repository ships: making `single.prefix_repeats` required refuses `short_prompt`, and dropping `version`/`comment` from the recognised keys refuses both the shipped and the smoke suites — control **exit 5** in each case, 3 and 9 tests red. **M11** and **M12**, the G4 narrowing pair, re-run against this source: **18 red** and **16 red**, unchanged.
- SCOPE: `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` — new `Sources/MLXSwiftRuntimeContract/PromptSuiteSchema.swift`; `BenchmarkScenarios.swift` (typed drivers, no casts); `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift` (the scenario loop); new `Tests/.../PromptSuiteSchemaTests.swift` (28 tests, mostly negatives); `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` section 5 (five malformed suites at the production entry, each asserted to exit nonzero, emit **no decision** and create **no session directory**, plus the shipped suite asserted to get past validation); `README.md`. Report `.research/260828_llamacpp-in-the-benchmark-gate.md` revision 4, §0 and §5.2.
- EVIDENCE: `swift build --build-tests` exit 0; `swift test` **379 tests / 30 suites** exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` against the Release product **84 checks, 0 failures** exit 0; `swift build -c release` exit 0; `xcrun swift-format lint --strict --recursive Sources Tests` exit 0; `shellcheck scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0. Each run directly as its own process. The 28 GB model was never loaded; revision 3's real 29 GB cross-format run is not repeated, and the part of it this delta could have broken — the shipped suite passing validation — is asserted at the production entry instead.
- STATUS: `TASK-260828-3fgca3` handed back to review.

### 0130 — Three Production-Entry Bypasses Were One Sentence: Something The Gate Could Not Establish, Spent As If It Had Been
- ROOT CAUSE, singular: **absence of evidence spent as evidence of the favourable case.** Review drove the shipped `benchmark-run` three times and got `exit 0, accepted=true` three times. F1: `--equivalence` read, hashed and observer-sealed a JSON path *the caller supplied*, so review minted a `comparable` verdict naming an arbitrary source of record, both artifact digests the gate had itself computed, and one generic note — and the three measured non-equivalences were dropped from a decision this gate produced. F2: a present-but-malformed `meta.n_ctx` collapsed into `.notReported`, which `contextPolicy` spends as permission to derive `unbounded`, so a runtime with a finite 32,768-token window that answered the *string* `"32768"` matched a genuinely unbounded baseline. F3: every non-200 from `/slots` became `.notReported` and the argv fallback then derived `off`, so a speculative fixture whose `/slots` answered **HTTP 500** was scored as MTP-off. This gate has now been rebuilt four times around that one sentence.
- INSIGHT, and it is the reusable one: **hashing and observer-sealing attacker-authored bytes authenticates nothing.** It proves only that the bytes did not change between the read and the seal. Every step of F1's read was individually correct; the construction was still worthless, because the caller decided what the evidence said.
- FIX (F1): admission is bound to a decision **the invocation cannot author for itself**. `TrustedEquivalenceDecisions.shipped` is a fixed list compiled into the gate from versioned repository source, holding one entry — TASK-260828-3g87i4's — with its document's SHA-256 `106edbf4…f09f962` and its three required non-equivalences stated in the store where a reviewer reads them, so no document may replace them with one note. The document is `equivalence/qwen3-8-27b-uncensored.equivalence.json`; both artifact digests in it were recomputed on this host the way the gate computes them (MLX directory `1b10f3fe…88460b`; GGUF `31756fca…24f8d6`, 95 s of streamed SHA-256 over 29,047,084,416 bytes). The caller still supplies the document — its contents must travel into both records — but not the decision that it counts. New reading `ModelEquivalenceReading.untrusted(path:digest:)`, refused before launch **and** again from the attestation, so absence, failed read and untrusted are three facts with three refusals.
