- DECISION, deliberate and costly: **the trust store holds no fixture entry.** An anchor written so the smoke could reach a cross-format *acceptance* would be a decision nobody took sitting in the production trust store — F1 with a test's name on it. So the smoke proves the refusals and proves the trusted path is live (the shipped document gets *past* the trust clause and is refused one clause further in), and the admitted class is proven by the contract suite against the unstubbed shipped store plus one run over the real 29 GB pair.
- FIX (F2/F3): both readings moved **out of the driver into the contract library** — `RuntimeContextWindow.read(fromModelsEntry:)` and `RuntimeSpeculation.read(slotsStatus:body:)` — because the executable target is not unit-testable and these are exactly the seams a smoke alone had to carry. `notReported` now means only *the runtime answered and named none*: no `meta`, or `meta` with no `n_ctx`; and for `/slots`, **only 404 and 501**, the two statuses that say the route is not served. Everything else is `unread` and refused. `as? Int` alone was not enough — `JSONSerialization` bridges JSON booleans to `NSNumber`, so `true` reads as 1 and `8192.5` truncates.
- CORRECTION, added 2026-08-29 by the revision-4 rework: the AUDIT bullet below claimed *"four more sites had the same shape"* after walking every reading. **That claim did not hold.** The audit walked the readings the gate performs against a *runtime* and never walked the readings it performs against its own *input files*; every reader of the prompt suite had exactly this defect, and one of them was reachable through the shipped entry for an `accepted=true`. See the F5 entry above.
- AUDIT, which the brief demanded and which paid: four more sites had the same shape. `declaredContextBound` read `--ctx-size abc` / `-c 0` / a trailing flag as "no bound was asked for", silencing the one clause that keeps a llama.cpp launch away from the argv fallback — F2 one level up, 15 tests red when reverted. `declaredSpeculation` did the same for an unreadable speculative flag. `hostIdentity()` joined `"unknown-model"` on a failed `sysctl`, and that pin is compared for **equality**, so two records from two different machines that both failed the read compare *equal*. `capture` merges stderr into its stdout pipe and ignored the exit status, so a failing `model-harness version` recorded its **error text** as the launcher revision. All four now refuse. `appendDelta` was already correct — an unmeasured metric emits `verdict: "unmeasured"` and blocks.
- ANOMALY, reported and not fixed, and it will bite the real 28 GB run: `ProcessObservation` compares the executable path at open and at close, and a launcher child that is an **exec stub** changes that path mid-life — `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3` runs `…/bin/python3.14`, which re-execs into `…/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python`. Whichever side of that exec the 200 ms child-resolution poll lands on decides, so on a loaded host a healthy pass is refused with "the attestation … was opened and never closed". Reproduced twice; the same pair in the other pass ordering is accepted. It **fails closed**, so it is not this class, and the repair is a real tradeoff: `p_starttime` alone is the pid-recycling defence, and dropping the path comparison would let a process exec into something the recorded `observedExecutableDigest` does not describe. Needs a decision before the migration measurement.
- REGRESSION: 10 mutants, **all killed, zero survivors**, and three of them are the acceptance questions answered at the production entry. Restore F1's read (`B3+A1`) and the shipped `benchmark-run` **accepts the caller's own verdict, exit 0**. Restore F2's (`B1+B1b`) and the 32,768-token window pins `unbounded` and is **accepted, exit 0**, with 23 contract tests red. Restore F3's (`B2`) and the HTTP-500 pair is **scored as MTP-off, exit 0**, with 20 red. Narrowing pair re-run against this source: `A2` (byte identity back on top of the evidence) 18 red including the positives; `A3` (any `/slots` answer read as speculating) 16 red. `A7` is the third narrowing mutant and the one that costs the incumbent — read every `/slots` answer as a failure and `mlx_lm.server`, which serves no such route, becomes inadmissible.
- EVIDENCE, the one that matters: the **real** cross-format pair driven through the shipped `benchmark-run` — the MLX 8-bit directory against the 29 GB Q8_0 GGUF, under the shipped decision — **exit 0, `accepted=true`**, `decision.json` carrying all three declared non-equivalences, both attestations reading the document at `106edbf4…`. No model was loaded: the runtimes are the smoke's stand-ins and the artifacts were read only to be digested.
- SCOPE: `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` — new `equivalence/qwen3-8-27b-uncensored.equivalence.json`, new `TrustedEquivalenceDecisions.swift`; `ModelEquivalence.swift`, `RuntimeAttestation.swift`, `RuntimeBenchmark.swift`, `BenchmarkRunPins.swift`, `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift`; new `RuntimeBenchmarkTrustedEquivalenceTests.swift` (11 tests, unstubbed store) and `RuntimeObservationReadingTests.swift` (16); `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` (three acceptance questions, speculation moved to a same-format pair, stand-in learned `--n-ctx-string` and `--slots-status`); `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml`; `README.md`. Report `.research/260828_llamacpp-in-the-benchmark-gate.md` revision 3, §0.
- EVIDENCE: `swift build --build-tests` 0; `swift test` **351/29** exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` against the Release product **68 checks, 0 failures** exit 0; `swift build -c release` 0; `xcrun swift-format lint --strict` exit 0; **`shellcheck` at its default level exit 0**, down from 16 findings (12 pre-existing, 4 added by revision 2) — F4; `git diff --check` 0.
- STATUS: `TASK-260828-3fgca3` handed back to review. llama.cpp is admissible as a benchmark candidate at the production entry, proven on the real artifacts; the real 28 GB comparison is still the story's measurement task, and the exec-stub anomaly above should be decided before it runs.

## 2026-08-28

### 2320 — The Model Pin Was Written About The File, Not The Model, So No Cross-Format Comparison Could Ever Be Admitted
- ROOT CAUSE: `Pins.modelPath` and `Pins.modelDigest` were compared for equality, and `BenchmarkRunPins.modelDigest` read `config.json` plus the safetensors index out of an MLX weight *directory*. `mlx_lm.server` loads a directory at `8bit/group64/affine`; `llama-server` loads a single `Q8_0` `.gguf`. Two files, two paths, two digests, one upstream model — so the gate demanded something that can never be true of any cross-format comparison and refused forever on a premise that does not fit the question. Not a bug in a derivation: a pin written about the local artifact when the question is about the model.
- DECISION (the story's, implemented here): **the pin identifies the shared source of record, and byte-identity of the local file is replaced by declared, digest-bound equivalence evidence — never by nothing.** Taken on TASK-260828-3g87i4's measurements: both schemes cost **8.5 bits/weight**, quantized-tensor sets match apart from the MTP block, mean relative RMS against the shared BF16 source is **0.766**, verdict COMPARABLE with three conditions.
- FIX: `Pins.modelOfRecord`, re-derived at admission from `RuntimeAttestation.observedModelEquivalence` — the document the *gate* wrote — so a record cannot claim two files are one model by writing a string. With no verdict it is `artifact:<digest>`, which pins byte identity exactly as before; with a verdict it is `source:<upstream>`, and `admitModelIdentity` then requires the **same** verdict on both sides *by the SHA-256 the gate computed over the document*, a `comparable` verdict, both artifacts named at the digests the gate computed for them, agreeing quantization labels, a non-empty set of declared non-equivalences, and **every one of them carried in both records**. `transcriptDigest` now covers `declaredAsymmetries`, so one cannot be deleted after the pass that produced it.
- INSIGHT, structural: there is deliberately **no "evidence absent" clause**. With no verdict both sides derive `artifact:<digest>`, the digests differ by construction, and the ordinary pin comparison refuses them — so absence refuses without anybody having to remember to call a check. A separate clause there could never fire, and a clause that cannot fail only makes a gate look more careful than it is. A **failed read** is a different fact and keeps its own refusal (`modelOfRecordUnread`).
- FIX: MTP refused rather than pinned. `llama-server` can draft off the model's MTP head and the MLX baseline structurally cannot, so a tokens/s measured with drafting on is a different algorithm. Requiring the two sides only to *match* would admit two speculating runtimes, so anything but `off` is refused.
- MEASUREMENT that changed the design: `/props` is the wrong endpoint. On build `b10621-c1d0e7a00` with the 0.5B Q8_0 fixture, `--spec-type ngram-mod` flips `GET /slots` → `[0].params.speculative` to **`true`** while `GET /props` → `default_generation_settings.params["speculative.types"]` keeps reporting **`"none"`**. `/props` reports the compiled default and does not move with the launch, so reading it would have reported a speculating server as quiet. Only `/slots` is read.
- FIX: the environment hole closed at the entry. `llama-server` reads `LLAMA_ARG_SPEC_TYPE`, and the gate's own environment is what it hands the launcher, so an inherited variable would put the runtime under test into drafting while every recorded argv showed nothing — the G2 defect one condition along. `benchmark-run` refuses to launch when its environment carries any `LLAMA_ARG_SPEC_*` / `LLAMA_ARG_DRAFT_*`, and refuses rather than scrubbing.
- REGRESSION: 16 mutants across the delta, **all killed, zero survivors**. **M13** is the sharpest: delete the `/slots` read from the driver and all **324 contract tests still pass** while the production entry returns **exit 0, `accepted=true`** for two runtimes that both reported they were speculating. **M10** and **M14** are the same seam — an unreadable verdict read as an absent one, and the declared non-equivalences no longer travelling — each invisible to the contract suite and each caught only by the shipped subcommand. **M11** and **M12** are the narrowing pair: restoring byte identity on top of the evidence reddens **22** tests including the cross-format acceptance, and reading every `reported` speculation state as `on` reddens **20** including the two positives, so the admitted class is pinned in both directions.
- SCOPE: `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` — new `ModelEquivalence.swift`; `RuntimeAttestation.swift` (`RuntimeSpeculation`, two new observed fields); `RuntimeBenchmark.swift` (`modelOfRecord`, `speculationPolicy`, `declaredSpeculation`, `admitModelIdentity`, eleven refusals); `BenchmarkRunPins.swift` (whole-file digest, artifact-shaped digest and quantization, verdict reader); `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift` (`--candidate-model`, `--equivalence`, per-pass `ModelPins`, the environment refusal, the `/slots` read); `BenchmarkPass.slots`; new `RuntimeBenchmarkModelOfRecordTests.swift` (22 tests, two positive); `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` (+13 G4 checks, stand-in learned `--slots`); `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml`; `README.md`. Report `.research/260828_llamacpp-in-the-benchmark-gate.md` revision 2.
- EVIDENCE: `swift build --build-tests` 0; `swift test` **324/27** exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` against the Release product **59 checks, 0 failures** exit 0; `xcodebuild` Release BUILD SUCCEEDED exit 0; `xcrun swift-format lint --strict` exit 0; `shellcheck -S warning` exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0. The 28 GB model was never loaded: the `/slots` probes used the 676 MB Qwen2.5-0.5B Q8_0 fixture on OS-assigned ephemeral ports, both probe servers were killed and verified gone, and the smoke's weight artifacts are a two-file fixture directory and a 50-byte file that exists only to be digested.
- STATUS: `TASK-260828-3fgca3` handed to review. llama.cpp is admissible as a benchmark candidate at the production entry; the real 28 GB comparison is not run, because it is an hour of exclusive host time deciding a migration rather than the admissibility question this task carries.

### 2145 — A Pin Derived From A Missing Flag Was False For A Runtime That Is Never Unbounded
- ROOT CAUSE: `RuntimeBenchmark.contextPolicy` derived the KV bound from argv and read the absence of `--max-kv-size` as `kv=unbounded`, justified by "absent on both sides means the same thing". True for the two MLX runtimes, false for `llama-server`, which has no unbounded mode. Measured on Homebrew `llama.cpp 0.3.0` build `b10621-c1d0e7a00`: `GET /v1/models` reports `meta.n_ctx` **32768** (the model's `n_ctx_train`) with no context flag and **8192** under `--ctx-size 8192`, corroborated by `/props default_generation_settings.n_ctx`. Because `Pins.firstMismatch` demands equality, a llama.cpp record would not merely have carried a false pin — it would have **matched** a genuinely unbounded MLX baseline.
- DECISION: the bound is read from the running process, not made runtime-aware. Runtime-awareness has nowhere trustworthy to get the runtime's identity — `record.runtime` is a declared string and the executable path is caller-controlled — and it keeps the false premise alive as a per-runtime table the next runtime must be added to correctly. Reading the process removes the premise. It also rides on an exchange the landed single-invocation gate already performs: the `GET /v1/models` at close that produces `servedModelID`.
- FIX: `RuntimeContextWindow` (`Sources/MLXSwiftRuntimeContract/RuntimeAttestation.swift`) with three cases that are never collapsed — `.reported(n)` (the runtime named its bound), `.notReported` (it answered and named none), `.unread` (no answer was read; derives `kv=unread`, which joined `unpinnableConditions`). Non-optional field of the attestation, so it lives in the document the *gate* wrote; `admitProvenance(_:observing:)` re-derives the pin from it. `BenchmarkRunCommand.servingAnswer` produces the window and `servedModelID` from one exchange, so a failed read can never present as an absence. `kv=max-kv-size=4096` became `kv=4096`: the value, not the spelling, so a llama.cpp `n_ctx` 8192 and an MLX `--max-kv-size 8192` are one reading of one condition. New `contextBoundNotHonoured` refuses a launch that pinned a bound the process did not run — the pin takes the process's number, so nothing above that clause can see the divergence.
- FIX: G1, additively. `-ub` / `--ubatch-size` read as the second and third spellings of the prefill pin. `--batch-size` deliberately **not** read: it is llama.cpp's logical batch (default 2048) and the physical prompt-evaluation chunk is `--ubatch-size` (default 512). `unpinnableConditions` was not relaxed — it grew by one entry, and a test asserts the whole list so a future removal happens in the open.
- REGRESSION: 8 mutants, all killed. **M1** restores the pre-fix derivation and the production entry then returns **exit 0, `accepted=true`** for a candidate running a 32,768-token window against an unbounded baseline; with the fix the same two spawned processes exit 4 naming both readings. **M8** deletes only the `meta.n_ctx` read in the driver: all **302 contract tests still pass** and six production-entry smoke checks go red — the seam a unit suite cannot see. **M3** and **M6** are the narrowing pair, each making the gate stricter and reddening the tests that pin the admitted class.
- G4, RAISED HERE AND DECIDED SINCE: `benchmark-run --model` demanded an MLX weight *directory* and `modelPath`/`modelDigest` were pins compared for equality, so a GGUF candidate could never satisfy them. Two production-entry refusals, exit **5** each, taken before any launch. Resolved by the story in the 2320 entry above; no format branch was added.
- SCOPE: `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` — `RuntimeAttestation.swift`, `RuntimeBenchmark.swift`, `BenchmarkRunCommand.swift`, new `RuntimeBenchmarkContextBoundTests.swift` (17 tests), `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` (+8 production-entry KV checks, stand-in learned `--n-ctx`), `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml` (`profiles.qwen-benchmark-llamacpp`), `README.md`. Report `.research/260828_llamacpp-in-the-benchmark-gate.md`.
- EVIDENCE: `swift build --build-tests` 0; `swift test` 302/25 exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` **46 checks, 0 failures** exit 0; `xcodebuild` Release BUILD SUCCEEDED exit 0; `swift-format lint --strict` exit 0; `shellcheck -S warning` exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0. The 28 GB model was never loaded; every llama.cpp measurement used the 676 MB Qwen2.5-0.5B Q8_0 fixture on an ephemeral port outside 18000-18999, and no other run's process was signalled.
- STATUS: superseded by the 2320 entry, which carries the final gate figures for `TASK-260828-3fgca3`.

### 2007 — One Binary Now Measures And Judges, And Two Of Three Blockers Did Not Reproduce
- FIX, structural, and it removes a class rather than a case: the migration decision no longer passes through a document. Three review rounds each ended with the production entry returning `accepted=true, exit 0` for a run in which nothing was served, and the third used **no tampering at all** — review started two placeholder HTTP servers that answered `GET /v1/models`, had the shipped `benchmark-attest` observe them correctly, typed the measurements, and had an acceptance in 7.2 seconds. The common shape was a seam: some other program measured and the gate was asked to believe a report about it. `benchmark-attest` is **deleted**, so no caller can direct this binary to observe a process of the caller's choosing. `benchmark-run` launches both runtimes, drives every scenario, samples, times, seals and judges inside one invocation, with no argument through which a measurement can be supplied. `benchmark-compare` is demoted to replay and **cannot return an acceptance at all** — its best outcome is a reproduced rejection, exit 3.
- EVIDENCE: review's own reproduction is now a case in the acceptance suite. Two live `/v1/models`-only runtimes, driven for real by the production subcommand, exit **4** with a refusal that names what was missing. `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` is 39 production-entry checks, and its control is not fabricated: it spawns two real runtimes through `model-harness` and derives every number from exchanges the gate performed.
- LIMIT, measured rather than asserted, and this is the honest part. Three probes replaced the real scenario driver with fabricated results while leaving launch, observation and seal genuine. **L-1** (no transcript) refused — admission requires every scenario to carry the exchanges it came from. **L-2** (synthetic transcript, 0.01 s/scenario) refused — "record claims 0.03s of scenario wall clock, but the gate watched the runtime for only 0.0159s". **L-3** (same, 0.0005 s/scenario) **ACCEPTED, exit 0**. The suite went red on L-3 only through an unrelated downstream fixture, and a red suite for the wrong reason is not detection. So: the **ordinary-caller** forgery class is closed; the **modified-build** class is not. What stands between a fabrication and an acceptance is arithmetic consistency with an observed interval, not any ability to tell a measured number from a typed one. No further hardening was attempted — every extra clause would raise the cost of the same attack without changing its class, which rounds 1 to 3 already demonstrated three times.
- REGRESSION IN THE EVIDENCE, not in the code: **two of revision 3's three blockers did not reproduce.** 8k TTFT 1.210x → **1.000x**; 8k prefill 0.826x → **1.000x**. The candidate reproduced *itself* to within **0.2%** (93.142→92.978 s, 83.572→83.718 tok/s); the incumbent came out **21% slower on its own 8k prefill** (101.126→83.744 tok/s) at the same config, prompts and policy. The per-scenario host load the harness now records does not explain it: 10.18 (Python) against 8.27 (Swift) inside the two 8k windows, nothing like the 3x contamination signature that ruined the first revision-4 attempt. Conclusion: **single-run throughput on this host is worth about ±20%**, the revision-3 throughput blockers are **withdrawn**, and any future accept needs repeats on the scored scenarios.
- DECISION: **REJECT stands, now on one blocker.** Python `mlx-lm` remains the default; `accepted=false`. The surviving blocker is the one that repeated: 8k scenario-local peak footprint **1.151x** (36.14 vs 31.39 GiB, bar 1.10), against **1.144x** measured a revision earlier on a different build. The decision rests on the metric that reproduced, not on the two that did not. No installed configuration changed; `profiles.qwen-local` still points at `mlx_lm.server`.
- MEASUREMENT: capacity, recorded not scored. Both serve 73,016 tokens. Python 971.41 s TTFT / 75.16 tok/s prefill / 7.93 tok/s decode / 45.20 GiB; Swift 1504.45 s / 48.53 / 1.12 / 49.36 GiB. The Swift decode figure is 16 tokens measured at a 1-minute load average of **35.21** with 49.4 GiB resident on a 64 GiB machine — memory pressure the scenario creates for itself. Read it as "much slower", not as 1.12 tok/s; revision 3 measured 4.94 on the same scenario.
- MEASUREMENT: what the candidate wins. `short_prompt` on every axis (TTFT 0.945x, prefill 1.058x, decode 1.061x, footprint 0.990x); 8k decode **1.083x**; soak median 6.62 s vs 7.28 s and aggregate output 9.58 vs 8.70 tok/s with **20/20** on both; first *served* completion **7.02 s vs 10.97 s**, and the Swift runtime answers `503` until the weights are resident where `mlx_lm.server` answers `200` in 2.9 s with nothing loaded.
- EVIDENCE, identity: the binary that served the candidate, the binary that observed both processes and the binary that judged are literally one file, `8a517b10…`. Both attestations name it as observer; the candidate attestation's `observedExecutableDigest` equals its `gateBinaryDigest`. That is R2-C as an enforced clause, checked from the kernel rather than promised.
- EVIDENCE, gates: `swift build` 0; `swift test` 285/24 exit 0; `xcodebuild` Release exit 0; `swift-format lint --strict` exit 0; `shellcheck -S warning` exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` 39 checks exit 0; `lifecycle-smoke.sh` 17 checks exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0; `benchmark-compare` replay exit **3** — expected-red, that is the reproduced rejection. Nine mutants: eight caught (three production bypasses that all compile and leave `admit` completely correct, caught **only** by the production-entry smoke), one — L-3 — recorded as a survivor.
- GAP, stated: the production `benchmark-run` invocation was launched through a `nohup … &` wrapper that discarded its exit status, so the exit code of the run that produced these measurements was **not recorded**. Its `decision.json`, the replay at exit 3 and the smoke's exit-code cases stand in for it; the hour-long measurement was not repeated to recover a number the wrapper should have captured.
- STATUS: `TASK-260827-2v13w8` revision 4 handed to review. Reopen the migration when the 8k scenario-local footprint comes inside 1.10x and the scored scenarios carry repeats rather than single runs. Neither needs upstream work.

### 1906 — The Harness Attested A Stopped Group It Could Not See
- ROOT CAUSE: `shutdownRuntime` in `tools/agents-infra/internal/modelharness/run.go` returned success the instant `exec.Cmd.Wait` reported the direct child, and printed `process group N stopped after ...` on that basis. Wait answers for the one process the harness holds a handle for. A runtime helper spawned into the same group that redirected its inherited stdout/stderr away is invisible to it: the child is reaped, Wait returns, the harness writes a stopped-group record and exits 0 while that member is still alive holding the runtime's port. Measured against the pre-fix revision at the production entry point: harness exit 0, holder pid alive at `kill(pid,0) == nil`, port unbindable.
- FINDING: This is the attestation half of blocker B7 from TASK-260828-28gdmq, whose other half was the orphaning itself. B7 was fixed by signalling `-pgid` instead of the child; the group was still only *assumed* stopped afterwards. An operator reads the exit code precisely to rule out the state the exit code was certifying.
- FIX: The group question is now asked of the kernel. `runProcessGroupStopped` (`run_process_posix.go`) sends signal 0 to `-pgid` and keeps the three answers apart: `ESRCH` is the only one that means empty, `EPERM` means a member is alive and not ours, anything else is a failed read reported as an error — a failed read is not an absence. `shutdownRuntime` polls it every 50 ms after the child is reaped, escalates to `SIGKILL` on the whole group if the grace expires, and writes the stopped record only once the group is empty; an unknown group state after the kill grace returns a failure instead of a stop. Windows keeps the direct-child-only answer explicitly, since this harness manages no job object there.
- REGRESSION: Two tests, both failing against the pre-fix revision. The fixture is a compiled port holder (`TestPortHolderHelperProcess`) that ignores `SIGTERM`, holds a loopback listener, and starts with its stdio redirected away from the pipes Wait watches — all three properties at once, which no shell line can supply. `TestRunSignalledShutdownWaitsForADetachedGroupMember` drives the seam with a tuned grace; `TestModelHarnessRunDoesNotReportStoppedWhileAGroupMemberHoldsThePort` drives the shipped binary with a real directed `SIGTERM`. Both assert group disappearance and port rebind at the instant the harness claims the stop finished, with no retry window — a retry would launder the false claim into a pass. Mutants killed: report-on-Wait-alone (the pre-fix revision) and report-stopped-when-the-grace-expires-instead-of-escalating.
- FINDING: The POSIX shutdown tests broke `GOOS=windows go test -c ./internal/modelharness` at six `syscall.Kill` lines. They now sit behind `//go:build !windows` in `run_shutdown_posix_test.go`, and `run_shutdown_test.go` keeps an unconstrained surface: `shutdownSignals` must include `os.Interrupt` on every platform, which is what makes `signal.Notify` install a handler at all.
- SCOPE: Shutdown attestation only. The fatal-marker restart path still kills the group and relaunches without confirming the group emptied first; a survivor there collides with the replacement runtime on the same port. Not touched here, and not covered by these tests.

### 1822 — llama.cpp Logs The Answer But Never The Question, And Has No Access Log At All
- FINDING: B8 of TASK-260828-28gdmq is closed on evidence against pinned `llama.cpp 0.3.0` build 10621. `llama-server` writes **0 bytes to stdout** and emits **no HTTP access line at any verbosity** — no method, path, status or client — so the two URL-path nonces that survived on `mlx_lm.server` do not survive here. On the URL channel it is strictly *less* observable than the incumbent.
- FINDING: It is also strictly *more* attributable. At the default verbosity it writes per-request `launch_slot_` / `print_timing` / `release` records keyed by a `task` id and a slot id, with prompt/eval token counts and a truncation flag. Three concurrent completions produced three distinct task ids where `mlx_lm.server` produced three byte-identical lines. B2 still stands: the response object and headers carry no slot or task field, so the join key exists engine-side only — the mirror image of the `mlx_lm.server` case.
- FINDING: First audited runtime where a model **output** reaches harness-captured bytes. From `-lv 5` upward the completion and tool calls appear on stderr as one `D Parsed message: {"role":"assistant",…,"tool_calls":[…]}` line, tool schemas appear as compiled GBNF. The **prompt never does** at any tested level (3, 5, 10) — the `<|im_start|>` fragments at lv5 are template machinery, not the assembled request. Do not default a managed profile to `-lv 5`: one startup plus one 24-token request wrote 158,391 bytes, and `runtime.log` is still unrotated (B6).
- FINDING: B4's shape does **not** apply to llama.cpp. Killed mid-body, the client got `http=000` and the capture holds no status line at all, because there is none to write. What it holds is a `launch_slot_` for that task with no matching `release`. Unknown, not misreported — read a launch without a release as an unfinished request.
- ROOT CAUSE: B7 reproduced against `llama-server` on the harness as it was at HEAD: `kill -TERM` at a standalone `model-harness run` left harness exit 143, **0 lifecycle records**, the runtime alive reparented to pid 1, and the port still held. `runOnce` installed no signal handler and set no `SysProcAttr`, so the runtime shared the harness's process group and outlived it.
- FIX: `run.go` handles `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`, starts the runtime with `Setpgid`, forwards to `-pgid`, escalates to `SIGKILL` on the group after 10 s, writes a lifecycle record both ways, and exits 0 on a completed stop. A signalled attempt is never restarted by `restart_on_failure`. Same fixture, same profile, with the fix: exit 0, runtime gone, port free, 2 records. This picks B7's own recommendation — `model-harness run` owns supervisor-signal cleanup — over the interactive-only alternative.
- ANOMALY: The narrowing mutant N1 (`syscall.Kill(pid,…)` instead of `-pid`) **survived** its first run. `exec.Cmd.Wait` blocks on the pipes the grandchild inherited, so the grace period expires and the *unmutated* SIGKILL escalation cleans up the group; the test saw a dead group and passed. Asserting the end state alone proves nothing when a second mechanism reaches the same end state. The test now asserts the group stopped **on the forwarded signal** — the absence of the escalation record — and N1 dies.
- FINDING: The mutant D1 (delete `signal.Notify`) is killed by exactly one of the four tests: the one that builds the shipped binary and sends a real signal. The three seam tests feed the channel by hand and cannot see whether a handler exists. Under D1 that test fails with `output=""` — literally B7's zero bytes.
- REGRESSION RISK: `benchmark-compare` cannot admit a llama.cpp candidate and **nothing was relaxed to make it**. Two clauses in `RuntimeBenchmark.contextPolicy(derivedFrom:)`, measured by compiling TASK-260827-2v13w8's real source read-only. G1: it reads `--prefill-step-size`, llama.cpp spells it `-ub`/`--ubatch-size`, so every record is refused — loud and safe. G2 is the dangerous one: absence of `--max-kv-size` is read as `kv=unbounded`, and **llama.cpp is never unbounded** (measured `n_ctx` 32768 from the model, 8192 with `--ctx-size 8192`). A llama.cpp record would pass while asserting something false, and would falsely *match* an MLX baseline because the pin comparison demands equality.
- DECISION: Do not relax `unpinnableConditions` to admit llama.cpp. Measured against the shipped function, dropping `prefill-step=unpinned` also admits mlx-swift without `--prefill-step-size` (512) and python-mlx-lm without it (2048) — all three derive the byte-identical string, so there is no llama.cpp-only relaxation. The additive path is to widen the *derivation* with the `-ub` spelling, as `--chat-template-args` was already added as a second spelling of reasoning effort. G2 has no additive argv fix at all and needs a trust-model decision from TASK-260827-2v13w8.
- SCOPE: `tools/agents-infra/internal/modelharness/run.go`, new `run_process_posix.go` / `run_process_windows.go` / `run_shutdown_test.go`, new `tools/agents-infra/llamacpp_profile_docs_test.go`, `README.md`, `.research/260828_llamacpp-under-the-managed-harness.md`. `profiles.qwen-local` untouched; the llama.cpp profile is additive. Nothing in `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` was touched — it is not on this branch.
- SCOPE SPLIT: TASK-260828-2jbufw was subsequently narrowed to the model-harness profile half only. G1/G2/G3 are carried by TASK-260828-3fgca3, blocked by TASK-260827-2v13w8 and by TASK-260828-2jbufw. The gate findings above changed no gate code — this delta touches nothing under `tools/mlx-swift-runtime-prototype`, which does not exist on this branch.
- STATUS: TASK-260828-2jbufw profile half ready for review; gate half split out, not delivered here. All runs used a 676 MB Qwen2.5-0.5B fixture on ephemeral ports outside 18000-18999 while TASK-260827-2v13w8 held the host with the 28 GB model; no other run's process was signalled.

### 1748 — The Skew Was A Template Default, And The Gate Now Needs A Witness
- ROOT CAUSE, closed: revision 2's "1.93x prompt-token skew, cause not diagnosed" on `short_prompt` was a harness defect, exactly as review said. The Swift profile passed `--reasoning-effort medium`; the Python profile passed no `--chat-template-args`, so this model's Jinja template resolved its own `xhigh` default and injected an extra system instruction. Reproduced from the tokenizer alone, before any runtime ran: **79/41, 7822/7784, 73054/73016 — a constant +38 tokens on every prompt in the suite**. Both profiles now pin `medium` in their own spelling, `contextPolicy` reads the condition from `--reasoning-effort` *and* from `--chat-template-args`, and the gate refuses a pair that left it to the template.
- MEASUREMENT: rerun of all six scenarios on both runtimes, one prompt policy, one binary (`ba1c7451…`) serving, observing and judging. Both render **41 / 7784 / 73016** tokens. **DECISION: REJECT stands**, `benchmark-compare` exit 3, three blockers, all on the 8k scenario — TTFT **1.210x** (bar 1.10), prefill **0.826x** (bar 0.90), scenario-local peak **1.144x** = 36.10 vs 31.55 GiB (bar 1.10).
- FINDING, and the reason the rerun mattered either way: the three 8k numbers moved by **less than two points** against the same measurements taken at the wrong policy (1.208x → 1.210x, 0.824x → 0.826x, 1.159x → 1.144x). The 8k result was never an artefact of the template mismatch. What the correction did change is `short_prompt`, which stops being disqualified and becomes a scenario the candidate marginally **wins**: TTFT 0.974x, prefill 1.026x, decode 1.000x, footprint 0.990x.
- MEASUREMENT: capacity, recorded not scored. Both serve 73,016 tokens. Python 951.68 s TTFT / 76.72 tok/s prefill / 8.095 tok/s decode / 45.20 GiB; Swift 1413.10 s / 51.67 / 4.942 / 49.36 GiB — **1.485x slower to first token and 0.610x decode**. Single runs on each side; review measured the same Swift scenario 20% slower again in revision 2, so read it as a magnitude, not a score.
- MEASUREMENT: what the candidate wins, reported because a rejection that hides them is an argument. 8k decode 1.008x; soak median 6.686 s vs 6.968 s and aggregate output 9.44 vs 9.16 tok/s with **20/20** on both; whole-process peak 1.092x, inside the band; first served completion 8.10 s vs 8.21 s, and the Swift runtime answers `503` until the weights are resident where `mlx_lm.server` answers `200` in 1.23 s with nothing loaded.
- FIX, and the structural one: `benchmark-compare` stopped verifying a document against itself. Revision 2 cross-checked ten provenance fields against the pins beside them and review supplied a pair where all ten agreed, none of it had happened, and the subcommand exited 0 with `accepted=true`. A new `benchmark-attest open|close` has the **gate binary** observe each pass while it runs and write what it reads itself: the pid's kernel executable path and **start time** (re-read at close, so a recycled pid is refused), the digest of the executable the kernel names, the config digest, the model ID the runtime answers `/v1/models` with over the wire, two readings of the gate's own clock, and its own SHA-256. `--attestations` is required with no default. The judging binary must be the observing binary — which is R2-C as an enforced clause, not a promise.
- EVIDENCE the acceptance was earned: the gate refuses the **real** records the moment the observation is taken away. Empty attestation directory → exit 4 ("the record is the only witness to itself"); judged by a different build → exit 4; `--attestations` omitted → exit 2. The candidate attestation records the kernel running the Release product with digest `ba1c7451…`, and both attestations name that digest as their observer, so served/observed/judged is literally one file.
- LIMIT, stated rather than implied: this is **tamper-evidence, not an adversarial boundary**. The attestation directory is owned by the same user as the driver, so a caller willing to fabricate a second document as well as the record can still get a pair admitted. What it buys is that filling in a record's own fields is no longer enough. The only construction with no residue is one where measurement and judgement never leave a single process.
- SCOPE, recorded where it belongs: an MLX error on an MLX-owned `asyncEval` thread reaches MLX's global handler and **traps**. `MLX.withError` converts only errors delivered to the awaiting task. So `TASK-260827-2h39ya`'s in-process 503 and `TASK-260827-2q77g8`'s batch-release attestation are reachable **only for failures delivered as throws**; otherwise recovery begins at supervisor detection of process death and cleanup is performed by process termination. Now written into `GenerationWorkerHealth` and `GenerationBatchRecovery`'s own documentation, not just a report.
- NOT DIAGNOSED, and stated as such: `tool_call` renders 313 Python tokens against 296 Swift for the same request (1.057x, inside the skew band, parity-scored only). Soak footprint drift +124.5 MB Swift, −496.2 MB Python — both bounded, Python's negative drift consistent with prompt-cache eviction.
- EVIDENCE: `swift test` 271/23 exit 0; `xcodebuild` Release exit 0; `swift-format lint --strict` exit 0; `shellcheck -S warning` exit 0; `py_compile` exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` 26 checks exit 0; `lifecycle-smoke.sh` exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0; `benchmark-compare` exit **3** — expected-red, that is the rejection. Ten mutants, **all caught**: six narrowing, one deleting, three production bypasses. **P1/P2/P3 all compile, leave `admit` completely correct, and are caught only by the production-entry smoke** — P2 (judge with the digest the attestation names) only fails on a pair some *other* build observed end to end, which is why the smoke carries both the single-forged and the forged-pair case.
- STATUS: `TASK-260827-2v13w8` revision 3 handed to review. No installed configuration changed; `profiles.qwen-local` still points at `mlx_lm.server`. Reopen the migration when 8k prefill reaches 0.90x, 8k TTFT 1.10x and the 8k scenario-local footprint 1.10x at the pinned equal chunk and prompt policy, and when the missing cross-request prompt cache is accounted for. None of the four needs upstream work.

### 1715 — A Gate You Prove By Reading Source Is Not Proven — The Bypass Mutant Survived
- ROOT CAUSE: rev2 claimed `quant_equivalence.main()` was bound to the single comparability decision by two source-string checks: `'comparability_verdict(' in inspect.getsource(main)` and a regex for bare numeric `> N` comparisons. Both are syntactic. Review kept `if False: comparability_verdict(rows)` so the first check passed, decided inline at `RATIO_CEIL + 1.0` — which the regex cannot see because the threshold is a *name* — and the entire suite exited 0 while the real CLI path admitted the FP8 ratio 3.889.
- FINDING: The general shape, worth carrying: a check that cannot observe behaviour cannot witness it. Source inspection tests the text of the call site, and a dead call is text. Only running the production entry point and reading what it emitted distinguishes "wired up" from "looks wired up".
- FIX: `quant_equivalence.py` grew exactly one seam — `collect_rows(n_rows)` — and `main()` now takes its rows from it. `test_alignment_guard.py` replaces that seam and runs the real `main()`, asserting the verdict line it printed (`COMPARABLE` / `NOT COMPARABLE` / `INCOMPLETE`) *and* its exit status, for all seven comparability cases. No test calls `comparability_verdict()` directly and no test reads production source to decide whether the gate is reachable. Suite: 16 checks, 0 failures.
- FINDING: Both production mutants are killed at the file level, not just in-suite. Copying the pair into a scratch dir and mutating only `quant_equivalence.py`: `RATIO_CEIL` 3.0→4.0 gives exit 1 / 3 failures; review's own dead-call bypass gives exit 1 / 3 failures. In both runs the FP8 gate-2 case is among the failures — the assertion that matters is the one that flips.
- FINDING: A mutant harness needs its own guard. `mutant()` fails its named check unless the mutation hit exactly one site, changed the source, AND produced a module that loads. Without the load check the bypass mutant applied to an already-bypassed file raised `IndentationError` and crashed the suite mid-run instead of reporting a failure; an unbuildable mutant proves nothing and must never pass silently.
- FINDING: The `collect_rows` extraction is behaviour-preserving — the 1024-row rerun (`quant-equivalence-04.log`) is byte-identical to rev2's: 16 paired tensors, mean ratio 0.766, COMPARABLE.
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260828-3g87i4/quant_equivalence.py`, `.temp/TASK-260828-3g87i4/test_alignment_guard.py`, board resources of the same names. No repository source changed.
- STATUS: TASK-260828-3g87i4 rev3 ready for review. F2-F5 were accepted in rev2 and are untouched; the report's check-count claim is corrected to the 16 the suite prints (rev1/rev2 claimed 10 while 12 ran).

### 1712 — The 75k Abort Was Ours, Not Upstream's: MLX Swift Serves The Context Through The Text-Only Factory
- CORRECTION: the 0552 entry below is wrong on its central claim. It reported the 75k-context abort as an upstream-only defect with "no repository workaround", and review rejected that. The byte arithmetic was right; the attribution was not. `ModelLoader` tried `MLXVLM.VLMModelFactory` **first**, and that is the only reason `MLXVLM.Qwen35VLModel.prepare` — which declares `windowSize _: Int?` and discards it — ever saw the prompt.
- FINDING: the same pinned `mlx-swift-lm bd4b743` registers `model_type` `qwen3_5` in `MLXLLM/LLMModelFactory.swift:45` too, building `MLXLLM.Qwen35Model`, whose prepare comes from the `LLMModel` extension and chunks at `windowSize ?? 512`. It loads this exact 28 GB directory in **4.94 s** and **serves the 73,016-token prompt**. `Qwen35Model.sanitize` drops `vision_tower` / `model.visual` outright, and `ChatCompletionRequest` already refuses image and audio parts — so text-only is not a degraded mode on this surface, it is the only capability it can reach. `--model-factory` now selects the order and **`text-only` is the default**; the `ready` event publishes the requested preference beside the factory that actually built the model, so a fallback is visible rather than assumed.
- FINDING: the previous prefill comparison was measuring a **hidden 4x asymmetry**. `mlx_lm.server` defaults `--prefill-step-size` to 2048; `MLXLMCommon.GenerateParameters` defaults `prefillStepSize` to 512. Revision 1 compared 512-token chunks against 2048-token ones and reported the difference as a difference between runtimes. A `--prefill-step-size` flag was added, both profiles state `2048`, and the comparison gate now refuses a pair that left the condition to either default. The incumbent's behaviour is unchanged — 2048 is what it was already doing.
- DECISION: **REJECT stands, on entirely different grounds.** Python `mlx-lm` stays the default; `benchmark-compare` exit 3, `accepted=false`. All six scenarios now pass on **both** runtimes, capacity included. What blocks is narrow and closable without upstream work: 8k prefill **0.824x** (bar 0.90), 8k TTFT **1.208x** (bar 1.10), 8k scenario-local peak **1.159x** (bar 1.10, 36.12 GiB vs 31.15 GiB), and `short_prompt` disqualified as a comparison by a **1.93x prompt-token skew** (41 Swift tokens against 79 Python for the same messages — cause not diagnosed, and stated as not diagnosed).
- MEASUREMENT: the capacity result, recorded rather than scored. Both runtimes serve ~73k. Python 819.25 s TTFT at 89.17 tok/s prefill and 45.20 GiB; Swift 1316.10 s at 55.48 tok/s and 49.36 GiB — **1.61x slower**, a capability the runtime has and a caller would notice.
- MEASUREMENT: what the candidate genuinely won, reported because a rejection that hides them is an argument rather than a decision. 8k decode 1.065x, soak median latency 6.507 s vs 7.415 s, soak aggregate output 9.79 vs 8.65 tok/s, whole-pass peak 1.092x (comparable this time, because both passes completed the same parity work). Both soaks 0/20 failures — a bounded pass for **both**, not a win for either.
- FIX: real MLX failures now reach the supervision contracts at all. `MLX/ErrorHandler.swift` dispatches to `fatalError` with no handler installed, so revision 1's abort was a SIGTRAP and `GenerationEngine.generate`'s `catch` — with the classifier, the batch ledger, `/health` and the supervision marker downstream of it — never ran. Generation is wrapped in `MLX.withError`, the `ErrorBox` is checked after every streamed item, and MLX's message is rethrown verbatim as `GenerationError.mlx(_:)`, the same bytes the fault seam feeds the classifier. Production-tested on the real model via `--model-factory vision-first`: the identical 255,904,140,288-byte allocation against the 41,747,087,360-byte limit now returns **HTTP 500 in 46.7 s**, `/health` stays **200** (the allocator's oversized-request throw is request-scoped and deliberately not an invalidating signature), `generation_batch_released` fires, and a following request on the same process succeeds. Limit stated rather than assumed: the handler is a task local, so an error on an `asyncEval` thread MLX owns still traps.
- FIX: the comparison gate stopped accepting evidence nobody had to earn. Review minted two records by hand — empty `revisions`, different `--config` paths, identical self-authored pins — and the shipped `benchmark-compare` exited 0 with `accepted=true`; the defect was that nothing in a record referred to a run. `RunRecord` now carries a **non-optional** `LaunchProvenance` (driver argv + script digest, launcher argv, config path + digest, profile, launch executable + digest, rendered launch argv, sampled pid), and `contextPolicy` is **derived** by `RuntimeBenchmark.contextPolicy(derivedFrom:)` from that rendered argv and **re-derived** by `admit`. Also refused: empty revisions, unequal config digests, one executable behind two runtime identities, a launch argv that never mentions the pinned model, a launcher command that does not carry its own config, a digest that is not 64 lowercase hex, and scenario wall clock that cannot fit the interval it claims.
- FIX: memory is scored where it means something. `FootprintSampler` keeps a **scenario-local window**; the running maximum survives as `processPeakSoFarBytes` and is never scored. The whole-process peak is scored only when every parity scenario had the same outcome on both runtimes — revision 1 divided two whole-pass maxima from different completed work, called 1.094x "within", and the candidate's lower number came from a pass that **aborted before the expensive scenario**. `run_soak` also stopped labelling `completion_tokens / total elapsed` as decode throughput; it is aggregate output throughput and now says so.
- EVIDENCE: `swift test` 253 tests / 21 suites exit 0; `xcodebuild` Release exit 0; `xcrun swift-format lint --strict` exit 0; `shellcheck -S warning scripts/*.sh` exit 0; `benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` 14 checks exit 0; `lifecycle-smoke.sh` exit 0; `mlx-error-handling-probe.sh` 7 checks exit 0; `python3 -m py_compile` exit 0; `git diff --check` exit 0. `benchmark-compare` exit **3** — expected-red, that is the rejection.
- EVIDENCE: 11 mutants, all caught. Seven narrowing (digest check reduced to presence; derivation stops reading the prefill chunk; interval check keeps 10x slack; config binding reduced to presence; skew scored in one direction; whole-process peak scored regardless of parity; model-path binding reduced to "an argv exists"), one deleting, three production bypasses. **P2** (subcommand logs the refusal and returns exit 0) and **P3** (subcommand stops naming its required scenarios) both compile, leave `admit` fully correct, and are invisible to the contract suite — caught **only** by the production-entry smoke. That is the gap review named.
- FINDING, and it cost a rebuild: the first `benchmark-compare` run against the real records exposed a live defect in the new skew clause. Scoring the skew as `candidate / baseline` passes every upper bound whenever the candidate renders the **shorter** prompt — 41/79 = 0.519, comfortably inside `<= 1.10`. The symmetric form is `N5`, kept as a mutant. The serving binary (`19c54c5…`) and the judging binary (`3e5fdcc…`) are therefore different builds, named separately in the report; the serving path is unchanged between them and the rebuilt binary was checked to emit a byte-identical `ready` event on the same model.
- STATUS: `TASK-260827-2v13w8` revision 2 handed to review. No installed configuration changed; `profiles.qwen-local` still points at `mlx_lm.server`. Re-open the migration when 8k prefill reaches 0.90x at a pinned equal chunk, the template divergence behind the 1.93x short-prompt skew is diagnosed, and the 8k scenario-local footprint comes inside 1.10x — none of which needs upstream work.

### 1710 — The Host-Contention Guard Can See Its Own Waiter
- ANOMALY: A background waiter written as an inline shell command that greps for `mlx_lm|mlx-swift-runtime-prototype|runtime-benchmark.py` matches *itself* — the pattern string is part of its own `/bin/zsh -c` command line, so `pgrep -f` returns the waiter and the host never reads as free. Worse, `load_and_answer.sh`'s own guard would then see that waiter and refuse with exit 3 forever.
- FIX: Put any such waiter in a script file (`.wait-then-load.sh`) so its command line carries only the file name, and filter the waiter's own PID out of `pgrep`. Do not weaken `CONTENTION_PROCS` to work around it.
- FINDING: `load_and_answer.sh` refused at 17:05 with exit 3 for the right reason — TASK-260827-2v13w8-rev4's benchmark held port 18031 with both `mlx_lm-relux.server` and `mlx-swift-runtime-prototype` live. Neither was signalled.

### 1557 — The MLX Vision Tower Is In The File, Not In Memory — And Two Gates Were Measuring Themselves
- REGRESSION: The 1510 entry and rev1 of the equivalence report both said the MLX vision tower is "resident whether used or not" (921460192 bytes). That is **false** and it distorted the exact memory criterion the llama.cpp-vs-MLX comparison exists to measure. `mlx_lm.models.qwen3_5.Model.sanitize` drops every `vision_tower.*` / `model.visual.*` key, and `mlx_lm.utils.load_model` sanitizes **before** `model.load_weights(...)` and **before** `mx.eval(model.parameters())`, so a text-only run never evaluates them into parameters. The mlx-swift prototype's `--model-factory text-only` drops them too.
- FINDING: Measured, not argued. A real text-only `mlx_lm.load` of the 8-bit build reports 1847 parameter tensors / 28579478528 bytes, `mx.get_active_memory()` 28579478536, and **zero** resident vision tensors — 921460192 bytes on disk never materialise. Vision placement is a file-membership difference; residency is zero on both runtimes on the default text path. Do not add or subtract 921/931 MB from any memory comparison.
- ANOMALY: Peak process RSS is the wrong metric for this model and disagrees with itself — 22305783808 then 18627723264 bytes across two runs of the identical load, because the weights are mmap-backed and paged lazily. `mx.get_active_memory()` was stable to the byte. The benchmark must name an allocator-level or `footprint`-level number.
- FINDING: Correction in the staged weights' favour. Hugging Face gating blocks the *blob*, not the *metadata*: `api/models/orcarouter/Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-GGUF?blobs=true` returns HTTP 200 and publishes the LFS SHA-256 of every file. Both staged files are byte-identical to the first-party artifacts — `31756fca...24f8d6` (Q8_0) and `add205b7...bc5288` (mmproj) match a full local recomputation exactly. The 1510 claim that byte-identity to the gated file was unverifiable was too weak. The gated blob is still not downloadable here (HTTP 401); that is a separate fact about access, not about content.
- ROOT CAUSE: Both delivered gates were verifying a copy of themselves. The FP8 negative test compared against a `RATIO_CEIL` the *test file* declared, so moving the production threshold from 3.0 to 4.0 admitted a 3.889 ratio while the test still exited 0. The load-and-answer script printed `finish_reason` and content and asserted neither, so a fake answering "The capital of Armenia is Baku." got exit 0.
- FIX: One production decision, one threshold: `quant_equivalence.comparability_verdict()` over `RATIO_CEIL`, called from `main()`, which now holds no ratio comparison of its own. The test declares no threshold and attacks that function. `load_and_answer.sh` exits non-zero on curl failure (7), unexpected JSON shape or zero completion tokens (8), `finish_reason != stop` (9), empty or wrong answer (10), early server exit (5); the prompt and required answer are not overridable.
- FINDING: Both fixes are bounded by mutants of the production code, not just covered by tests. `RATIO_CEIL` 3.0→4.0 and `main()` deciding inline again each drive the guard suite to exit 1. Deleting the answer assertion fails 6 of 11 load-gate cases; merely widening the accepted answer from `\byerevan\b` to `\bcapital\b` — enough to admit "…is Baku." — fails exactly one. The narrowing mutant is the one that proves the gate bounds the answer rather than the presence of a string.
- ANOMALY: A shared host can turn negative tests green for the wrong reason. Six load-gate cases "passed" as rejections while actually exiting 3 — the host-contention guard firing on a neighbouring run's port 18799 and mlx-swift build, not the answer gate. Asserting merely "non-zero" hid it; asserting the EXACT exit code exposed it. The suite now plants its own port/process sentinels, scans only those for the answer cases, and runs three further cases at the untouched defaults to prove the real guard still refuses. `load_and_answer.sh` itself keeps the global defaults: if it exits 3, wait for the host rather than narrowing its scans.
- ANOMALY: The rev1 analysis was not runnable from its own shebang. `#!/usr/bin/env python3` resolves to Homebrew 3.14.7 with no NumPy; the reproducible interpreter is the existing `/Users/alexis/.local/pipx/venvs/mlx-lm/bin/python` (numpy 2.5.2, mlx_lm 0.31.3). Now recorded in every script docstring and in §8 of the report. Sample size is also part of the result: rev1 ran 512 rows per tensor, rev2 runs the 1024-row default; per-tensor ratios move in the third decimal, the 0.766 mean does not.
- STATUS: TASK-260828-3g87i4 rev2 ready for review. COMPARABLE-with-conditions stands; condition 2 changed from "note the MLX vision tower is resident-but-unused" to "compare measured footprint, not file membership". Real `llama-server` load-and-answer rerun green with the delivered asserting script at untouched defaults (exit 0 at 16:36, ready ~25s warm, `finish_reason stop`, correct answer). It first refused with exit 3 for ~25 minutes while TASK-260827-2v13w8-rev4 smoke held port 18799; that neighbour was not signalled and the guard was not narrowed to get past it.

### 1510 — GGUF Q8_0 And MLX 8-bit Quantize The Same Numbers; The MLX Build Lost The MTP Head
- DECISION: Homebrew `llama.cpp` 0.3.0 is the pinned runtime — upstream tag `v0.3.0`, commit `c1d0e7a004015f23bc0233470b747b596f29b264`, build 10621, released 2026-08-25. `brew pin llama.cpp` applied. Its `libllama.dylib` carries the `qwen35` architecture and `llama_model_qwen35::graph_mtp`, which the publisher requires. Nothing here becomes a default; `profiles.qwen-local` stays on Python `mlx_lm.server`.
- FINDING: MLX 8-bit affine group64 and GGUF Q8_0 cost exactly the same 8.5 bits per weight by different routes — MLX pays 8 bits plus a bf16 scale and a bf16 zero point per 64 weights; Q8_0 pays 8 bits plus an fp16 scale per 32, symmetric, no zero point. Measured against the shared BF16 source of record, the GGUF is consistently the *more* faithful of the two: mean relative-RMS ratio 0.766 over 16 paired tensors, so Q8_0's 2x finer block beats MLX's zero point at 8 bits.
- FINDING: The staged GGUF did **not** come through the `orcarouter/...-FP8` checkpoint that its own model card links as base. A BF16 tensor re-rounded to the E4M3 grid lands at ratio 3.889 against the same MLX baseline; the real artifact lands at 0.790. The measurement discriminates the hypothesis rather than assuming it.
- ANOMALY: The MLX 8-bit conversion **dropped the MTP head that exists in the BF16 source** — zero `mtp`/`nextn` tensors in its index, while the source has `mtp.fc` and `mtp.layers.0.*`. The GGUF keeps it as `blk.64` with `qwen35.nextn_predict_layers = 1`. So llama.cpp can run MTP speculative decoding on these weights and the MLX baseline structurally cannot: any tokens/s produced with MTP enabled is a different algorithm, not a faster runtime, and must not be compared as one.
- FINDING: llama.cpp build 10621 logs `model has unused tensor blk.64.* -- ignoring` for all 15 MTP tensors on the default path, summing to exactly 451319808 bytes. The MTP block is a file-size and capability difference, not a default-path memory difference.
- ANOMALY: The `qwen35` GGUF converter reorders the Gated-DeltaNet value-head axis relative to the HF layout used by both BF16 and MLX. Five tensors first compared as pure noise at cosine 0.04; under the recovered bijection they land at the same 0.75 ratio as everything else. Tensor-by-tensor tooling cannot assume matching indices on `attn_qkv`, `attn_gate`, `ssm_alpha`, `ssm_beta`, `ssm_out`.
- FINDING: Vision placement differs and skews naive memory comparisons. MLX carries the vision tower in-file in bf16 (921460192 bytes, resident whether used or not); the GGUF text model has zero vision tensors and vision lives in a separate 931145984-byte mmproj loaded only with `--mmproj`. That mmproj is BF16 despite the `F16` in its filename.
- FINDING: Language-model weight bytes, MTP excluded on both sides: 28579478528 (MLX) vs 28584769536 (GGUF), a 0.02% difference.
- SCOPE: `orcarouter/Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-GGUF` is a gated repo and this host has no Hugging Face token, so the first-party Q8_0 is unreachable. Staged the ungated `chimingw/Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-OrcaRouter-GGUF` Q8_0 instead, whose manifest records `llama-quantize ... Q8_0 64` from the same orcarouter F16 GGUF and whose four quant files are byte-size identical to the gated originals. Local sha256 matches the publisher manifest; byte-identity to the gated file itself is unverifiable and is not claimed.
- STATUS: TASK-260828-3g87i4 ready for review. Weights at `/Users/alexis/src/local-models/Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-GGUF-Q8_0/` with `PROVENANCE.md`. Load-and-answer passed through `llama-server` at exit 0 after waiting ~16 minutes for TASK-260827-2v13w8 to release the host; no other run's process was signalled.

### 1410 — A Near Miss Bounds Only The End It Diverges At
- ANOMALY: The rev1 observability report claimed its supervision-marker gate was bounded against truncation "from either end", but only one near miss existed and it diverged solely at the marker's trailing `(`. Re-running the mutants proved the claim false: `marker[:14]` and `marker[:len(marker)-1]` were killed, while `marker[14:]` — a genuine head truncation — **survived with exit 0**. A near miss can only kill mutants that widen the match toward the end where it diverges.
- FIX: `TestSupervisionMarkerGateRejectsNearMissOutput` is now table-driven with two near misses — one sharing the marker's whole head, one sharing its whole tail including the `(` — and each asserts at run time that it does not contain the full marker, so it cannot silently degrade into a positive case. All six mutants now exit 1.
- FINDING: Reporting-accuracy corrections to the same investigation. The rev1 headline ("nothing captured on the managed path contains a prompt or completion") was overstated and contradicted the task's own Pi artifact, which carries the user message and the assistant completion text. The established result is narrower and stronger: harness-captured engine streams contain neither the assembled HTTP request nor the response body, and Pi-side records cannot be joined to engine-side requests.
- ANOMALY: B8's stated reason was false on this host. `brew search llama` returns the `llama.cpp` formula, available but uninstalled; rev1 claimed Homebrew offered only `ollama` and `whisper-talk-llama`. The conclusion (`llama-server` untested) was right for the wrong reason — the barrier is a scoped install plus a GGUF fixture, now owned by TASK-260828-3g87i4.
- DECISION: Every blocker B1–B8 now carries one explicit owning decision plus a recommendation instead of a menu of options; B2 stays explicitly dependent on B1 because no engine-side component exists to write the record.
- STATUS: TASK-260828-28gdmq rev2 ready for review. Board report resource and `.research/` copy verified byte-identical (sha256 `5e44bebf…`). Both declared board validation commands (`go vet ./...`, `go test ./... -count=1` in `tools/agents-infra`) rerun green at rev2.

### 1339 — Managed Harness Captures No Model I/O
- FINDING: `model-harness run` forwards child stdout/stderr verbatim and authors nothing per request (`internal/modelharness/run.go:30-39,77-105`). On `mlx_lm.server` and the Swift prototype, prompts, completions, tool schemas, tool calls and response ids travel over the HTTP socket only; live nonce trace found 0 hits for all of them in harness-captured output. Only nonces planted in a URL survive, via the access-log request line.
- FINDING: No request identity is carried end to end. Three concurrent completions produced three byte-identical `POST /v1/chat/completions ... 200 -` lines. Pi records the engine's `responseId`; the engine never emits it. The join key exists on one side of the boundary only.
- ANOMALY: A worker killed mid-body leaves an access line reading `200` for a request whose caller received zero bytes — status is logged at header-write time. Failure-path evidence is not merely absent, it misreports the outcome.
- FINDING: A directed `SIGTERM` to a standalone `model-harness run` kills the harness with zero bytes written and reparents the runtime to PID 1, still holding its port. No signal handler, no `SysProcAttr`. Interactive Ctrl-C and the Pi-managed path both clean up correctly; the gap is specific to a directed signal.
- FINDING: Shared-broker traffic is unattributable — one `runtime.log` per runtime key, no lease/session/run marker, `broker.log` 0 bytes, and `leases/` emptied on release. `runtime.log` is `O_APPEND` with no rotation, no size cap, and `cleanupSharedRuntimeState` (`pi_shared_broker_darwin.go:490-500`) deliberately preserves it.
- SCOPE: Report `.research/260828_model-io-observability-through-harness.md`; tests `tools/agents-infra/internal/modelharness/run_observability_test.go` (4 characterisation/negative tests, each verified against a killing mutant).
- DECISION: Nothing was implemented. Eight gaps recorded as named blockers B1–B8 — fixing one in passing would have made the report describe a system that did not exist when the question was asked.
- STATUS: TASK-260828-28gdmq handed to review; see the 1410 entry for the rev2 reporting corrections. `llama-server` untested (absent from PATH; the Homebrew formula does exist — see 1410); retention under sustained load untested.

### 1326 — Benchmark Provenance Is Still Self-Mintable
- REGRESSION: revision 2's production `benchmark-compare` still accepts two records whose pids, digests, argv, timings, pins, scenarios, and metrics were all authored by the caller. `RuntimeBenchmark.admit` cross-checks the fields but never binds them to evidence outside the records; the caller can mint a consistent fiction.
- EVIDENCE: the old provenance-free forgery is now refused with exit 4, but `scripts/benchmark-gate-smoke.sh` itself constructs a fully fabricated pair and the production `BenchmarkCompareCommand.run(arguments:)` returns exit 0 with `accepted=true`. Negative shape: forged or self-minted evidence. Production call sites: `RuntimeBenchmark.swift:472`, `RuntimeBenchmark.swift:564`, `BenchmarkCompareCommand.swift:91`.
- STATUS: `TASK-260827-2v13w8` revision 2 changes requested. Bind comparison admission to driver-owned evidence the input-record author cannot synthesize, and keep the generalized fully populated forgery as a production-entry negative test.

### 1325 — The Benchmark Compared Medium Against Implicit Xhigh
- ROOT CAUSE: the Swift profile passes `--reasoning-effort medium`; the Python `mlx_lm.server` profile passes no equivalent chat-template argument. This model's template defaults the absent value to `xhigh` and injects an extra system instruction.
- EVIDENCE: independent tokenizer rendering gives 79 tokens for the Python profile default and 41 for explicit `medium`, exactly matching the reported `short_prompt` records. The same template mismatch adds 38 tokens to the long and 75k prompts. Configuration sites: `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml:49`, `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml:60`, `examples/model-harness.benchmark.toml:77`.
- DECISION: the 1.927x short-prompt skew is a harness defect, not runtime behavior. The conservative migration REJECT remains directionally correct because the 8k prefill, TTFT, and footprint thresholds also fail, but every scenario must be rerun with the same explicit template policy before the report is a like-for-like comparison.

### 1324 — MLX Error Recovery Ends At The Task-Local Boundary
- FINDING: `GenerationEngine.run` correctly wraps generation in `MLX.withError`; a synchronous allocator error returns HTTP 500, releases the batch, preserves health, and permits a following request. `GenerationEngine.swift:231` also correctly states the limit: an MLX-owned `asyncEval` thread without task context still reaches the global trap.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260827-2h39ya` and `TASK-260827-2q77g8` therefore cover thrown/task-local failures, not arbitrary MLX backend faults. On an `asyncEval` trap there is no in-process health-503 transition, batch-release attestation, or teardown decision; recovery is process-death detection and supervisor replacement.

### 1120 — Every Threshold Above Zero Is A Band, And Review Lives In The Band
- CORRECTION: the 0745 entry above is wrong where it presents the absolute-residue clause as the fix. `activeBytes < weightFootprintBytes` is not a bound, it is an *interval* — everything strictly below one full model — and review walked a production input straight into it. The claim that "if this model's weight set were still held, MLX would be calling at least the whole footprint active" quietly assumes the weight set is retained whole. It is not, and this candidate's own seams prove it.
- REGRESSION: review defeated the revision-5 teardown with a narrowed production input, the fifth consecutive round in the same shape. A strict subset of the model's parameter arrays — copied out, so all 316 registered `Module` owners died and ownership reported the model released — left 255,724,192 B of a 262,361,760 B footprint active. Container gone, zero live owners, nothing in flight, 3 stable samples, `returned_bytes` 615,883,060 clearing the footprint: every clause green, `generation_shared_cache_rebuilt` emitted, pending cleared, over ~97.5% of the model.
- ROOT CAUSE: the **bypass path around the check** shape, and the deeper error is arithmetic rather than plumbing. MLX's allocator counters are process-global, so any allowance `A > 0` is a promise that nothing weight-sized fits underneath it — and there is no reading that separates `A` bytes of sampler state from `A` bytes of retained weights. Four rounds moved the threshold; moving it again only moves the band.
- FIX: the band is removed, not narrowed. `GenerationBatchRecovery.residualNonWeightAllowanceBytes` is **0**, and the clause is `activeBytes <= allowance`. `activeMemory == 0` means no MLX buffer of any kind is alive, so no *weight* buffer is alive either — the one reading a process-global counter can carry an attribution claim on, because it leaves nothing to attribute. Maximum admissible residue: **0 bytes**. Nothing hides beneath zero.
- FIX: weak `Module` ownership is now documented and tested as a **veto and never a proof**. Copied `MLXArray` values outlive the modules that held them; the candidate's own `--fault-inject-teardown-retain-weight-arrays` seam demonstrates it, which made "no live owner" evidence review could turn around. The unit expectations that accepted `footprint - 1` and half-footprint residue with owners gone are deleted — they encoded the defect.
- FINDING, and the honest cost: this runtime's *clean* condemned teardown leaves 2,720 B of post-generation MLX state (sampler and RNG arrays) active. That is not zero, so **the clean path abandons too** and a completed rebuild is essentially never attested on any path here. Not engineered around, because the only way to engineer around it is to re-open a band. The pool is then left holding the freed model — measured 331,887,724 B of `cache_bytes` — until the supervisor replaces the process the abandonment's marker already demands. A false abandonment costs a restart condemnation had made necessary anyway; a false attestation tells an operator the host is free with a condemned model still on it.
- DECISION: an opportunistic `Memory.clearCache()` on the abandoned path was considered and rejected. It would return the pool on the clean path at the cost of splitting "cleared" from "attested" into two states an operator has to reason about, in the exact area where five rounds of review found conflation. Phase 6a asserts the cost instead, and a mutant that clears quietly reddens it alone.
- FIX: review's bypass is kept as a maintained production input rather than a scratch mutant. `--fault-inject-teardown-retain-weight-array-subset` holds the largest half of the flattened parameter arrays by `nbytes`; phase 6h reproduces the reviewer's figure exactly — `observed_active=255,724,192` against a `262,361,760` footprint, `returned=615,547,160`, 0 of 316 owners live, at rest — and requires abandonment.
- EVIDENCE: 193 checks exit 0; contract suite 204 tests / 19 suites exit 0; `dead-generation-smoke.sh` 45 checks exit 0, so the TASK-260827-2h39ya 503 boundary is intact; `xcodebuild` Release, `swift-format lint --strict`, `shellcheck -S warning` all exit 0.
- EVIDENCE: seven mutants die, and the two production ones separate the intervals. P1 restores the revision-5 gate (`activeBytes < weightFootprintBytes`): 32 failures, including `SUBSET GATE: a rebuild was attested with 255,724,192B of weights still resident` — review's finding measured directly at the production call site. P2, **narrowing**, raises the allowance to 4,096 B: 19 failures, all in the clean-path phase, while phase 6h stays green on its verdict — proving the two phases cover different intervals and that the bound is proved by tightening, not only by deletion. P3 clears the pool quietly on abandonment: exactly 1 failure, the cost gate. Contract M1 (allowance 4,096), M2 (revision-5 gate), M3 (residue clause deleted) and M4 (ownership clause deleted) each redden `swift test`.
- STATUS: `TASK-260827-2q77g8` revision 6 handed back to review.

### 0745 — A Process-Global Counter Cannot Say Whose Bytes Came Back
- REGRESSION: review defeated the revision-4 teardown. The release verdict was a **process-global byte delta** — `baselineActiveBytes - activeBytes >= weightFootprintBytes`, with the baseline sampled in `GenerationEngine.deinit`. Review drove the composed production path with a six-thousand-word prompt, which makes the failed request's own KV state larger than the model, and the release of that request alone paid the subtraction. Two consecutive runs: `returned_bytes` 608,909,592 against a 262,361,760 B footprint, `release_observed=true`, `shared_cache_rebuilds=1`, pending cleared — with post-teardown `active_bytes` at exactly 262,361,760. Every weight still resident.
- ROOT CAUSE: the **forged or self-minted evidence** shape, in its subtlest form yet. The measurement was real and taken from MLX; what was invented was the *attribution*. A counter that aggregates the whole process cannot say which owner returned a byte, so any large enough unrelated release satisfies a comparison scoped to the model.
- FIX: attribution is now read where it actually lives. `WeightOwnerRegistry` registers this model's whole module tree — `ModelContext.model.modules()`, 316 objects for the 0.5B acceptance model — weakly, at load. Every weight array of an MLX Swift model is a stored property of one of those objects, so "none of them is alive" is a statement about *these* weights rather than about the process's byte total. An unpopulated registry fails closed: "zero live owners" would otherwise be true forever.
- FIX: the byte test is now **absolute, not a delta**. `activeBytes` must be strictly below the model's measured load footprint — what is still resident, not what moved. The revision-4 delta is kept only as a *necessary* condition, so the fall still has to have happened inside this teardown window. Two further clauses close what review named beside the finding: no generation may be in flight (a concurrent request's allocation is equally unattributable), and the reading must be at rest for `minimumStableActiveSamples` consecutive polls (a destruction still running is a falling count, and a sample taken during that fall is a partial release read as a finished one).
- FINDING: adding a clause is worth nothing if no negative can isolate it, and the first cut of this rework had that defect. The one-byte narrowing mutant (`<` relaxed to `<=`) reddened the contract suite and left the whole production suite **green**, because every existing retention seam keeps some object of the model tree alive and the ownership clause refused first. Two new seams fix that, each isolating one clause: `--fault-inject-teardown-retain-weight-modules` parks a strict subset of the tree, so the residue lands *below* the footprint — what a released model looks like to a counter — while ownership refuses; `--fault-inject-teardown-retain-weight-arrays` parks the flattened parameter arrays and no object at all, so ownership reports the model released and only the residue can refuse.
- EVIDENCE: separation on MLX's own figures, all with `container_deallocated=true` and `/health` 503. Clean: 0 of 316 owners live, `observed_active_bytes` 2,720, returned 301,226,372, rebuilds 1, pending false. Long-context whole-model retention (6e): 316 of 316 live, active 262,361,760, **returned 608,909,584 — the revision-4 gate would have attested here**, abandoned 1. Module subset (6f): 158 of 316 live, active 174,944,928 (below the footprint), returned 696,326,408, abandoned 1. Weight arrays (6g): **0 of 316 live**, in-flight 0, at rest, returned 608,909,584, active 262,361,760, abandoned 1.
- EVIDENCE: six mutants die. Production P1, ownership clause deleted (the revision-4 shape): 12 failures reddening **only** phase 6f, at `shared_cache_rebuilds=1` and `cache_bytes=0` beside 158 live owners — the defect class measured directly. Production N1, **narrowing**, `<` relaxed to `<=`: 12 failures reddening **only** phase 6g, at `observed_active_bytes=262,361,760` attested as released; the bound is proven by tightening, not only by deletion. Production P2, the registration call deleted from `ModelLoader.load` while the registry type stays perfect: contract suite **exit 0, all 197 green**, and the smoke catches it with 18 failures — phase 6a fails *closed*, abandoning with 332,234,364 B of pool still held. Contract N2 (empty-registry guard), N3 (stability bound relaxed to one sample) and N4 (in-flight veto deleted) each redden `swift test`.
- FINDING: P2 is the one worth remembering. A registry that is never populated reports "no live owners", which reads exactly like a released model; the unit suite cannot tell a correct helper from an uncalled one, and only driving the production entry point catches it. Same lesson as the 2325 ledger mutant, one layer up.
- FINDING: the clean phase had a latent race the new gate exposed rather than caused. The deferred teardown finishes after the response is written, and 6a read `/debug/generation-state` immediately; the three extra stability polls were enough to lose the race and report `pending=true`, which looks identical to a refusal. The phase now waits, bounded, for a terminal teardown event to *exist* before asserting anything about what it says.
- EVIDENCE: 170 checks exit 0; contract suite 197 tests / 19 suites exit 0; `dead-generation-smoke.sh` still 45 checks exit 0, so the 2h39ya contract is intact; `xcodebuild` Release, `swift-format lint --strict`, `shellcheck -S warning` and `git diff --check` all exit 0. Pristine sources restored and verified byte-for-byte by SHA-256 before the final run.
- FINDING, stated rather than papered over: MLX's counters are process-global and nothing here can attribute an individual byte. The gate is built to refuse everything it cannot account for — ownership from the model tree, the allocator idle and at rest, anything model-sized a refusal. A false abandonment costs a supervision marker and a replacement process; a false attestation tells an operator the host is free while a condemned model is still holding it.
- STATUS: `TASK-260827-2q77g8` revision 5 handed back to review.

### 0552 — MLX Swift Cannot Serve This Model's Context, And The Failure Traps Rather Than Throws
- DECISION: **REJECT the MLX Swift migration.** Python `mlx-lm` stays the default local Qwen runtime. `benchmark-compare` exit 3, four blockers, two hard. Measured sequentially on one host (M1 Max / 64 GiB / build 25F80) against the real 28 GB `Qwen3.8-27B-Uncensored-MLX-8bit`: Python `mlx-lm` 0.32.0 @ `9150698` with `mlx` 0.32.2, Swift `mlx-swift` 0.31.6 / `mlx-swift-lm` 3.31.4.
- REGRESSION: the 75k-context run **aborts the Swift process**. `Fatal error: [metal::malloc] Attempting to allocate 255904140288 bytes which is greater than the maximum allowed buffer size of 41747087360 bytes`, SIGTRAP. The Python baseline served the same 73,054-token prompt in 822 s.
- ROOT CAUSE, verified to the byte: 255,904,140,288 ÷ (24 heads × 2 bytes) = 73,016² exactly — one full `[1, 24, n, n]` fp16 attention array over the whole prompt. `MLXVLM.Qwen35VLModel.prepare(_:cache:windowSize:)` declares its window parameter as `windowSize _: Int?` and **discards it**, pushing the entire prompt through the language model in one call. `GenerateParameters.prefillStepSize` (default 512) is therefore inert for this model, and no prototype flag can chunk that prefill. `mlx_lm` chunks its own, which is why the same prompt succeeds there.
- FINDING, and the one with the widest blast radius: MLX's default error handler calls `fatalError`, so an allocator failure is a **trap, not a throw**. None of the machinery TASK-260827-2h39ya and TASK-260827-2q77g8 built ran — no `generation_worker_failed`, no `/health` 503, no `generation_worker_unavailable` marker, no deferred teardown, no supervised restart. All of it was built for a thrown error. mlx-swift 0.31.6 *does* expose `withErrorHandler` / `withError` to convert MLX errors into Swift throws and the prototype does not use them; adopting them would buy a clean condemnation and a supervised replacement, not a served request.
