### 1905 — A Comparison Cannot Close A Field Set
- REGRESSION: Revision 5 declared the authorization frame's field set closed at five members and enforced that closure with five equality comparisons. The fifth review minted two frames — five valid fields plus an unknown sixth member, and `protocol_version` written twice as `999` then the valid value — and **both reached `execve` with all five comparisons green**. They were green because they were all true. An ordinary JSON decode discards unknown members and keeps one of a duplicate pair, so by the time the comparisons ran, the evidence that the frame had carried anything else was already gone.
- ROOT CAUSE: The rule and the mechanism were in different places. A comparison answers *is this value the one I expect*; it structurally cannot answer *were these the only bytes the frame carried*. Only the decoder is in a position to answer the second question, and revision 5 put the closure claim on the side that could not make it.
- DECISION: Move the closure to the decoder. B12 gains step 4 — one JSON object, each of the five allowed keys exactly once, nothing after it — refused as `protocol_violation` with a named reason and the offending member, before any value is compared. Section 9's rule becomes "no field uncompared **and** no field uncounted", because an implementation that cannot tell whether a sixth field arrived has no basis for claiming the set was closed.
- FINDING: Neither duplicate order is evidence, and this was measured rather than reasoned. The first draft of the mutant table expected one deleted-clause mutant to admit both duplicate orders; the run contradicted it. Go's last-wins decode admits wrong-then-valid and refuses valid-then-wrong, because last-wins hands the equality gate the *valid* value in one order and `999` in the other; a first-wins decoder admits exactly the reverse. Each order is refused by whichever dedup rule the implementation did not choose — so a single-order test shows a green duplicate gate against a launcher that has no duplicate rule at all. Two mutants now, one per dedup rule.
- DECISION: Record the non-discriminating rows instead of dropping them. The absent-member clause admits nothing — the equality gate already refuses a zero value — so its mutant changes a refusal's reason and never reaches `execve`, and the missing-member row does not redden under the whole-gate-deleted mutant either. Both facts are asserted in the test. A mutant table that keeps only the rows that discriminate reads as broader coverage than it has.
- FINDING: The wiring-proof obligation was satisfiable by an implementation incapable of detecting the violation it was asked to report. Revision 5 required the instrumented run to fail if a transmitted field went uncompared, but specified the compared set as the launcher's five struct members — and a launcher with five struct members can never report a sixth. It is now stated over the **decoded key multiset**, duplicates retained, written before the verdict is acted on. Deduplicating that record would report exactly the shape the forged frame presents, so that is a mutant too.
- FINDING: The general form, now met twice from two sides. **A check can only be as strong as the evidence still present when it runs.** Revision 4 stated a comparison and shipped a launcher that had discarded the value before making it. Revision 5 made five true comparisons after discarding the knowledge that there had been six fields. Both suites were green; every individual assertion in both was true.
- DECISION: Bump the protocol version to `6`, on the rule and for the second revision running. The frame's bytes did not change again, and the launcher's gate set changed again. Skipping a bump because nothing moved on the wire is the exception that makes the version unreliable; applying one rule twice to two changes that differ is the only test a rule of this kind gets.
- STATUS: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` revision 6 (`RUN-260825-c9afbf`) closes review `RUN-260825-a8a4ef` F1 and its five rework items. Probes P22.A-F pass on darwin 25.5.0/arm64 go1.25.5, with five decoder mutants each reddening its own named row; the reviewer's two frames are rerun verbatim in shape and now refuse, with the valid control still reaching `execve`. P1-P21 rerun green in their own modules. `tools/agents-infra` builds, vets and passes all three packages as a regression baseline — the delta is the specification and this logbook.

### 1815 — A Field You Transmit And Do Not Compare Is A Field You Do Not Have
- FINDING: The defect the fourth review found was not a wrong comparison, it was a *missing* one hiding behind a correct specification. B11 transmitted `protocol_version`, B12 required equality, the refusal table promised `runtime_authorization_mismatch` — and the attached probe parsed the field and dropped it. A frame with the correct pid, runtime key and plan digest and `protocol_version = 999` reached `execve`. Every named test was green the whole time, because no test varied that dimension. Green suites do not report the dimensions they never touched.
- DECISION: Close the class, not the instance. The authorization frame's field set is now **closed at five fields and every one of them is a gate**, with the rule stated normatively: an implementation may not carry a field the launcher ignores, and may not read one it does not compare. Adding a field is a protocol bump. Fixing only the version comparison would have left the next transmitted-and-ignored field to be found by the next reviewer.
- DECISION: Prove a bound by narrowing it, not only by deleting it. The version row carries two mutants: delete the comparison, and narrow it to `>= 1`. Both reach `execve` on 999, and the narrowed one still refuses 0 — so what is proved is that the gate is an *equality*, which is the whole content of the bump rule. A delete-only mutant would have shown the check exists and said nothing about the class it admits.
- FINDING: The launcher's version skew is reachable, not ceremonial. B9 spawns the broker's own executable **by path**, so an in-place `rename`-over upgrade landing between a broker's start and its B9 hands a new-build launcher a frame written by an old-build broker — the launcher-side twin of the stale-broker case gate 3b exists for, and the launcher has no handshake with which to run gate 3b.
- REGRESSION: One code, four branches, and a test that asserted the code. `runtime_launch_unauthorized` covers an absent descriptor, an unarmable bound, a dead broker and a deadline; revision 4's test claimed "absent FIFO refuses" while asserting an exit code the EOF branch produces too. Refusals now carry a `reason`, and gate-4 refusals a `mismatch_field`, because a refusal that cannot say which gate fired is not evidence that the gate fired.
- FINDING: Self-found, and the one that would have shipped. B12 said the launcher reads its frame "with a deadline". A descriptor inherited across `fork`+`exec` arrives **blocking**, and a read deadline cannot be armed on a blocking descriptor; revision 4's launcher discarded the error, so its read had no bound and its own deadline branch was unreachable rather than untested. An implementation following it literally waits forever for a broker that never writes. The bound must be armed before the read, and a launcher that cannot arm it must refuse rather than wait.
- FINDING: Self-found, evidence quality. Revision 4's probe harness recorded exec-path observations into a 64-slot channel with a non-blocking send, so a full buffer could silently drop the single observation proving the launcher had `execve`d the target. Every "never carried the exec path" assertion was satisfiable by a full buffer. It did not bite while refusals took milliseconds; it would have bitten the first deliberately slow one.
- DECISION: Bump the protocol version anyway. The frame's bytes did not change, so a compatibility argument is available and was rejected: the rule says a change to the gate set bumps the version, and the launcher's gate set changed. The version gate is enforced by the reading side, so a stale build can never be caught by the check it lacks — what the bump protects is the *client*, which would otherwise attach to a revision-4 broker while assuming a launcher gate set that broker never applied.
- STATUS: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` revision 5 (`RUN-260825-e7aee1`) closes review `RUN-260825-668303` F1 plus two self-found corrections. Probes P21.A-F pass on darwin 25.5.0/arm64 go1.25.5; P21.B/C/E/F carry deliberate defect reproductions and P21.A and the D all-correct row are the discriminating controls. The reviewer's attack is rerun verbatim in shape and now refuses, with the valid-version control still reaching `execve`. P1-P20 rerun green. `tools/agents-infra` builds, vets, and all three packages pass as a regression baseline — the delta is the specification and this logbook.

### 1810 — Every Fix Opens The Door It Was Not Watching
- FINDING: A hash is not a channel. Revision 3 handed the runtime launcher a `--runtime-key` and then required it to apply a startup timeout and `execve` an executable with an exact argv that nothing supplied. It read as implementable only because the probes fed their launcher `PROBE_TARGET`/`PROBE_TARGET_ARG`/`PROBE_AUTH_TIMEOUT_MS` through the environment — a capability with no normative counterpart. A probe that supplies from outside what the protocol must supply from inside proves the mechanism and hides the contract.
- DECISION: The launcher recomposes its own target. It is the broker's own binary, so it re-runs the same full profile composition from the same project directory and derives executable, exact argv, cwd and timeout itself; `runtime_key` equality pins it to the broker's, because executable, argc, every argv token and both runtime timeouts are already inside the digest the key covers. Putting the plan in the authorization frame would also have worked and was rejected: it makes the pipe a *command* channel, so whoever holds the write end chooses what runs rather than only whether it runs.
- ROOT CAUSE: "My child process is still alive" was read as "my broker is still starting". The broker deliberately stays its starter's OS child for its whole *serving* life, so the two states return the identical `wait4(WNOHANG)` answer. A client that timed out therefore terminated a broker that had already granted another RUN's lease — a bypass around final-release ownership needing no attacker and no unusual timing.
- DECISION: Delete the authority, do not improve the check. A client at its deadline now signals nothing and reports the broker's state as `unknown`. Nothing is stranded: every broker startup step is independently bounded, and a broker that reached `serving` with no lease is already bounded by the first-lease grace — which this makes load-bearing rather than tidy-up.
- FINDING: Ownership had been beginning several steps before identity. The broker became owner by taking `broker.lock` but published its owner record only after composition, reclamation and preflight, and the spec called the gap impossible. One `SIGSTOP` produces it. A false impossibility claim is worse than a named limitation, because it stops anyone from handling the case at all.
- DECISION: Publish the owner record as the broker's first side effect after the election, name the residual window `starting-unverified`, report it with kernel-visible candidate holders and their `p_stat`, and still refuse to signal — because two frozen candidates can be byte-identical on uid, binary inode, argv and `p_stat` while exactly one holds the lock. `flock` cannot name its holder on darwin, so a set is all there is, and signalling one member of a set is guessing.
- REGRESSION: Moving that publication forward reintroduced the *previous* revision's blocking defect by another route. `broker-state.json` is one file: a successor publishing its own record overwrote the predecessor's before reclaiming the runtime that record named, so a kill in between left a live runtime nothing named. Fixed by carrying the predecessor's block forward verbatim as `inherited-unreclaimed` — and reproduced as a probe before fixing, because the fix is invisible in every scenario except the one where a predecessor's runtime is alive.
- REGRESSION: The same door, second hinge. Every fatal broker outcome said "removes its own record and exits", which was safe only while the record could never name a runtime the broker had not created. A `--runtime-key` mismatch or an occupied port would now delete a record still naming a live predecessor. The rule underneath the fix is one sentence worth keeping: **a record may only stop naming a process once that process is proven gone** — so leave it while a block is unreclaimed, reap before removing, and never remove it when the reap failed.
- STATUS: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` revision 4 (`RUN-260825-59c781`) closes review `RUN-260825-969723` F1-F4 plus three self-found corrections. Probes P16-P20 pass on darwin 25.5.0/arm64; P17.A, P18.C and P19.A are deliberate defect reproductions and P16.B/P16.C/P17.C/P18.C-mutant/P19.D/P20.C are discriminating controls. P8-P15 rerun green. `tools/agents-infra` builds, vets, and all three packages pass.

### 1723 — Launcher Version Gate Exists Only In The Spec
- REGRESSION: Revision-4 B12 requires authorization-frame `protocol_version` equality before `execve`, but attached P18 parses `Protocol` and never compares it; `protocol_version=999` with correct pid, runtime key, and plan digest executes `/bin/sleep` on the launcher pid.
- ROOT CAUSE: Tests 12.2.18b/30 attack pid, composition, digest, truncation, and missing FIFO but never wrong protocol version; the narrowing-mutant table has no version-check mutant. The claimed `runtime_authorization_mismatch` branch is positive-path-only evidence.
- EVIDENCE: `.temp/TASK-260825-imfmgz/probe-rev4/review_rev4_attack_test.go`; uncached `TestReviewAttackWrongProtocolVersionIsAccepted` passes by reproducing the forbidden exec.
- STATUS: CR revision 4 routed to `analysis`; add the wrong-version production negative and a narrowed/deleted version-check mutant, correct P18, and decide whether the frame `schema` is also a validated gate.

### 1710 — Invert The Order Instead Of Inspecting Harder
- FINDING: `flock` cannot tell you *who* holds a lock, only that someone does, and revision 2 built a broker gate on that inference. The consequence was exploitable exactly where it mattered: during a peer broker's startup, a hand-invoked second broker with a descriptor open on the correct `broker.lock` inode but not holding it passed the inode check, got its `EWOULDBLOCK` from the incumbent, passed the port preflight because the runtime had not bound yet, and passed the rendezvous bind because the incumbent binds only after readiness. Four gates, all green, wrong answer.
- DECISION: Stop asking the question. The broker now acquires `broker.lock` in its own process as its first action and a loser exits before reading, killing, preflighting or binding; the client never opens the file at all. Two things fell out for free: waiter promotion needs no code — the kernel frees the lock and the next candidate wins — and the ad-hoc-invocation gate becomes `getsid(0) == getpid()`, a fact the process reads about itself instead of inferring about another.
- FINDING: A record written *after* the thing it describes is not evidence, it is a race. Revision 2 spawned the runtime and published its identity afterwards, so a `SIGKILL` in between left a live runtime, a freed lock, and an absent record that recovery read as "nothing to reclaim". Reproduced end to end before fixing it: runtime alive, state file absent, lock free, verdict `no-record` while that runtime was still serving.
- DECISION: Publish-before-run. The runtime is created as an *unauthorized launcher* — our own binary, blocked on a pipe whose write end only the broker holds — the record naming its pid is published, and only then is the launcher authorized to `execve` the real runtime in place. Broker dies first ⇒ EOF ⇒ the launcher removes itself, having never been the runtime. This works because `execve` preserves pid, pgid and process **start time byte-for-byte**, so a record written before the runtime existed still identifies it after.
- FINDING: `kern.proc.pid` is **not** a liveness test on darwin — it answers successfully for an exited, unreaped process. Only `kern.procargs2` refuses a zombie, and `p_stat == SZOMB` is the discriminator. Found the honest way: the probe harness reported a `SIGKILL`ed broker as live and broke its own force-stop probe. Left unfixed in the spec, one unreaped corpse would have been read as a live runtime and blocked every future start forever.
- DECISION: When a timing knob sits outside the identity digest, do not resolve the divergence with a rule — resolve it with the schema. `broker_start_timeout_seconds >= startup + shutdown + 30`, both terms digest-covered, so the *smallest admissible value is identical for every authorized client and already exceeds a correct startup*. Divergence can then only mean one client is more patient, never that a correct peer start is refused.
- STATUS: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` revision 3 (`RUN-260825-a6390b`) closes review `RUN-260825-ac68af` F1-F5 plus two self-found corrections. Probes P8-P15 pass on darwin 25.5.0/arm64; P12.A and P15 are deliberate defect reproductions. `tools/agents-infra` builds, vets, and all three packages pass.

### 1630 — A Launcher Needs The Values It Executes
- FINDING: Revision-3 `runtime-launch` receives only `--runtime-key`, while its authorization frame carries only schema, protocol, runtime key, and pid; the same entry point is required to choose `runtime.startup_timeout_seconds` and `execve(runtime.executable, runtime.argv)` with no specified source for any of them (`.research/260825_shared-local-runtime-broker.spec.md:666`).
- FINDING: P10-P12 do not close this contract: their launcher receives target, argv, and timeout through `PROBE_*` environment variables, a transport absent from the normative B7/B9 protocol.
- STATUS: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` revision 3 review requests an explicit, fail-closed launcher input/binding contract and production-path negatives before implementation starts.

### 1630 — A Live Child Is Not A Starting Broker
- ROOT CAUSE: Client deadline step 4d infers `broker_child != none` means its broker is still starting, but the broker intentionally remains the starter's live child for its whole serving lifetime (`.research/260825_shared-local-runtime-broker.spec.md:530`).
- FINDING: A valid interleaving is `connect -> ENOENT`, broker reaches B13, a peer acquires a lease, then the starter reaches its deadline and sends `SIGTERM`; the timeout path revokes a live peer and violates final-release ownership.
- STATUS: Replace the proxy with an explicit startup-only cancellation boundary, or do not signal on client timeout; add the deterministic production-path race and a narrowing mutant.

### 1630 — The Owner Record Starts After Ownership
- ANOMALY: B1 makes the broker owner by acquiring `broker.lock`, but B5 publishes its owner record only after detachment, profile composition, and predecessor recovery (`.research/260825_shared-local-runtime-broker.spec.md:575`, `:651`). A valid broker can therefore hold the lock with no owner record.
- REGRESSION: `stop --force` calls that state impossible for this protocol and returns `shared_runtime_owner_unidentifiable` (`.research/260825_shared-local-runtime-broker.spec.md:1134`); a broker paused in B1-B4 is neither reportable nor stoppable through the promised operator surface.
- STATUS: Revision-3 review requests either kernel-verifiable ownership throughout B1-B5 or an honest state/refusal contract with a bounded recovery path; do not infer an arbitrary lock holder is absent or safe to signal.

### 1615 — Probe The Mechanism You Are About To Mandate
- FINDING: Two mechanisms in revision 1 of `.research/260825_shared-local-runtime-broker.spec.md` were unimplementable, and both were mechanisms nobody probed. `flock` is per open file description, so the specified "confirm you hold the inherited `broker.lock` by re-locking it" always succeeds — a gate that admits everything. The working check is a *second* descriptor: `EWOULDBLOCK` proves held, and an unheld control proves the negative distinguishes. `fcntl F_GETLK` cannot see a BSD `flock` at all. Revision 1 probed `flock` *handoff* across `fork`+`exec` and then specified `flock` *self-inspection*, which is a different question.
- FINDING: The same revision took `broker.lock` with a blocking `LOCK_EX`. Because the broker holds that lock for its whole serving life and binds its socket only once the runtime is ready, a second client arriving during a 27B model's startup got `ENOENT`, blocked on a lock released only by the broker's death, and timed out while a healthy broker served — i.e. the story's own reference scenario failed. Fix: one bounded loop racing a connect poll against `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`, with `shared_runtime_peer_start_timeout` distinct from `broker_start_timeout` so a client that started nothing never reports the starter's error.
- FINDING: On darwin, `close(2)` on a descriptor another thread is blocked in `flock(LOCK_EX)` on **itself blocks** until the `flock` returns. A process that abandons a blocking lock acquisition cannot release the descriptor at all; it can only exit. Independent reason never to block on a lock you might want to stop waiting for.
- FINDING: Comparing a peer's executable by stat'ing its recorded exec path does **not** detect an in-place `rename`-over upgrade: the stale process's path now resolves to the new inode, so the comparison passes and a process running the previous build is admitted. The defence is the binary identity the process recorded for *itself at its own startup*. A security section that names the wrong gate is worse than one that names none.
- FINDING: A `Setsid` child is **not** reparented to init immediately — `ppid` stays the parent's until the parent exits. Terminal independence comes from the new session, not from reparenting. Keeping the child a real child is what lets a starter detect a startup failure with `wait4(WNOHANG)` instead of waiting out a full timeout, so a double-fork would trade a useful signal for nothing.
- DECISION: When a config table is deliberately excluded from an identity digest (here `[runtime.sharing]`), the spec must still say whose copy governs. Revision 2: the starter's policy governs for the broker's lifetime, is announced in the handshake, and is reported by `status` and in the refusal beside the caller's configured value. `linger_seconds` made this load-bearing — a peer's nonzero linger would silently defeat the cleanup proof.
- STATUS: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` revision 2 (`RUN-260825-f23f2d`) closes review `RUN-260825-626994` F1-F7 plus one self-found correction. Probes P1-P7 pass on darwin 25.5.0/arm64; P7 is shaped as a negative — it reproduces the starvation under the old algorithm before showing the new one attach. `tools/agents-infra` builds and all three packages pass.

### 1520 — A Lease Is A Connection, Not A Timer
- DECISION: `TASK-260825-imfmgz` specifies shared local-runtime ownership as a detached session-leader broker that stays the *direct parent* of `mlx_lm.server`, so cycle-8's "endpoint is backed by a live child we spawned" invariant moves from the Pi client to the broker instead of being dropped. Leases are open `AF_UNIX` connections: kernel teardown releases exactly, including on `SIGKILL`, so heartbeats are observability only and never revoke.
- DECISION: `runtime_key` excludes the project and is `SHA-256(endpoint || profile_digest)`. `127.0.0.1:<port>` is machine-global, so per-project brokers would make two projects with byte-identical profiles deadlock on the same port — the exact failure the story exists to remove. Client state stays project- and RUN-keyed.
- FINDING: A full-key broker socket path is impossible on this host. `/Users/alexis/Library/Caches` + `agents-infra/pi/` + two 64-hex keys + `broker.sock` is 186 bytes against a 103-byte `sun_path`; a 189-byte bind returns `invalid argument`. The rendezvous address is therefore a 32-hex truncation and is never an identity — the full key, profile digest, and endpoint are verified over the connection.
- FINDING: Probed on darwin 25.5.0/arm64 with `x/sys v0.30.0`, no cgo: `SysctlKinfoProc("kern.proc.pid")` gives uid and start time and errors on a dead pid; `SysctlRaw("kern.procargs2")` gives exec path and argv for same-uid processes and returns `EINVAL` for another uid; `GetsockoptXucred(LOCAL_PEERCRED)` and `LOCAL_PEERPID` both work. Foreign-uid `EINVAL` is a refusal input, not an absence.
- FINDING: `flock` survives `fork`+`exec` on the shared open file description, so the starter can hand `broker.lock` to the detached broker and close its own copy with no unheld window; the kernel releases it if and only if the broker dies. That makes `broker.lock held` equivalent to `a broker exists or is starting`, which is what makes single-flight exact without a timer.
- DECISION: Attaching is admitted only through a 12-gate chain (13 from revision 2, see 1615) whose bootstrap is broker-executable `dev`/`ino` equality with the caller's own binary; the client re-verifies runtime uid, start time, exec path, exact argv, and `/v1/models` itself rather than trusting the announcement. Socket-ownership attestation stays *unclaimed* — it needs cgo or a raw `proc_info` syscall to close only the bind race cycle 8 already accepted.
- DECISION: A broker killed with `SIGKILL` orphans the runtime; the next starter verifies and *kills* that orphan. It is never attached to, because no live process is its parent and the direct-child invariant cannot be restored.
- SCOPE: `STORY-260825-1r7z9o`; `mode` defaults to `exclusive` so all 121 existing project configs keep today's behavior byte-for-byte. Spec: `.research/260825_shared-local-runtime-broker.spec.md`.

Concurrent reviewers must isolate mutants in a private copy, or serialize.

- DECISION: Verify a docs premise against the shipped binary, not against the task description that introduced it. `llama --version` plus `strings`/`nm` on the ggml dylib settles it in seconds; `README.md:604` and `SKILL.md:320` state build 10470 as fact and now have that backing.
- FINDING: A narrowing mutant alone under-tests an exact-name gate. Adding `"GGML_"` to the loader prefix list in the same function reddens the `GGML_METAL_PATH` clean control on the in-process lifecycle test *and* both installed launcher surfaces — so the suite pins the upper bound of the class too, not just its lower bound. Reviewers should run the broadening mutant next to the narrowing one whenever a gate is deliberately scoped narrower than its name family.
- FINDING: Mutating `RunPi`'s gate order (moving `ValidatePiExecutionEnvironment` after `CreatePiStateTree`) reddens `environment refusal created managed state` for *every* member of the refusal table, not only the new one. Ordering is genuinely asserted rather than incidentally satisfied.
- ANOMALY: `TestPiLaunchForwardsSignalsThenCleansRuntime` fails in any rsync copy of the tree that reaches the Pi asset through a symlink — `officialPiAsset` uses `filepath.Abs` while `identity.Entrypoint` resolves symlinks, so the spawn-path assertion mismatches. Copy artifact only; green in the real checkout. Budget for it when reviewing in a disposable copy.
- STATUS: `BUG-260818-76hkcb` accepted by review (`BUG-260818-76hkcb_review-verdict.md`); routed `to-review` for the commit-owning mover, since a reviewer archetype must not supply `commit_ack`.

### 0105 — Managed Pi Denies Exact GGML Backend Loader Path
- ROOT CAUSE: llama.cpp build 10470 passes inherited `GGML_BACKEND_PATH` to `dlopen()` during backend discovery, bypassing the intent of managed `DYLD_*`/`LD_*` loader-injection gates.
- FIX: `tools/agents-infra/internal/infra/pi_catalog.go:295` refuses exact `GGML_BACKEND_PATH` at `RunPi`'s shared environment boundary before state or runtime spawn and reports only the name; other `GGML_*` remain admitted absent an established runtime effect.
- EVIDENCE: Production and bootstrap-global/project-local launcher negatives fail under a `GGML_BACKEND_PATH_V2` narrowing mutant; restored controls reach runtime backend initialization with `GGML_METAL_PATH`; full tests, vet, bootstrap, and global/local verification exit 0.
- STATUS: `BUG-260818-76hkcb` implementation ready for review handoff.

### 0010 — `strings(1)` Is A False-Negative Proxy For Go Gate Literals
- FINDING: `strings ~/.local/bin/agents-infra | grep HF_ENDPOINT` returns zero hits on a binary that demonstrably refuses `HF_ENDPOINT`. Go compiles short literal string equality (`name == "HF_ENDPOINT"`, 11 bytes) into a length check plus immediate-constant comparisons, so the literal never lands in rodata. Literals reached through a slice or a format string (`LLAMA_ARG_`, `runtime-affecting …`) do appear, which makes the proxy look reliable right up to the point it lies.
- IMPACT: The installed-binary staleness check used in `BUG-260817-161m6u` (LOGBOOK 2310) would have reported this gate as absent from a correctly built binary. Do not use `strings` to establish whether an installed launcher carries a gate; run the gate with a positive clean control instead.
- EVIDENCE: `BUG-260817-2bh9nk` review — real `~/.local/bin/pi-infra` (target sha256 `9859d5…a058`) under `env -i`: clean control reaches runtime spawn and creates the runtime marker; `HF_ENDPOINT` and `MODEL_ENDPOINT` each exit 1 with `runtime-affecting environment name "<NAME>" is denied`, no value in output, no runtime child.
- STATUS: `BUG-260817-2bh9nk` accepted by review; awaiting the commit-owning mover for the final `done` transition.

## 2026-08-17

### 2349 — Managed Pi Pins Model-Origin Environment
- ROOT CAUSE: llama.cpp build 10470 honors `HF_ENDPOINT` and `MODEL_ENDPOINT` during `-hf` resolution, so inherited values could redirect reviewed model IDs to an unreviewed origin without changing managed argv.
- FIX: `tools/agents-infra/internal/infra/pi_catalog.go:295` refuses both exact names at the shared `RunPi` environment gate before state or runtime spawn; diagnostics expose names only. Tokens, cache variables, and case-sensitive lookalikes remain separate policy questions.
- EVIDENCE: Clean, denial, no-value-leak, production-entry, bootstrap-global, and project-local wrapper tests pass; HF-only and MODEL-only narrowing mutants each fail the opposite endpoint's unit and production `RunPi` cases.
- STATUS: `BUG-260817-2bh9nk` implementation and validation ready for review handoff.

### 2323 — Installed-Launcher Behavioral Proof Beats Binary String Parity
- FINDING: A managed-profile probe of the installed `~/.local/bin/pi-infra` with a clean control is a complete ordering proof: the control walks argv plan, environment gate, Pi identity, runtime identity and creates `Caches/agents-infra/pi/<state-key>/…` before spawning the runtime child, while `LLAMA_ARG_MODEL`/`LLAMA_ARG_CTX_SIZE` runs stop with only `Caches/` present. Without that control, "no state was created" is indistinguishable from failing early for an unrelated reason.
- CONSTRAINT: `DYLD_*` cannot be probed through the `pi-infra` shim — macOS SIP strips it when exec'ing `/usr/bin/env sh`, so that gate stays covered at source level only.
- EVIDENCE: `BUG-260817-161m6u` cycle-2 review — installed binary rebuilt by `./setup.sh` (SHA-256 `df62cd…f42c3` → `3cd24e…a0d`); denied probes name only the variable and leak no value; README delete/narrow/ordering mutants all redden `TestPiOperatorContractDocumentsCycle10Boundary`; `pi_catalog.go` delete and narrow-to-`LLAMA_ARG_MODEL` mutants redden validator and production-entry tests.
- STATUS: `BUG-260817-161m6u` accepted by review; awaiting the commit-owning mover for the final `done` transition.

### 2310 — `setup global` Never Refreshes The Bootstrap-Owned CLI Binary
- FINDING: `agents-infra setup global` syncs source into `~/.agents` and prints `Skipping local CLI wrapper install for global setup; bootstrap owns ~/.local/bin/agents-infra`, so the compiled binary that `~/.local/bin/pi-infra` execs keeps running pre-change code. Only the repo bootstrap `./setup.sh` (`scripts/setup.sh:182` `go build -trimpath`) rebuilds it.
- FINDING: `verify global` inspects the runtime tree, not the executable, so a green `setup global` + `verify global` pair is a proxy signal and does not establish that the installed launcher carries a new gate.
- EVIDENCE: `BUG-260817-161m6u` review — `strings ~/.local/bin/agents-infra` (mtime 21:11) has `DYLD_`, `pi_execution_environment_invalid`, `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` but zero `LLAMA_ARG_`/`runtime-affecting`; a fresh `go build -trimpath` of the same source has both.
- NOTE: The generated project-local wrapper is a shell script that rebuilds from `AGENTS_INFRA_SOURCE_DIR` on every invocation, so local installs do pick changes up — the asymmetry is global-only.
- STATUS: `BUG-260817-161m6u` routed to `to-dev`; source gate and its mutants are sound, installed global runtime refresh and a README contract fragment in `pi_operator_docs_test.go` are the outstanding rework.

- FINDING: Real runtime `llama-b10470` exports `LLAMA_ARG_HOST`/`LLAMA_ARG_PORT` and `pi_launch_posix.go:142` passes the inherited environment through unfiltered, but driving the binary directly proves CLI wins — it logs `LLAMA_ARG_HOST environment variable is set, but will be overwritten by command line argument --host`. Not a bypass of the argv endpoint gate.
- FINDING: `validatePiRuntimeEndpointArgv` runs at config-parse time (`pi_config.go:207`), so it still refuses divergence when Pi is absent from `PATH` and compose would otherwise report `managed:false`. No unmanaged-fallback bypass.
- FINDING: Narrowing mutants prove both bounds, not just gate presence — unbinding the port value reddens `runtime_port_drift`, unbinding the host value reddens `wildcard_runtime_bind`, and relaxing exactly-one to at-least-one reddens `duplicate_runtime_port`.

Story-wide fixture convention (helper is not in `HEAD`, backs 6 tests), not introduced by this bug.

- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` cycle 7 adds a production-entry self-minted proxy attack and a narrowing mutant that replaces authoritative observation with config/environment echo.

### 1437 — Runtime Attestation Authority Is Self-Minted
- FINDING: `agents-infra.runtime-attestation.v1` validates a nonce, direct-child PID, model, and capability fields, but the arbitrary project-selected runtime or adapter that receives those expected values is also allowed to mint the JSON claim.
- ROOT CAUSE: The contract defines exact response shape without an independent authority anchor for socket/process ownership or active DFlash state; a direct-child proxy can own the public listener, echo the expected attestation, and forward inference to a foreign or target-only backend.
- STATUS: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` review cycle 6 requires analysis rework with a trusted attestation authority/ownership mechanism and a production-entry self-minted proxy negative case.

### 1433 — Managed Runtime Ownership Uses One Child-Bound Attestation
- DECISION: Every managed Pi runtime must return `agents-infra.runtime-attestation.v1` bound to a fresh 256-bit launcher nonce, exact direct-child PID, exact model, and exact byte-sorted capability set; Qwen uses `dflash: null`, Muse adds the exact active DFlash target/draft object.
- ROOT CAUSE: Child liveness and `/v1/models` allowed a foreign listener to satisfy Qwen readiness while the selected non-binding child stayed alive; a preflight port check cannot close the check-to-bind race.
- STATUS: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` cycle 6 decision and downstream implementation/operator AC now require absent, unreadable, malformed, stale, replayed, wrong-nonce/PID/model/capability, and foreign-listener refusals through the real launcher.

### 1426 — Pi Qwen Readiness Is Not Child-Bound
- FINDING: The managed Qwen contract accepts `/v1/models` from the configured loopback origin without a nonce, PID, or other proof that the selected runtime child owns the listener.
- ROOT CAUSE: Child-bound nonce/PID attestation exists only for the Muse DFlash profile; process liveness plus model readiness cannot distinguish the new child from a pre-existing listener.
- STATUS: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` review cycle 5 requires analysis rework; bind readiness to the owned child and add a production-entry foreign-listener bypass plus narrowing evidence.

### 1422 — Managed Pi Uses Verified Standalone Execution Closure
- ROOT CAUSE: Byte-exact npm Pi verification left the `#!/usr/bin/env node` host, installed dependencies, `PATH`, and loader environment outside the gate; `NODE_OPTIONS=--require` could rewrite managed argv before Pi parsed it.
- DECISION: Managed profiles admit only a compiled-catalog official standalone release tree, initially Pi `v0.84.2` darwin-arm64; npm/shebang Pi remains native-passthrough-only.
- DECISION: Direct launch rejects loader-affecting environment names before side effects, invokes the verified canonical standalone path, and repeats full tree/environment-name verification immediately before Pi spawn.
- SCOPE: `.research/260817_pi-local-model-launch-contract.md`, `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` review cycle 5; downstream `TASK-260817-ccpnlm` and `TASK-260817-3a0zr3` AC updated.

### 1406 — Pi Managed Launch Bound to Immutable Package Identity
- DECISION: Managed `agents-infra pi` policy selects a read-only compatibility catalog entry compiled into the agents-infra release; project TOML cannot mint digests or extend the catalog.
- FINDING: Published `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@0.84.2` is bound by npm SRI, tarball SHA-256, exact `dist/cli.js` and `dist/cli/args.js` hashes, and a 972-file canonical package manifest digest; semver alone is non-authoritative.
- DECISION: Direct launch statically verifies the canonical package tree before lock/file/runtime/Pi side effects. Absent, malformed, unsupported, mismatch, and unknown identities remain distinct fail-closed states; non-launching diagnostics never execute Pi/npm/Node/Bun to discover identity.
- SCOPE: `.research/260817_pi-local-model-launch-contract.md`, `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` review cycle 4.

### 1358 — Pi Argv Contract Lacks Runtime Identity Gate
- FINDING: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` proves argv normalization against Pi commit `a1bc0ec79010887210cc7de28714d72c78577dab` (`@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` 0.84.2), but the proposed launcher resolves an arbitrary `pi` executable and never establishes that its parser grammar matches the pinned snapshot.
- ROOT CAUSE: Parser-dependent option arities, separator handling, equal forms, and extension-flag consumption are specified without a supported Pi version/build identity, compatibility probe, or deterministic parser catalog selected from verified identity.
- STATUS: Review cycle 3 requires analysis rework; bind managed launch to exact verified Pi identity or define a deterministic generated compatibility catalog with mismatch/unknown refusal and production-entry negative evidence before runtime start.

### 1351 — Pi Separator Is Not End-of-Options
- ROOT CAUSE: Pi `parseArgs()` at revision `a1bc0ec79010887210cc7de28714d72c78577dab` treats literal `--` as an unknown flag and continues parsing later model, credential, thinking, and trust options.
- DECISION: Managed `agents-infra pi` strips its wrapper-only delimiter, forwards only exact safe operands, rejects suffix tokens beginning with `-` or `@`, prevents value consumption across the removed boundary, and normalizes recognized `--flag=value` wrapper forms to Pi's spaced argv.
- STATUS: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` contract and production-entry negative/mutant scenarios revised; no runtime may start on unsafe or ambiguous operand boundaries.

### 1337 — Pi Managed Identity and DFlash Gates Defined
- DECISION: Managed Pi profiles generate one provider/model catalog identity; CLI `--provider` and `--model` are accepted only when they resolve byte-for-byte to that identity. Different endpoint-exposed IDs, patterns, mismatches, and separator lookalikes refuse before runtime start.
- DECISION: DFlash launch requires a runtime-owned JSON attestation bound to a fresh 256-bit launcher nonce, direct child PID, fresh timestamp, and exact target/draft identities; absent, unreadable, malformed, false, stale, or mismatched evidence terminates runtime before Pi starts.
- STATUS: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` contract and production-entry negative scenarios revised; `unknown` remains non-launching diagnostics only.

### 1332 — Pi Local Profile Override and DFlash Attestation Gaps
- FINDING: The draft Pi contract generates one provider/model entry but permits CLI provider/model overrides without defining how an overridden selection enters the generated catalog or remains bound to the selected runtime profile.
- FINDING: The DFlash profile requires fail-closed capability proof but defines no authoritative attestation source; its negative scenario permits `unknown`, allowing absent evidence to reach the permissive branch.
- STATUS: `TASK-260817-2h8hn4` requires analysis rework before implementation; make override materialization/refusal exact and define a launch-time DFlash attestation gate with negative production-entry evidence.

### 1325 — Pi Local Profiles Require Isolated Agent State
- FINDING: Pi custom providers/models are global-agent-dir data (`models.json`), while project settings use `.pi/settings.json`; project settings alone cannot register local models.
- DECISION: `agents-infra pi` selected local profiles use `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`/`PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR` under agents-infra cache, an atomic generated local-only catalog, a loopback managed runtime child, and no reads/writes under `~/.pi/agent`.
- ANOMALY: Public DFlash documentation checked 2026-08-17 lists Qwen 3.5/3.6 targets but not the task labels `Qwen 3.8 27B` or `Muse Glimmer 30B`; profile artifact/runtime strings remain exact operator inputs and must not be guessed.
- SCOPE: `.research/260817_pi-local-model-launch-contract.md`, `TASK-260817-2h8hn4`.

### 0252 — Policy Value Domains Validated Against Providers (0126 Resolved)
- FIX: The shared launch-plan parsers validate native policy values against probe-verified provider domains before serializing them as effective. Codex typed flags (`--sandbox`/`-s`, `--ask-for-approval`/`-a` in spaced/`=`/attached forms) validate against the clap enums (`read-only|workspace-write|danger-full-access`; `untrusted|on-request|never`); `-c`/`--config` `sandbox_mode=`/`approval_policy=` overrides validate against the config deserialization domains, where `on-failure` and `granular` are approval variants the typed flag rejects. Claude `--permission-mode` validates every occurrence against the case-sensitive commander choices (`acceptEdits|auto|bypassPermissions|manual|dontAsk|plan`). All rejects fail closed via `ProviderArgumentError` → `invalid_provider_arguments` in compose and identically in the launchers.
- FIX: Claude `--effort` mirrors the provider exactly instead of failing closed: values match case-insensitively and canonicalize to lowercase (`HIGH` → effective `high`); an unknown value is NOT rejected — Claude launches, warns, and applies its own default — so the token stays in argv but `resolved.reasoning` reports the provider-native fallback (`value:null, source:"native"`) via the new `ExplicitEffortRecognized` plan field.
- DECISION: Config-domain validation mirrors Codex deserialization semantics precisely: only the last `-c` override per policy key is validated (earlier repeats are masked by last-wins, probe exit 0), and a typed flag does not mask an invalid `-c` override (probe `codex exec --sandbox read-only -c 'sandbox_mode="banana"'` exit 1). Non-string TOML values (`sandbox_mode=true`) fail closed like the provider's "invalid type" reject. `model_reasoning_effort` was probed and is NOT config-validated by codex 0.145.0 (`"banana"` exit 0), so it stays unvalidated — no domain exists to enforce.
- EVIDENCE: Probes on codex-cli 0.145.0 / Claude Code 2.1.217 in `.temp/TASK-260724-35d94i/rework6/` (probe-01..27). All four reviewer repros re-run on the fixed binary: exit 1 + safe schema-v1 `invalid_provider_arguments` envelope; `--effort banana` composes ok with native reasoning. Child schema-v1 byte-identical to a HEAD-built binary on the MCP fixture; no-launch sentinel proof; full tests/vet/gofmt/diff-check green; no task-board dependency.

- ROOT CAUSE: `tools/agents-infra/internal/attachments/attachments.go:733` previously matched a thread ID anywhere in a rollout basename and could select a newer unrelated session.
- FIX: require the legacy `rollout-*<thread-id>.jsonl` suffix before modification-time ordering.
- EVIDENCE: regression coverage creates competing files under a temporary HOME and selects only `rollout-run-needle.jsonl`.
- STATUS: Resolved.

### 1219 — Attachment Usage Exit-Code Fix
- FIX: `tools/agents-infra/main.go` now preserves typed attachment helper exit codes at the process boundary.
- EVIDENCE: `/Users/alexis/.local/bin/agents-infra attachments` and `/Users/alexis/.local/bin/agents-attachments` both print usage and exit `2`.
- STATUS: Regression from 1217 resolved; re-review required for `TASK-260721-2c1847`.

### 1217 — Attachment Usage Exit-Code Regression
- REGRESSION: `tools/agents-infra/main.go:25` prints every returned error then exits `1`; `attachments.Run` marks usage as code `2`, but that signal is discarded.
- EVIDENCE: `go run . attachments` prints the Go helper's `exit code 2` error but exits `1`; legacy `.scripts/agents-attachments` returned `usage()` directly with `2`.
- STATUS: `TASK-260721-2c1847` requires a top-level usage-exit mapping and subprocess-level regression test before acceptance.

### 1211 — Windows Attachment Launcher Exit Regression
- REGRESSION: `tools/agents-infra/internal/infra/infra.go:945` joins each conditional launch with `& exit /b %ERRORLEVEL%`; CMD executes `exit` unconditionally and expands `%ERRORLEVEL%` before the delegated command.
- SCOPE: Installed `agents-attachments.cmd` cannot fall back to PATH when sibling launchers are absent and can return a stale success status after a Go-helper failure.
- STATUS: `TASK-260721-2c1847` routed to rework; add a Windows launcher-contract test and error-path coverage for the new attachment package.
